![[Amadea Wandering Violet 2.png]]

In the grand scale of [[Arcanoria]], [[Amadea]]'s life and the [[Sonata]] of the Violet Empress represent the transition of [[Ages]] II, the [[Age Crisis]] of [[The Hollowing]] and [[Ages]] III. [[Amadea]] is born in the last [[Act of Fate]] of [[Ages]] II of the [[Age of Embers]], through this entire [[Act of Fate]] she founds [[Iridia]] and fights [[Pollux]]. The [[Age Crisis]] begins in Movement 4: The Resistance, which features the [[Age Crisis]] after [[A Pact of a Thousand Eyes]], and it ends with the death of [[Pagiel]]. When [[Pagiel]] dies, this is the first [[Act of Fate]] of [[Ages]] III. [[Amadea]] dies at 60 of old age in the first [[Act of Fate]] of [[Ages]] III, leaving the legacy of the [[Age of Legends]] that eventually concludes [[The Hollowing]] and its transition to the [[Law of Relics]] in the next two [[Act of Fate]], and its future [[Age Crisis]] of [[The Birth of the Hyper Chord]].

Whenever [[Amadea]] is about to do something dangerous or she's lost, such as the moment when she's infiltrating the aristocracy, she tends to utter a few words to herself. _“She implored the heavens until dawn. Tell me, Silver Moon, which voice is the note of Consonance?”_

This is a Ballad fragment from [[Vaelia]], mother of all bards, that she learned from [[Elaine]] as [[Elaine]] used this to ground herself when she's doing something dangerous. This is a memory that survived in [[Amadea]], as it also was the first time [[Elaine]] singed to her when she braided the knot of her hair.

[[Auric Geometry]] matters a lot for the chalice basin landmark that is [[Iridia]] sitting on for reasons related to her childhood caravan:

- **Theological Perfection:** In the mythos of the Auric Order, a chalice-like basin is not just a valley; it is a manifestation of the Auric Aria's perfect geometry. Because of its steep, encircling walls and recessed floor, the basin acts as a natural acoustic trap. It catches the flowing Resonance of the Leylines and harmonizes it, creating an area of high Coherence where the primal white noise and Static Criticality cannot easily penetrate.
- **Corvin’s Ultimate Redemption:** As a Threat Cartographer, Corvin is haunted by the fact that he miscalculated the boundaries of the Militant Enclave's spire, leading to its implosion and the death of his children. A sacred chalice basin represents a boundary built by the cosmos itself. For Corvin, bringing The Last Procession to a natural, mathematically perfect fortress means he doesn't have to engineer the walls himself—meaning he cannot miscalculate, and he cannot fail the children again. It is the ultimate psychological and architectural redemption.
- **The Geographic Anchor:** The basin that eventually becomes Iridia is hidden behind the Crescent Mist Peaks, sheltered by the colossal, mist-shrouded drops of the Eclipsed Waterfalls. The running water naturally purifies the air of Luminant Rot and grounds the environmental magic.
- **The Soil of the Grove:** The constant mist and deep Leyline convergence in this basin create the exact, impossibly rare soil conditions required for the Auric Peach tree—the seed Artus originally gave Amadea—to bloom with the specific, violet-hued radiance that gives the Violet Grove its name.
- Iridia is the private dream with [[Artus]] **The Public Myth vs. The Private Vow:** To the rest of the world, the city Amadea founds is a sanctuary, the capital of the Obsidian Feather Society, and a bastion against the Purest of Love. But the name "Iridia" is the private, whispered promise she shared with Artus in the dark of [[Age of Behemoths]] looking at the [[Grand Thread Rings]].
- **Honoring the Knight:** Naming the city Iridia is Amadea's way of ensuring that the nameless orphan boy who spun ballads to keep her sane is cemented into the bedrock of reality.
- **Stasis vs. Motion:** Corvin’s tragedy was attempting to keep a fragile sanctuary alive while in constant, desperate motion across the Age of Embers. Amadea learns from his failure. She realizes that to protect the vulnerable, the sanctuary must be rooted in unyielding stone. Iridia is The Last Procession finally coming to a halt.
- **The Living Artifacts:** Amadea fills Iridia's chalice basin with the exact things Corvin tried to save: the Pure Light buffalo, the children who survived the wastelands, the Civic plays and ballads that keep memory alive, and the protective, sweeping perimeters. She succeeds where her surrogate father failed, becoming the architect of the safe haven he died trying to find.

Add soft worldbuilding details of food, [[Auric Peach]], celebrations, [[Civic]]s, and communal rites of [[Spellweaving]] as celebration. Expand upon preparation of food within the caravan and later in [[Iridia]]. Detailing how [[Amadea]] and [[Cordelia]] cook meals on the road—perhaps roasting the mundane [[Auric Peach]]es over [[Cindergale]]-sparked fires—could serve as a crucial pacing mechanism. Eating together is the ultimate act of vulnerability in a predatory world. The sharing of [[Auric Peach]] preserves could eventually become a formal [[Civic]] sacrament in [[Iridia]], transforming a mundane survival tactic into a holy communion given that this was the first gift of [[Amadea]]. Cooking is introduced as early as chapter 2 as one of the utilities of magic in the caravan.

Amadea scene on massages and she hates being touched while Cordelia likes them this is asymmetrical showing that Cordelia does like care and touch without violence. These are two scenes of these massages, during the travel before Iridia and in the cycles between Iridia and the war with Pollux, the latter of this scenes features Cordelia having a healed back after the scars have receded.

Moonlit vigil attended by Cordelia hoping to find someone but comes back alone- She does this twice in her life, and these are the key flag events for gateway to genesis. To marry Cordelia, one of the player characters has to appear in these events.

[[Amadea]] does harbor some resentment for the aristocracy given that she was deprived of a safe household to live when she was younger, this develops as jealousy during her teenage [[Cycle]]s and even becomes a trope she channels into humor with [[Cordelia]] later down her life. As [[Cordelia]] had a background where she was fed properly regularly while [[Amadea]] was starving, [[Amadea]] views this as a direct impact in their development, particularly on the fact that [[Cordelia]] is slightly taller than her, and that she has a bigger chest. This difference is usually buried beneath her [[Fatalistic Embracer]] shield, but whenever she allows herself to feel, like on the [[Eclipsed Waterfalls]] scenes, or when she's drunk, she does ask [[Cordelia]] why are her breasts bigger than hers, and if it's because she was well fed, usually using this humor as a way to vent given the embarrassment from the aristocrat. This is the reason why on the second scene of the [[Eclipsed Waterfalls]], she pursues [[Cordelia]] and grabbing her breasts from behind to Supplex [[Cordelia]] into the water.

Moreover, [[Amadea]]'s resentment toward [[Cordelia]] becomes ambivalent as the story progresses: _"You can hate the system and still love the person who is part of it." - [[Amadea]] to [[Cyril]] her bartender informant._

- She resents [[Cordelia]]'s privilege (she never starved, never watched her mother die), and it's the insecurity of her own body regarding [[Cordelia]] being more bodily developed than her.
- She pities [[Cordelia]]'s suffering (she was beaten, humiliated, caged). She is as much of a victim as she is, just under a different cage. During her time as a spy in the aristocracy she learns that the grass is never greener on the other side.
- She admires [[Cordelia]]'s courage (she chose to follow [[Amadea]] despite knowing the cost).
- She feels guilt for resenting her, because [[Cordelia]] is genuinely kind and has done nothing wrong except be born into the wrong class. Which is the reason of [[Amadea]]'s borderline suicidal mission of rescue that culminates in Act 21.

This ambivalence also is drawn with [[Luminaire]] who had a different type of abuse despite being the royal heir and having a different issue than [[Cordelia]], yet the attraction [[Amadea]] develops for [[Luminaire]] is complicated too as desire learns to separate personhood from the symbol she represents.

[[Luminaire]] views [[Amadea]] as her [[Vowed Nemesis]] before the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] forced proximity due to [[Amadea]] constantly ruining and actively teasing [[Luminaire]] when [[Amadea]] outwits her smarts. [[Amadea]]'s constant insults while being elusive and running away, is what originally begins making [[Luminaire]] obnoxious to her presence. However, it completely tips for [[Luminaire]] when [[Amadea]] ruins her 5th [[Real Cycle Birthday]] party as that was the singlehandedly most important cultural, and theological moment in her entire life, even more so because her last [[Real Cycle Birthday]] happened after [[Daedalus]] died and she was doomed to be crowned the crystalline heir of [[Hollowmarch]] and her own [[Motif Awakening]]. She saw her future 5th [[Real Cycle Birthday]] as the escape she desperately needed, and instead she was humiliated in the most important theological moment of her life. While, [[Luminaire]] gets to hate [[Amadea]] for a while, the fact that she saved her life when they were both cast down the [[Lunar Abyss]] is the first domino that leads towards the healing of the resolution of both of their arcs and the eventual enemy-to-lovers as [[Amadea]]'s teasing invertedly is making [[Luminaire]] feel treated as a real person. The maximum their relationship realizes is [[Sworn Duet]] but it degrades for [[Luminaire]] into [[Dim Tuning]] after she is being crushed by the [[Seven Weights]] before deciding to defect to [[The Hollowing]] of [[Lacrimosa]].

The lore introduces the "Age of Silk," featuring Memory Weavers who use magic to craft cloth into armor. The narrative should detail the tactile, mundane maintenance of [[Amadea]]'s [[Shadow Order]] garments. Later, the cloaks of the Great Justiciars are woven with Strand magic, requiring the wearers to manually stitch the names or memories of the children they protect into the hems. The physical act of sewing and repairing torn cloaks by firelight emphasizes the grueling labor of protection, making the armor a literal fabric of memory. This type of cloak is used by [[Lenore]] as it's part of the [[Textile Arts]]. [[Amadea]] uses this as her first type of [[Strand]] magic as it's useful for repairing clothing as well.


[[Amadea]] wears her [[Soul Leitmotif]] on the back of her right hand, while wearing [[Artus]] [[Sky Glass]] on her lap because [[Artus]] wore his [[Soul Leitmotif]] on his own lap. She does this to honor and mirror his memory while maintaining in her hands the agency of her own power. (And since she uses the [[Crystal]] [[Hollow-Point]] it helps to have it appear on command from her hand to avoid the mili second delay of [[Signal Loss]]) 

Explore more the mundane, frustrating process of maintaining a [[Primary Instrument]]. Wood warps in the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] forcing her to change violin; strings snap during combat without a [[Soul-Binding]] ritual.

[[Amadea]] takes her time to carefully re-rosin her bow, tune her strings, and polish the wood of her [[Primary Instrument]]. She learned this from [[Elaine]] in her childhood and it's one of the grounding practices and rituals that she has whenever she's feeling down. She uses it primarily to think, and the specific task is one of the triggers for [[Perfect Focus]] when planning, which becomes very apparent on drafting  the plans when she's about to infiltrate the estate to rescue [[Cordelia]] and later on the narrative during the [[Shadow Order]] wars with [[Pollux]].

[[Cordelia]]'s Libretto, also known as the [[Wandering Libretto]], or by the formal name [[Cordelia]] gave it "A Rhapsody for the Unfinished Waltz" is the diary of [[Cordelia]], which is the record log where she tracks her emotions and her journey alongside [[Amadea]]. This libretto is the record of their travels and contains notes on many things, from the early version of [[The Principles of Magic]], to the [[Hollow-Point]], [[Detective Magic]], [[Illusory Magic]], the other [[Mythical Virtuoso]], and most, notably the Sheet Music and the lyrics of the [[Waltz of Wandering Love]].  The name itself is an interaction between the characters, [[Amadea]] is the one that calls it the [[Wandering Libretto]] but [[Cordelia]] insists its real name is what she named it as the "Rhapsody for the Unfinished Waltz." Only after it becomes a sacred text in [[Iridia]] does [[Amadea]] call it by the name [[Cordelia]] gave it as its official title.

The [[Wandering Libretto]] is one of the "[[Magical Relic]]s" that precede the [[Law of Relics]]despite its lack of actual tangible magical properties due to the amount of [[Echoing Bonds]] infused in it. Furthermore, within the lore, it is one of the foundational books for [[Ballad]]s for [[Iridia]], and after [[Cordelia]]'s hollowing, the libretto becomes one of the sacred objects of [[Iridia]], being the infallible evidence of [[Cordelia]]'s ability as a [[Great Chronicler]], and as a reminder that her friend was once [[Jolly]] which makes the emotions of [[Humanity]] worth fighting for. After [[The Hollowing]] ends, in [[Ages]] IV onwards, the [[Wandering Libretto]] does shift from being a sacred book into one of the first [[World-Bending Relics]] due to the amount of [[Consensual Anchoring]] and the roots it has to [[Amadea]] and [[Cordelia]]. This transition in [[Ages]] IV formally makes it a [[Magical Relic]] and [[Magical Catalyst]] that lasted for much longer than the lives of either [[Cordelia]] or [[Amadea]].

[[Iridia]]'s [[World-Bending Relics]] are the [[Wandering Libretto]] and the [[Crown of Feathered Obsidian]] which are both tied to [[Amadea]]'s journey and her identity as the founder of the Violet Grove. One contains the entire passage with [[Cordelia]], the second is the crown of [[Iridia]] that has the [[Sky Glass]] [[Resonance Box]] of [[Artus]] as the core jewel in its center. The [[Crown of Feathered Obsidian]] is also imbued with the essence of [[Dear Fugue]], as they're essentially the same construct.

Outside of [[Arcanoria]], the diary of the [[Wandering Libretto]] is accessible through the Patreon as the artbook of [[Amadea]] in [[The White-Haven Library]].

Tease one of the [[Crisis Wonders]] from "[[Ages]] V" of the [[First Reset]] during the encounter of [[Formless Father]] vs the [[Auric Aria]].

Add sensory and physical details of all the characters and their visual qualities.

When [[Luminaire]] goes back to [[Hollowmarch]], her family and the state is relieved that she is alive, and the ideas of her getting killed and betrayed by [[Sephira]] were nothing more than rumors. However, they will say that they are relieved because they don't know what they would do without their greatest asset in the entire empire. This will hurt [[Luminaire]] while accepting it as her duty to see the future entrusted to her, she was expecting relief that she's alive because she is their daughter, but instead she finds that they like that she's back because they were mourning her function as a tool for the empire.

Talk more about the environments during [[Amadea]]'s travels pointing at the gothic aesthetic and the legendary beasts of the places in the [[Age of Behemoths]] and the [[Age of Glyphs]]. Primarily through more types of [[Enclave]]s.

The fighting style of [[Amadea]] has incorporated direct movements of [[Artus]] [[Spellweaving]]. When [[Artus]] fought, he used to channel spikes and layers of [[Crystal]] as a massive slash that followed after his saber attacks. [[Amadea]] later adopts this into the [[Hollow-Point]] by increasing her own range through [[Crystal]] infused weapons, and her signature volley of [[Obsidian Feather]]s is a jagged version of this same attack but with [[Amadea]] conjuring the [[Crystal]]s to be attached with [[Void]], and ripple outward as waves of her attacks as delayed lethal traps. She also uses a variation of [[Resonance]] leaps based on [[Artus]] technique but with [[Topological Arts]] and [[Feather Fall Arts]].

Originally, as [[Artus]] and her grew up with [[Lenore]], they all questioned who would be the next of the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] like [[Daedalus]] and [[Aurelian]]. [[Amadea]] fantasized about using [[Luminance]] once she awakens so that she would become the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] of [[Luminance]] alongside [[Artus]] who would outshine [[Daedalus]] or reinvent [[Crystal]] magic entirely with his confidence and [[Ballad]]s making it applicable to other elements, it didn't matter that there was already a [[Crystal]] [[Mythical Virtuoso]], they would rewrite history together. In the end, [[Amadea]] does become a [[Mythical Virtuoso]] but of [[Void]], and she enters a love-hate relationship with the actual [[Mythical Virtuoso]] of [[Luminance]], [[Luminaire]].

In the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]], [[Cordelia]] adopts a [[Pure Light]] creature on her time alone. When it grows up much later, this creature becomes cornerstone for [[Iridia]] later when [[Cordelia]] defects to [[The Hollowing]] and it's part of the emblem of the shield of the [[Artusian Knight]]s as it grew to be a massive fluff protector. The fluff ball is [[Cordelia]]'s own [[Dear Fugue]], and is what she rides into battle alongside [[Amadea]] as a [[Mythical Virtuoso]]. With this, it explores [[Eleos Arts]] and [[Soul-Casing]] of [[Familiar]]s.

[[Elaine]] is a very influential figure for [[Amadea]] as she basically learns from the the Violin and [[Dance]] which is the signature of her magic. Moreover, [[Elaine]] has a prismatic spear made of [[Crystal]] by his partner [[Kay]] that she syncs with [[Luminance]]. This is one of the vivid memories [[Amadea]] has of her past, and it's what she uses for the [[Dancing Blade Arts]] and the very principle of her [[Obsidian Feather]]s of [[Crystal]] / [[Void]]. Later during the war of the [[Shadow Order]]s, [[Ligeia]] the armored singer of the [[Court of Delicacies]] uses a very similar technique, which is a shocking moment for [[Amadea]] when she sees [[Sephira]] lose against her.

Moreover, [[Elaine]] and [[Kay]] danced together to fight, this was her ideal relationship that she wanted to have with [[Artus]], and when [[Artus]] dies it's one of the things that haunts her, especially when dealing with the [[Purest of Love]] because [[Seraph]] & [[Arioch]] are both dancers together that are a glimpse into the future that [[Amadea]] wanted with [[Artus]]. Both her past and her future are haunted by seeing two couples do dance fighting while she had to be alone by herself. The partner [[Dance]] style is adopted by the [[Artusian Knight]]s as her atonement when she founds [[Iridia]],

[[Lazarus]] and the [[Eleos Bloom]] carnivorous nymphs that he has are the first pre cursor to [[Velvet Nectar]] which is what the certain variations of [[Eleos Bloom]] nymphs produce by feeding on [[Emotional Residue]] and physical fluids of a subjugated victim, the byproduct creates flowers the same type as their [[Soul Leitmotif]] primary color, these are harvested afterwards as they are highly exquisite leaves that produce incredibly rich tea. [[Lazarus]] dubs these as [[Velvet Nectar]] and it's the first variation. However, they don't go anywhere besides his own garden once he is assassinated by [[Pollux]] and take only after [[Ages]] IV with the velvet cage continuing the legacy once [[Lacrimosa]] became the [[Law of Relics]]. The Velvet Cage enclave reclaims the identity of the [[Court of Delicacies]] much later, and treats [[Lazarus]] as the first gardener while despising [[Pollux]] for being the barbarian who interrupted the bloom out of "envy" of his garden.

Amadea drinks one of the velvet nectar teas during the infiltration missions before she knows what they are, she says it's the sweetest thing she's ever tasted and it's only rivalled by the purest of auric peaches but that this herbal infusion is arguably better. She receives it from a noble close to Lazarus garden, it's the distilled version of a subjugated victim that fell to the corpse bouquet but she doesn't know this yet.  She becomes horrified by the revelation later in the narrative when she discovers after killing the nymph what the tea actually is and that she almost became tea herself if she was beaten by the nymph and if it "pollinated" her.

[[Amadea]] has a conflicting problem of figuring out who her mother is, whether her biological mother or [[Elaine]]. This stems from the fact that she can't recall most of her time with her biological mother besides some details of her singing, the fact that she called her [[Amadea]] as her only true inheritance, and the memories of when they were running together and that she sacrificed herself before she blew up in [[Static Criticality]] when she left her with [[Corvin]] and the caravan. While [[Elaine]], while not her mother, did provide for her the architecture of her magic. This becomes notorious when she's isolated and mourning in the acts where she feels lonely the most, like the moment she's having nightmares about [[Artus]] that then spiral into nightmares about forgetting her past in general. She wants to recall more about her time with her biological mother but she is unable to do so, she doesn't truly remember her name either.

She doesn't recall her father at all so she sees [[Corvin]] entirely as her father. She speculates that it is likely that his mother raised her alone as his father died with the [[Great Plague]] much earlier than she can remember. For one moment in Score VI of [[Strand]] she decides if she should go back to her homeland to find her past through any type of [[Memory Field]], but decides not to as she has to look first for the Violet Grove or else she will stop moving forward entirely. This lack of information gets weaponized by [[Pollux]] when she prompts her about her past as he realizes [[Amadea]] desperately wants to know about her biological family whenever she's at her worst. [[Pollux]] doesn't know either because those specific details were impossible for him to recover when she went to hunt for [[Amadea]]'s past but uses it as bluff for her to break and submit to the [[Court of Delicacies]].

When [[Amadea]] uncovers the story of [[Mira]] and the Girl Left Behind Who Changed a God she realizes the profound similarities of her story with [[Mira]], both being left by the sacrifice of their respective parents due to the [[Great Plague]], orphaned, and had a surrogate father figure that was slightly detached due to their own issues with repressed trauma and their respective couples. It offers a mirror to herself on how she could have been [[Mira]], and that it is haunting how [[Lacrimosa]] came to resonate so deeply with her. A terrifying "What if" that could've invited the greatest antagonist of the age on her very own flesh and body.

[[Amadea]] encounters early in the journey the [[Sprite-Light Conclave]] with [[Cordelia]] and she loves these creatures a lot. When she later founds [[Iridia]] she particularly the branch that focuses on [[Slime]]s for personal relief and actively patronizes them once she has founded [[Iridia]] and found stable footing, having a branch within [[Iridia]] of these creatures added into her [[Civic]] plays. In the canon, the [[Sprite-Light Conclave]] submits to [[The Hollowing]] as part of the league of [[Enclave]]s that fall and it's one of the primary reasons that [[Orphael]] decides to hollow as well seeing the amount of despair on the falling [[Enclave]]s. Later this is one of [[Amadea]]'s critical moments against [[Lacrimosa]] as [[Iridia]] is a safe-haven for their branch of [[Slime]]s giving her purpose to defend not only [[Iridia]] but the world of [[Arcanoria]] in general.

The [[Orphael]] + [[Sephira]] dynamic is based on one being Dumb and the other being Dumber, which is why they ended up being so fitting for each other, and with one another is the only place they can fully be both dumb instead of the paranoid [[Volatile]] [[Coward]] [[Sephira]] usually is and [[Orphael]] being [[Philosophical]] and drowning in self deprecating guilt. This is also what makes the loss of [[Orphael]] to [[The Hollowing]] so devastating for [[Sephira]].

The fractured [[Silver Blood]] kingdom is where the court of delicacies operates on the south eastern border of the main continent, [[Luminaire]] despises having to have political connections to them due to the fact that they deal with [[Demi-Human]] slavery and that's the main political and ideological clash with [[Hollowmarch]]. Moreover, [[Luminaire]] says the only reason [[Hollowmarch]] hasn't conquered the entire area is because it's too far away and the empire is big enough as it is. They're geographically lucky else one more [[Atrocity]] would justify their entire anhilation.

[[Iridia]] is founded beneath one of the mythical and largest [[Eclipsed Waterfalls]] in [[Arcanoria]] that separates the Violet Grove from the normal landscape. [[Orphael]] has connections to it given his tide singing nature, and he wasn't born that far away from it which is the home he ran away from.

After achieving her [[Reality Bender]] [[Legend Trait]] by confronting [[Lazarus]] and rescuing [[Cordelia]], [[Amadea]] begins making a name for herself, which is further popularized by her [[Ballad]]s on the Boreal Reach. [[Amadea]] has been hearing about a legendary warrior throughout the plot but she didn't know she was that same warrior being her under a different name. It's the beginning of her story as a [[Legend]]. This moment of realization to [[Amadea]] that the legendary prophet that has been on the ballads is her is properly signaled on a [[Civic]] play in [[Iridia]] during the seed of [[Revelation]]. The [[Ballad]]s she played and the fame she made against [[Lazarus]] made her famous, and for a while she was tracking the [[Legend]] of herself without knowing.

The title "Violet Empress" was not born from flowers, but from blood. As the historian of the [[Court of Delicacies]] reveals during the planted agent that [[Pollux]] sent to destroy [[Amadea]]'s worldview: "That's the color the [[Glimmerfern]] tree drenched the moment her [[Silver Blood]] blended with the [[Lunehymn]] at the steps of her throne. The name itself, is the color that [[Junius]] recorded after the tragedy when it appeared in the ground the moment [[Medea]] died from bleeding out. The entire legend [[Amadea]] modeled her life after was built upon the bloody, violet-stained ground of a murdered woman killed by her closest companions. [[Cordelia]] realizes this way before the truth is said by [[Pollux]] when she sees the wounded armored singer bleed in the [[Lunehymn]] near [[Glimmerfern]]. She doesn't tell [[Amadea]], and the fact that she got half the truth right, but completely failed to uncover the link of the [[Waltz of Wandering Love]] is what sends her in the Spiraling state. [[Cordelia]] did figure out that the [[Ballad]] [[Amadea]] had was about [[Medea]] dying and violet is the color of the drenched blood.
 
[[Orphael]] is a profound figure for [[Amadea]] to begin learning how to express and process her own emotions and turn the grief of her [[Flux]] into transforming music. He does this from his own experience with being [[Lustful]] and the same it leaves behind translating directly into his [[Tide Singing Arts]] as a [[Romantic Hedonist]] and with his worst parts of his [[Self-Indulgent Slave]] [[Weight of Nature]] that drove him to the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] in a suicidal mission in the first place. The exchange is the key that [[Amadea]] requires to change the ghost of [[Artus]] through [[Flux]] into the music and accompaniment of [[Dear Fugue]]. He is also a critical piece for establishing the [[Enclave]] connection of [[Iridia]] and developing the [[Civic]] of [[Ballad]]s & Fantasy plays.

Some of the inspiration for the practices of the [[Civic]] of [[Ballad]]s & Fantasy plays is directly inspired by the named children of her childhood in the caravan. [[Lynette]] inspires the ritual before performers enter where the brief exchange of the actors signals that no show starts until every hand is accounted for. [[Chen]] mastery over repairing is the symbol he corrected for [[Artus]] that eventually becomes the sigil of [[Iridia]]'s entire waterworks division, and even [[Carmina]] becomes the scripts that represent the lead roles through shapes to explain the main characters.

When the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] begin losing to [[The Hollowing]] [[Amadea]] adopts a ring in each of her fingers of her left hand to represent the 5 [[Mythical Virtuoso]], she has a ring in each finger for [[Sephira]], [[Cordelia]], [[Orphael]], [[Luminaire]], and herself, and it's her reminder of their legacy against the [[All-Loving Moon]]. She also roots herself with her fingers as [[Amadea]] usually relies on tactile intelligence to ground herself as she's been doing since childhood.

[[Amadea]]'s journey in childhood has the essence of made in abyss walking through mythical dark fantasy landscapes on the original continent where she was born. There's a scene where they're all taking a bath in the mythical [[Eclipsed Waterfalls]] and that's something that sticks in the mind of young [[Amadea]], becomes a motif when she's with Artus, and again with [[Cordelia]] when they're together. Ultimately, this is the reason why the Thermal Baths of [[Iridia]] exist as a [[Civic]]. They are bound to the volcanoes region near [[Iridia]] as much as they are to [[Amadea]]'s memory of her childhood,  [[Artus]], and the journey with the [[Mythical Virtuoso]].

The [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] have a similar scenery on the crescent veils and that's why it triggers her memories of childhood compounding her nostalgia and isolation. Likewise, [[Amadea]]'s feeling of inhaling cold air is a quirk of [[Amadea]] that gets developed during these times with the caravan and becomes a recurring motif for her own environmental experience recurring from childhood to the very [[Crescent Mist Peaks]].

While [[Cordelia]] canonically chooses [[The Hollowing]] and this is the moment [[Amadea]]
reaches her [[Singularity of True Love]] [[Void]] [[Apex Trait]] in [[Gateway To Genesis]] it is possible to change the course of history just like how it is possible to prevent [[Medea]]'s tragedy. It changes parts of the storyline but it maintains [[Cordelia]] as a core member of the resistance instead of [[The Hollowing]]. To achieve this, the [[Third Actor]] has to engage in the [[Romantic Interest]] system and marry [[Cordelia]]. Regardless of the gender, triggering this specific quest for building a family for her is the lifeline that saves her just like [[Ligeia]]. Canonically it doesn't arrive, but [[Cordelia]] is the only of the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] that has a romance option precisely for this interaction to completing the [[Waltz of Wandering Love]]. If it's a [[Romantic Interest]] of the opposite gender, it is possible to have her children as founding [[Legend]]s and [[Artusian Knight]]s.

The decision of saving [[Cordelia]] does change which [[Apex Trait]] [[Amadea]] has as her [[Void]] [[Apex Trait]] but she still gets the three [[Apex Trait]]s as it is the "Good Ending" of the [[Mythical Virtuoso]]. It's not possible to save either [[Orphael]] or [[Luminaire]], but [[Cordelia]] is the one exception. If she doesn't hollow, she will die alongside [[Amadea]] and [[Sephira]] in the start of [[Ages]] III leaving the legacy to [[Iridia]]. This weakens the [[Purest of Love]] as she is no longer a key actor in [[Ages]] III during the fights with the [[Eight-Wings of Lacrimosa]]. While [[Amadea]] is a [[Constellation]] individually stronger if [[Cordelia]] defects, she obtains a unique [[Legend Opus]] for dying of old age while [[Cordelia]] is still alive. [[Flesh-Binding Ritual]]

#### Evolution & Physical Description of [[Amadea]]

[[Amadea]] has 7 phases across her life that correlate to major time skips, plot arcs, and her biological age that are a clear indicator of time passing from just character design. Her character design is condensed in the following:

**Core Physical Features**

Regardless of the phase of her life, [[Amadea]] possesses a consistent set of striking physical traits that anchor her identity.

- **Hair:** She has thick, wavy, silvery-white hair. In her younger traveling days, she wears it loose around her face, with a prominent, thick braid draped over her left shoulder.

- **Eyes:** Her eyes are a luminous, piercing teal or cyan-green. They are highly expressive, capturing both the playful, theatrical side she uses as a facade and the hardened, intense glare of a survivor.

- **Build & Complexion:** She is 1.66 cm tall as an adult. She has a fair complexion with a slender, athletic build that gets forged further by [[Cycle]]s of rugged survival, rigorous physical training, and the demanding choreography of her [[Dancing Blade Arts]]. Across all of her life, she carries the same Origin [[Legend Trait]]s of [[Attractive]] and [[Cunning]] though they only become useful when she reaches phase 3 of Violet, the Wandering Actress.

Finally, [[Amadea]] has a total of 4 violin changes during her entire epic, mirroring how [[Void]] is the fourth auric thread of the [[Auric Heptacode]].

- **Violin 1 | Ash-Choked Violin (Act 7-18):** This is the one she grew with from the [[Militant Enclave]]. it gets destroyed and broken when [[Kastor]] defeats her and steals [[Artus]] [[Sky Glass]].

- **Violin 2 | Velvet Linen Violin (Act 19-35):** She gets it from the aristocracy through [[Cyril]], the bartender informant when she infiltrates, it's broken by the climax of the [[Facewalker]] [[Erosyx]] encounter after saying _"So Long, [[Artus]]."_

- **Violin 3 | Boreal Threaded Violin (Act 37-54):** She commissions it to the Boreal Reach pilgrims, and it's the one she uses when she founds [[Iridia]], it is broken on the aerial fight with [[Pollux]] on the crumbling [[Dear Fugue]] alongside the [[Celestial Astrolabe]] that [[Lenore]] gifted her as she is saved upward.

- **Violin 4 | Featherwood Threaded Violin (Act 55-84):** This is the signature violin of [[Amadea]], the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] of the [[Void]] that she gains on her title change to the Obsidian Empress. This final violin is the one that the statues remember her with.

##### Phase 1. Child [[Amadea]]
_First Half of Movement 1: The Dream_

This is the pre-design phase, she has no defined look of her own. She's a feral, forgotten child. Wearing whatever scraps the caravan gives her. Mostly oversized, patched, and burned. She tends to wear the clothing of a child that inherited a dead person's clothes. However, [[Corvin]] does the same, she's surrounded by this culture of salvaged cloth.

Her hair is first unkempt, tangled, as she's a child who's been through static criticality, starvation, and grief. The only comfort comes from [[Elaine]] who teaches her how to braid her hair during her late travels on the nights of the caravan fires. This is the only anchor that becomes identity later as a mixture of pragmatism, survival, and the basics of care.

##### Phase 2. Teenager [[Amadea]] (Age 15-19)
_Second Half of Movement 1: The Dream_

This is the proper first half design that considers [[Amadea]] as she's grown up into her teenage [[Cycle]]s and the golden era with [[Artus]] and [[Lenore]] at the [[Militant Enclave]]. She keeps the tradition of having her hair for practical use tied as having it down signals that she's stepping back into the time in her childhood where she was abandoned. It's the first steps of the masks that become justified by the pragmatism of staying alive in the heat of battle.

[[Artus]] and [[Amadea]] develop their dancing style which becomes the root of why [[Amadea]] focuses on having functional clothing before it is regal looking, opting for anything that functions as protection.

##### Phase 3. The Wandering Actress, Violet (Age 20-23)
_First Half of Movement 2: The World_

![[Amadea Wandering Violet Sheet.png]]

During her time performing as "Violet" and traveling with [[Cordelia]], her aesthetic blends theatrical flair with practical wilderness survival. It is a sort of "mix" of every features the adults in the caravan had, from the waist down, she looks exactly like [[Corvin]] wearing too metallic kneepads, she also has pair of gauntlets of [[Kay]], and the fur collar of [[Elaine]]. It refines her identity as a performer but still rooted in the lessons she learned in childhood, she reclaims the singular messy braid as a connection to her past.

- **The Violet Tunic:** She wears a vibrant magenta or dark violet tunic featuring intricate, dark cross-lacing and reduced trim detailing.
    
- **The Dark Capelet:** Draped over her shoulders is a dark black capelet, heavily accented by a black fur collar ("Fluff Black Fur Trim") that provides warmth in harsh environments. This is later replaced by the proper cape she wears during the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] that later becomes her [[Obsidian Feather]] mantle once she becomes the Obsidian Empress of [[Iridia]].
    
- **Utility Gear:** Her lower half is outfitted for relentless travel, featuring dark brown utility leather pants, a series of leather belts holding small pouches, and sturdy strapped boots equipped with metallic knee pads, these knee pads are the same as the ones [[Corvin]] used.
    
- **Gloves & Sleeves:** She wears brown, fingerless leather gloves that allow her the tactile dexterity required to play her violin and wield her saber.

This phase also features her alternate dress as the aristocratic infiltrator after [[Kastor]] steals the [[Sky Glass]] and [[Amadea]] rescues [[Cordelia]] from her family estate, she goes undercover in aristocratic circles to track down the [[Court of Delicacies]].

Moreover, this phase also includes the clothing she adopts as an aristocratic disguise as the clothing of the class she's been stealing from her entire life. This is deeply ironic and uncomfortable for her. She's infiltrating [[Dreamweaver]] parades, [[Luminaire]]'s parties, and [[Cordelia]]'s family estate.

- **Key Detail:** She's wearing the costume of the enemy — the people who built "rooms with cotton sponges." This is a visual inversion of the Violet phase: instead of standing out as a performer, she's blending into high society.

- **Origin of the [[Obsidian Feather]]s:** The [[Hollow-Point]] and her [[Obsidian Feather]]s are born here as well as her [[Void]] + [[Crystal]] compressed saber. The design of her combat style shifts from theatrical dance to assassination precision.

- **Transitional Disguise Phase:** She's not wandering as Violet anymore, but reclaiming her name on the dream of the Violet Empress. She's not yet the imposing figure she becomes afterward, she's a spy in borrowed clothes. The visual tension of "[[Amadea]] wearing aristocratic finery" is a powerful contrast to the ragged caravan child.
##### Phase 4. The [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] [[Amadea]] (Age 23-25)
_Second Half of Movement 2: The World_

The character of [[Amadea]] evolves to survive the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] with winter clothing, adopts the signature fluff cape that will be replaced with [[Obsidian Feather]]s, and she let's looser her hair. She keeps most of her looks from the previous phase but adapted to her new clothing, and begins developing her relationship with [[Luminance]] and [[Illusory Magic]] with [[Luminaire]] as she embeds her signature [[Obsidian Feather]]s to be used as attractive beacons filled with light that confuse or captivate whomever sees [[Amadea]], or her [[Silhouette Arts]] clones, practicing her [[Dancing Blade Arts]] mirroring the modern complex moodhoops of LED hula hoops displays. 

It's the middle point between her grounded and practical outfit as a survivor while adopting the aristocracy class and her own signature style to reclaim and build an identity for her own so that she believes to be one day like the Violet Empress.

##### Phase 5. The Violet Empress of [[Iridia]] [[Amadea]] (Age 26-32)
_Entire Movement 3: The Shattering_

This is the first time she has proper clothing and swaps out the fluff cape for a proper regal cloak by putting more violet into it. While originally she's against the idea of the aristocracy, she uses the opportunity to create a new fashion of goth styled clothing based on her [[Obsidian Feather]]s. This is also reflected in her hairstyle as now she wears a waterfall braid, with the rest of her hair left down and loose. The braid stars at one temple worked horizontally across her head, at each cross, one strand is dropped and a new piece of loose hair picked up, the dropped strands fall into the length below, blending into loose waves.

She's now a ruler in progress, not yet the Obsidian Empress, but no longer the wandering actress. The capelet structure from the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] is still with her, but she's adding regal elements, the beginnings of the crown, more structured clothing that reflects her role as [[Great Justiciar]] under her own terms.

She's wearing a mix of the old traveling clothes and new regal elements. The crown isn't fully formed yet. The mantle isn't obsidian yet. She's in transition.

##### Phase 6. The Obsidian Empress [[Amadea]] (Age 32-36)
_First Half of Movement 4: The Resistance_

The war transforms her clothing into armor. The [[Court of Delicacies]] conflict is a shadow war — [[Amadea]] is leading the [[Obsidian Feather Society]] against fox-masked assassins. Her clothing becomes more martial — the capelet is now a proper combat feathered mantle, the tunic is reinforced. Her hair is fully down now as she's reclaimed agency over her own life.

As [[Amadea]] steps into her role as the North Star of [[Iridia]] and the leader of the [[Shadow Order]] of the [[Obsidian Feather Society]], right on the aftermath of the climax with [[Pollux]] and her win against the [[Court of Delicacies]]. Her appearance matures into a darker, more imposing, and regal gothic aesthetic.

- **The Feathered Mantle:** She trades the simple cloak she's been carrying since the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] for a dramatic mantle made of crystallized, dark [[Obsidian Feather]]s, directly reflecting her signature [[Dear Fugue]] construct.

- **The Crown of Feathered Obsidian:** She wears a striking, jagged crown that partially masks the right side of her face. The crown is forged from dark, feather-like spikes and houses the glowing, refined [[Sky Glass]] [[Resonance Box]] of [[Artus]] as its central jewel.

##### Phase 7. The Great Owl [[Amadea]] (Age 36+)
_Second Half of Movement 4: The Resistance_

The exhausted but resolute architect, she fully leans to ruling from the shadows as the Great Owl of the [[Obsidian Feather Society]]. The crown is worn in. The mantle is fully integrated. She's been ruling for decades. The five rings are all present. This is the "final" look — the one that appears in the later acts on the transition to the [[Age of Legends]] where she's paving the path for the next generation into the resolution of [[The Hollowing]] and the opposition of [[Iridia]]'s next generations against [[Lacrimosa]].

**Signature Marks & Relics

| **Feature**              | **Description & Lore Significance**                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **The Void Rhombus Gem** | Amadea's Soul Leitmotif manifests as a purple Void gem etched onto the back of her right hand. She wears it here deliberately to maintain agency over her own power, contrasting with Artus, who wore his on his lap.                                         |
| **The Ink Scars**        | She bears dark, intricate scars on her left arm, inflicted by Pollux's ink strings when he saved her from the collapsing Dear Fugue construct. She refuses to heal these scars, keeping them as a tether to her tragic past and the legacy of her sacrifices. |
| **The Five Rings**       | In her later life, Amadea wears five rings on the fingers of her left hand. These serve as a constant physical reminder of the five Mythical Virtuosi (herself, Sephira, Cordelia, Orphael, and Luminaire) who fought against the All-Loving Moon.            |
| **Her Instruments**      | She carries her worn featherwood violin—which she meticulously polishes and re-rosins as a grounding ritual—and her Hollow-Point Saber.                                                                                                                       |

##### Medea, the Silver Blood, and the Haunting of the Violet Empress

**The Unseen Architecture: How Medea Prefigures Amadea

The revelation that Cordelia's aristocratic family descends from the fragmentation of the Silver Blood kingdom—and that Medea's death precipitated that fragmentation—is not mere background lore. It is **structural prophecy**. Medea's story is Amadea's story, played in a minor key, ended in tragedy. The Waltz of Wandering Love, composed by Medea at the founding of her kingdom, is the *ghost note* that has been playing beneath Amadea and Cordelia's relationship from the beginning, unrecognized, unfelt, but acoustically present.

When the revelation finally lands—in the chapters of the courts of delicacies and the [[Obsidian Feather]]s opposing each other—it does not merely inform. It **shatters**. For Cordelia, it is a spiraling moment because she realizes that the family that betrayed her, the family whose blood runs in her veins, is *complicit* in the destruction of the very lineage that produced the song she has been playing. For Amadea, it is a devastating recognition: the Violet Empress—the legendary figure she has been unconsciously modeling herself after, the archetype of the sovereign who rose from loss—*never existed*. Or rather, she existed, but not as the legend tells it. Medea was not a triumphant empress. She was a woman who was betrayed, stabbed thirty-three times, left to die on the steps of her own throne, crawling toward a silver tree that could not save her.

The name "Amadea" itself—the name Artus heard in a ballad and gave to her—is a **mythic drift** of Medea's story, filtered through generations of retelling, stripped of its tragedy, polished into a fantasy of untroubled sovereignty. Amadea has been chasing a ghost who never was.

**Part I: The Silver Blood and the Fracture of Legacy

**Medea's Kingdom: A Foundation Built on Fragile Silver

The Silver Blood kingdom was not a golden age. It was a **wound made polity**—founded by Medea after the devastation of the Demi-Human wars (in the Old Testament timeline) or after some earlier catastrophe (in the New Testament). The "silver blood" itself was the mark of Selenea's blessing—a direct lineage of Pure Light that gave the ruling family heightened resonance, longer life, and a deep connection to the Lunehymn that flowed through the Leylines.

But the kingdom fell. Medea was betrayed—stabbed thirty-three times, according to the lore, by those closest to her, including [[Junius]], one of her most important confidants. She did not die immediately. She crawled to the top of her silver throne, but fell midway. The cinematic sequence of her death shows her imagining a peaceful scene: her confidants by her side, the dying tree gleaming blissfully in moonlight. The final shot transitions from her daydreaming escapist fantasy to the reality: she is alone, dying on a pool of silver blood in her ruined cathedral, having failed to crawl the steps of her throne.

**The tragedy of Medea:** She was not defeated by an external enemy. She was betrayed by her own blood, by those she trusted. Her death was not heroic sacrifice; it was *abandonment*. She died imagining a family that was not there, a peace that had never been.

**Cordelia's Family: The Inheritors of Betrayal

Cordelia's aristocratic family are not the Silver Bloods themselves—they are the **fragments** that survived the collapse, the nobles who either participated in the betrayal or stood by while it happened, and then rebuilt their power on the ruins of Medea's dream. They are the *inheritors of complicity*.

**The hidden guilt:** Cordelia does not know this. Not consciously. But the Submissive trait she carries—the readiness to be punished, the expectation that her worth is contingent on enduring suffering, the belief that love must be earned through pain—is the *psychological residue* of her family's original sin. They betrayed Medea, and then they betrayed Cordelia. The pattern repeats across generations: the family destroys what it claims to love.

**The fragmentation as original wound:** The Silver Blood kingdom did not fall because of external invasion. It fell because those who were closest to power turned on each other. The fragmentation is not just political; it is *relational*. Cordelia's family history is not a story of glory lost; it is a story of *trust broken*.

**Artus's Mythic Drift: The Ballad That Became a Lie

Artus heard a ballad about "Amadea"—the empress from the Silver Blood kingdom, the one who kept her people alive, the one who was crowned in a field of violet flowers. But that ballad was a **mythic drift** of Medea's story, filtered through centuries of retelling, stripped of its horror, polished into a fantasy of untroubled sovereignty.

**What Artus believed:** He believed in a Violet Empress who was triumphant, who built a kingdom from nothing, who was crowned in beauty and ruled in wisdom. He gave that [[Legend Title]] to Amadea when she thought her name was only the inheritance of weight, and he gave her the seed, and he told her she would be that empress.

**What was actually true:** Medea was betrayed, stabbed, left to die alone on the steps of her throne. Her kingdom fell not because she was weak, but because she trusted the wrong people. She died not in glory, but in a pool of her own silver blood, imagining a peace that never came.

**The cruel irony:** Artus's gift—the name "Amadea"—was a promise of a future that had already failed. The empress he believed in never existed except as a fantasy constructed from the ruins of a real woman's tragedy.

**Part II: The Waltz of Wandering Love as Haunted Song

**Medea's Composition: A Love That Could Not Find Its Home

The Waltz of Wandering Love was composed by Medea at the founding of the Silver Blood kingdom. It is, ostensibly, a love song—a melody about a love that tries to find love, that wanders through the world seeking its other half, that never quite arrives but never stops searching.

**The hidden meaning:** But the song was written by a woman who would later be betrayed and left to die. The "wandering love" is not just romantic longing; it is the love of a queen for her kingdom, for her family, for the people she trusted—love that was not returned, love that was answered with knives. The song's underlying somber undertones are not decoration; they are the *truth* of the composition, buried beneath the surface melody.

**The transmission to Cordelia:** Cordelia learned the Waltz from her family—not as a historical artifact, but as a *lullaby*, a traditional tune passed down through generations. She does not know its origin. She does not know it is Medea's song. She plays it every day because it is the only song she knows that makes her feel less alone. But the song carries Medea's grief in its structure, Medea's betrayal in its harmonic shifts, Medea's death in its unresolved cadences.

**The transmission to Amadea:** Cordelia plays the Waltz to Amadea in the wilderness clearing. Amadea hears it. She does not know its origin either. But the song *works*—it heals her, because the emotional authenticity of Cordelia's performance carries the accumulated weight of Medea's original grief, layered through generations of Silver Blood survivors, now offered freely to someone who is also grieving, also wandering, also looking for a love that will not betray her.

**The Spiraling Moment: When the Truth Lands

In the chapters of the courts of delicacies and the [[Obsidian Feather]]s opposing each other—when the [[Court of Delicacies]] (the Loss-Beauty Lunatics who followed [[Junius]] after Medea's death) confront the [[Obsidian Feather Society]] (Amadea's shadow order)—the truth of the Waltz's origin will be revealed.

**For Cordelia:** This is a spiraling moment. She has been playing a song composed by the woman her family betrayed. The song that has been her comfort, her identity, her connection to Amadea—was written by the *victim* of her own bloodline. She has been carrying Medea's grief without knowing it, playing Medea's longing as if it were her own, and in a sense it *is* her own—because the family that betrayed Medea also betrayed Cordelia. The pattern is the same. The song is the witness.

**For Amadea:** This is a devastating recognition. The Violet Empress—the figure she has been unconsciously modeling herself after, the archetype Artus gave her—never existed. What existed was Medea: a woman who loved, who trusted, who was stabbed thirty-three times, who died alone on the steps of her throne, who crawled toward a dying tree that could not save her. The legend is a lie. The hope Artus planted was built on a myth that erased the tragedy at its core.

**The realization:** "The Violet Empress never existed." The woman who came before ended in tragedy. There is no unbroken lineage of sovereign power. There is only Medea, bleeding out, alone, imagining a peace that was not there.

**Part III: The [[Court of Delicacies]] — Loss-Beauty Lunacy

**[[Junius]] and the Followers: The Aestheticization of Grief

[[Junius]] was one of Medea's most important confidants—the one who betrayed her, who stabbed her, who left her to die. After Medea's death, [[Junius]] and his followers did not simply move on. They *aestheticized* the tragedy.

**Loss-Beauty Lunatic:** This is the evolution of those who followed [[Junius]]. They do not grieve Medea; they *worship* the image of her death. The silver blood, the dying tree, the moonlit throne, the final cinematic of her imagining peace—these become *artifacts* of a beauty that can only exist through loss. They wear masks not to hide their faces, but to *become* the masks—to perform the tragedy, to live inside the aesthetic of betrayal, to turn Medea's suffering into a *style*.

**The [[Court of Delicacies]]:** This is the organization that has been haunting Cordelia and Amadea since the pugilist attack. [[Kastor]], the fox-masked pugilist is one of them. They are not merely assassins; they are *theologians of loss*. They believe that beauty is only real when it is destroyed, that love is only pure when it ends in betrayal, that the highest form of devotion is to *preserve the moment of breaking*.

**The contrast with the [[Obsidian Feather Society]]:** Amadea's shadow order operates on the opposite principle: "We die in the silence so that you may sing of tomorrow." The [[Obsidian Feather Society]] does not aestheticize loss; they *transform* it. They do not worship the moment of breaking; they use it as fuel to protect others from breaking. The [[Court of Delicacies]] preserves the wound; the [[Obsidian Feather Society]] heals the wound—or at least, prevents it from being inflicted on others.

**The Mirror Between Courts

| [[Court of Delicacies]]                 | [[Obsidian Feather Society]]                                            |
| --------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Founded by [[Junius]] (the betrayer)    | Founded by Amadea (the betrayed)                                        |
| Loss-Beauty Lunatic: loss as aesthetic  | Loss as transformation: "To have lost greatly is to have loved greatly" |
| Preserves the moment of breaking        | Prevents breaking from happening to others                              |
| Worships Medea's death                  | Honors Medea's life (eventually, after the revelation)                  |
| Masks as identity (perform the tragedy) | Shadows as protection (die in the silence)                              |
| The past as eternal wound               | The past as foundation for the future                                   |

The [[Court of Delicacies]] is what happens when grief is not witnessed, when betrayal is not healed, when the only response to loss is to *enshrine* it. The [[Obsidian Feather Society]] is what happens when grief is transformed into service, when the witness refuses to look away, when the only response to loss is to *prevent* it from happening to someone else.

**Amadea's Shattering on the [[Weight of Purpose]] and Rebuilding

**"The Violet Empress Never Existed"

This realization—arriving in the confrontation between the courts—is Amadea's second death of Artus. The first was his physical death. This is the death of the *fantasy* he gave her: the dream of a pure, unbroken lineage of violet sovereigns, the hope that she could become the empress of a legend that had never been true.

**The crisis of identity:** If Medea was not the triumphant Violet Empress, then what is Amadea building toward? If the legend is a lie, then is Iridia also a lie? If the name "Amadea" was given to her based on a mythic drift of a tragedy, then who is she really?

**The answer that emerges:** The legend was a lie, but the *song* was true. Medea did compose the Waltz of Wandering Love. Medea did love, did trust, did hope. She was betrayed, but the love was real. The tragedy does not erase the love. It *includes* it.

**Artus's Mythic Drift as Gift and Curse

Artus heard a ballad that had drifted from Medea's story, and he believed it. He gave Amadea the name, the seed, the promise. He did not know he was passing on a myth. He believed—with all his heart—that the Violet Empress was real, that the violet grove existed, that the future could be built on the foundation of that legend.

**The gift:** Artus gave Amadea a *direction*. Without the legend, she might never have left the caravan, never have picked up the violin, never have survived the death of her mother. The myth was a *necessary fiction*—a star to navigate by, even if the star was not actually a star but a reflection of a reflection.

**The curse:** The myth also blinded her. She has been chasing an ideal of untroubled sovereignty that never existed, believing that if she could just reach the violet grove, everything would be resolved. The revelation that the violet grove is not a place—that Medea died alone, that the silver blood kingdom fell, that the legend is a lie *and always was*—is the breaking of the [[Celestial Astrolabe]]. She must learn to navigate without the star.

**The Reconstruction: Iridia as Honest Foundation

What rises from this shattering is not a continuation of the myth, but a **new foundation**—one built not on the fantasy of an untroubled past, but on the *recognition* that all founding is flawed, all myth is drift, all legends are filtered through loss.

**Amadea's new understanding:** She does not need to be the Violet Empress. The Violet Empress never existed. What exists is Medea's love, Medea's betrayal, Medea's death—and Cordelia's playing of the Waltz, and Artus's belief, and her own stubborn refusal to stop moving forward even when the ground collapses beneath her.

**The name "Amadea" reclaimed:** The name is no longer a promise from Artus about a future that was never real. It is a *responsibility* to the truth—to remember Medea not as a legend, but as a woman who loved and was betrayed, who died alone, whose song became a comfort to a girl whose family had betrayed her, whose tragedy became the foundation of a city built on the principle that *no one should have to bleed alone*.

**The Echo Across Generations

The Medea-Silver Blood backstory creates a **three-generation resonance**:

| Generation | Figure | Role | Outcome |
|------------|--------|------|---------|
| First | Medea | Founder of Silver Blood kingdom | Betrayed, stabbed, dies alone |
| Second | Cordelia's family | Inheritors of fragmentation | Betray Cordelia (pattern repeats) |
| Third | Cordelia | Heir to the trauma | Breaks the pattern (with Amadea's help) |

But also:

| Generation | Figure | Role | Outcome |
|------------|--------|------|---------|
| First | Medea | Composer of the Waltz | Dies, but the song survives |
| Second | Cordelia's family | Pass down the Waltz (as lullaby) | Unaware of its origin |
| Third | Cordelia | Plays the Waltz to Amadea | Heals her, bonds with her, unknowingly reenacts Medea's love |

And:

| Generation | Figure | Role | Outcome |
|------------|--------|------|---------|
| First | Medea | The "Violet Empress" of legend | Never existed as legend claims |
| Second | Artus | Hears the ballad, believes the myth | Passes the name to Amadea |
| Third | Amadea | Lives the name, builds Iridia | Discovers the myth was a lie, builds anyway |

**The Waltz as Strand: Echoing Bonds Across Time

The Waltz of Wandering Love is not just a song. It is a **Strand anchor**—a memory made audible, a bond woven across generations. Medea composed it; it passed through Cordelia's family (as a lullaby, as a tradition, as an unconscious inheritance); Cordelia played it to Amadea; Amadea will pass it to Iridia.

**The Echoing Bond:** Medea and Cordelia never met. Medea and Amadea never met. But the song connects them—not because of blood, but because of *shared wound*. Medea was betrayed by those she trusted; Cordelia was betrayed by her family; Amadea lost Artus, the only person who ever believed in her. The Waltz is the frequency that resonates through all three.

**The revelation's impact:** When Cordelia learns that the Waltz was Medea's composition, she is not learning *new* information about a historical figure. She is learning that her own grief—the grief she has been playing every day, the grief that connected her to Amadea—has a *history*. She is part of a lineage that she never chose, a lineage of pain and love that stretches back to a woman who died on the steps of her throne. The spiraling is not just shock; it is *recognition*. She has been singing Medea's story without knowing it. She has been Medea, in a sense—loving, trusting, being betrayed—and the song has been carrying her through it.

**The [[Court of Delicacies]] as Dark Mirror of Iridia

The [[Court of Delicacies]] is what Iridia could have become if Amadea had surrendered to loss rather than transforming it. They worship the moment of Medea's death; Amadea builds a city in the name of preventing such deaths. They wear masks to become the tragedy; Amadea's [[Obsidian Feather]]s work in the shadows to ensure others do not have to carry the same weight.

**The final contrast:** The [[Court of Delicacies]] preserves the wound. The [[Obsidian Feather Society]] *heals*—not by erasing the wound, but by turning it into a *shield* for others. The Loss-Beauty Lunatics say: "Loss is beautiful. We will keep it forever." Amadea says: "Loss is transformation. We will build something from it."

**Conclusion — The Haunted Song and the Honest City

The revelation of Medea's true story, the origin of the Waltz, and the nature of the [[Court of Delicacies]] does not destroy Amadea. It **rebuilds** her—on a foundation that is not the fantasy of an untroubled past, but the *truth* of a tragic one.

**For Cordelia:** She learns that her family's betrayal of her is not an isolated cruelty but a *pattern*—the same pattern that destroyed Medea. She also learns that the song she has been playing is not just her own grief; it is Medea's grief, and Selenea's grief (through the Silver Blood), and the grief of everyone who has ever loved and been betrayed. She is not alone in her wound. The Waltz is the proof.

**For Amadea:** She learns that the [[Legend Title]] Artus gave her was built on a myth—but the myth was not *empty*. It was a mythic drift of a real woman's life, a real woman's love, a real woman's death. The legend was a lie, but the *truth* beneath the legend—Medea's courage, Medea's love, Medea's refusal to stop trusting even when trust killed her—is the foundation on which Iridia can be built.

**For the novel:** The Medea backstory transforms the Waltz of Wandering Love from a simple healing song into a **haunted chord**—a melody that carries the weight of betrayal, the ache of abandonment, and the stubborn, irrational persistence of love that keeps loving even after it has been stabbed thirty-three times and left to die. That is the song Cordelia plays. That is the song Amadea hears. That is the song that becomes the anthem of Iridia—not a fantasy of untroubled sovereignty, but a hymn to the ones who keep loving anyway, who keep building anyway, who keep playing the song even after they learn that the Violet Empress never existed.

*The city is built not on a legend, but on a grave. The song is played not in triumph, but in memory. The name is carried not as a promise, but as a question: What do we owe the dead who dreamed of a future they never saw?*

*Amadea's answer: We build it anyway. We plant the seed. We play the Waltz. We keep wandering, keep loving, keep searching for a love that may never find us—because the wandering itself is the song, and the song is the only thing that makes the silence bearable.*

- [[Corvin]] pulls [[Artus]] aside when he says in one of his [[Ballad]]s a story about love, and they fight over it as it triggers [[Corvin]].

- _"No, that's wrong" [[Corvin]] says. "Great things are lost when the weather goes rampant. Always. But love is the only thing that survives. Not people. Not places. The love itself. Don't ever stop."_

- [[Artus]], confused: _"Why are you telling me this?"_
    
- [[Corvin]]: _"Because I almost did. Once. And I don't want you to make the same mistake. Here he hints at his own breakup with [[Lenore]]"_

As they learn to play, [[Artus]] changes his hummed melody of the Violet Empress while teaching [[Amadea]] the melody, but he consistently messes up the fourth bar. When [[Amadea]] points it out, [[Artus]] smiles and shares a trivial piece of lore: _"The old ballad says the Queen always paused on the thirty-third step of her [[Dance]] to look back at her court. It's just a clumsy note for a perfect queen. But in the end, it doesn't change anything if I can't figure it out correctly. A song only matters because of the silence it breaks."_

However this is not a trivial piece of lore, it signals the truth of [[Mythic Drift]], and that the unresolved cadence was not a romantic pause in a [[Dance]]. It is the acoustic scar of a murder, recording the exact moment [[Junius]] drove the first of thirty-three daggers into her, and that's the reason it's incomplete. It's what [[Junius]] heard from her, not what she wrote.
