_Act 12: A Wish For My Greatest Knight_

The transition point; Amadea's Motif Awakening and fatalistic vow.

[[Ascendant]] [[Anxithor]] at dawn, of the dreaded [[Legend]] of [[The Seven Hands]] the entire [[Militant Enclave]] had collapsed, [[Artus]]' sacrifice; the final gaze; his death; her Void awakening.

- When [[The Seven Hands]]' scythe rises, the grip is wrapped in the same leather as Corvin's coat patches. [[Artus]] realizes mid-fight who this [[Atonalis]] is, although he doesn't tell [[Amadea]]. Instead he deduces that the [[Dissonance Core]] is where [[Corvin]] wore his [[Soul Leitmotif]] as a ring in the middle finger of his right hand, which for the monster the signature matches as it is in the fourth hand of the monster on the middle finger. This is how they understand the weak point that [[Artus]] realized as a "stroke of genius" it wasn't coincidence, it was him mourning [[Corvin]].

- The hands twitch in the same counting rhythm.

- Amadea does not notice. She is fighting for her life. But the reader _might_ just like [[Artus]]—and if they do, the dread will be unbearable.

The seed pouch. The circlet on the saber. "I will plant it. At the center."

Ends the performance of the [[Ballad]], and [[Amadea]] plants the seed of [[The Seven Hands]] by [[Artus]] while taking his seed to plant her at the center of [[Iridia]].

[[Amadea]] finalizes her [[Motif Awakening]] wish: _"I wish I had the power to transform all of this agonizing loss into anything that would, one day, make real the title of my greatest knight"_.

How will a broken, fatalistic girl, operating entirely out of a sense of sacrificial debt, manage to build an empire of light? The dissonance between her dark, void-aligned psychological state and her luminous, world-building goal propels the reader seamlessly to see what is next.

On reread, or after learning the truth of Chapter VIII, the burial of [[Artus]] and the planting of the [[Rose Seed]] instead of the violet one is a double funeral, she is burying both [[Corvin]] and [[Artus]].

#### Score III: The Violet in the [[Waltz of Wandering Love]]

[[Flux]] | [[Emotional Authenticity]]

_Act 13. The Violet Actress of Living Grief

Amadea commercializes her trauma as a [[Stage Performer]].

Change into first person of the novel, it appears retroactively that everything before was [[Amadea]] performing her own past story. Immediate dissonance for the reader with so many unanswered questions.

[[Amadea]], performing under the stage identity of Violet, travels [[Enclave]] to [[Enclave]] following the words of her [[Militant Enclave]] mentor as reconciliation to make peace with the death of [[Artus]] and follow the dream of the Violet Grove.

She faces the critics and travels through the [[Age of Embers]] detailing static criticality. She embarks alone through repurposed granaries, the devastated threshold wasteland of the [[Great Plague]], demonstrating what [[Static Criticality]] is firsthand, and how to detect detonations before they occur through the rise of hairs and the [[Celestial Astrolabe]].

[[Amadea]] admits her determination on jumping between enclaves is walking toward something by not stopping. If she stops, she won't find the grove.

_Act 14. Across the Sea to the Land Beyond_

Geographic modulation; entering the Great Expanse and retroactive explanation of the time between [[Artus]] death and now, following her departure with the last survivor of the [[Militant Enclave]]. 

Retroactively she begins narrating what happened between [[Artus]] death, why she is acting as a suggestion from [[Lenore]]. [[Amadea]] speaks how [[Lenore]] stood behind because she had to rebuild from the ashes. When [[Amadea]] said she wanted to stay with her, [[Lenore]] told her that [[Consonance]] lies in the future, not by staying with her. [[Lenore]] tells [[Amadea]] that the future of [[Artus]] lies in the main continent of [[Arcanoria]].

To help the departure, [[Lenore]] told [[Amadea]] to chase after the tide singer for the Violet Grove [[Legend]] and that she should perform grief before she dies of it which is the entire reason she adopted it as a coping mechanism until she is able to make sense of her own grief. [[Lenore]] told the sobbing [[Amadea]]: _"Look for the north star [[Amadea]], if the skies allow us to cross paths once again. We will. I wish to learn your story once you find the Violet Grove. I promise, I'll be there when your own [[Legend]] grows. Until then, keep moving, and never look backwards. You need to set your eyes on the horizon or else you will be trapped by the past."_

Personally, [[Amadea]] also acts as violet because being a [[Stage Performer]] was the coping mechanism of [[Elaine]]. She learned from her surrogate mother that it's easier to act feelings when reality is too [[Moody]] and too messy to survive. As [[Kay]] once said, survival comes before emotion as everything is physical before it is emotional.

As a departure gift, [[Lenore]] gives to her the detailed [[Celestial Astrolabe]] she used all the way back when she met [[Amadea]] and [[Artus]]. It's [[Lenore]]'s original [[Celestial Astrolabe]]. _"If you ever need it, look at the [[Grand Thread Rings]] of the sky, and you will know that I am also looking at the same stars as you are."_

Moreover, we retroactively learn the names of those in the [[Militant Enclave]], and how she is given the rumor of the violet grove plus an attendance by crossing the sea. [[Lenore]] also speaks about something that [[Amadea]] isn't quite sure to grasp, but it seems she was heavy with the voice of something she didn't quite confess to [[Amadea]]. While [[Amadea]] doesn't know this yet, it is about the fact that The [[Ascendant]] Seven Hands [[Atonalis]] was [[Corvin]].

First she travels without companion, no destination beyond the name of a place she half-believes exists. She carries the [[Sky Glass]] shard and the memory of [[Artus]] as her only luggage. She departs her natal continent, leaving her past behind, and through sea enters the continent that shares the [[Great Expanse]].

On arrival of the eastern side of the continent, She resumes performing. The play she stages is the story of herself and Artus — the orphan girl and the knight who kept sacrificing himself for her. She stages it as Ballad, as Legend, as something that happened to someone else. The distance of fiction is the only form in which she can bear to carry it.

In the [[Enclave]] circuits she passes through between performances, she begins hearing a name, a Tide Singer who was also a performer, whose [[Legend]] is attached to the deeper reaches of the [[Great Expanse]]. She begins following this lead to uncover the location of the Violet Grove given his relationship to [[Lunehymn]]. But she hears the rumor not loud enough to be certain, but consistent enough to be a direction. She files the name. She keeps moving.

The name of this act is the lullaby of [[Mira]]'s [[Soul Leitmotif]] which becomes important once [[Amadea]] learns the origin of [[Lacrimosa]] incarnation much after. This is the first major subtle reveal of the entire novel.

_Act 15. The Waltz of Wandering Love_

Cordelia's introduction; healing Amadea through musical resonance.

[[Cordelia]] attends one of these performances. She is not seeking [[Amadea]], she is seeking escape from her own personal tragedy that recently occurred from the betrayal that has cost her the family she loved and confined her back within the aristocratic estate's expectations. She escaped home sitting in the audience expecting theater.

She receives instead an accidental portrait of everything she has lost and a mirror to the love song of her [[Waltz of Wandering Love]], she is moved by a devotion that sacrifices without asking, a love that costs everything and offers it without ceremony, and it deeply ties to her desire of loving again. The play devastates her precisely because it is not abstract. it is specific, intimate, and performed by someone who clearly lived it. When the performance ends, Cordelia does not leave. She waits. She talks to her, but [[Amadea]] dismisses her as she dismisses most critics. But [[Cordelia]] is the first one to follow her. She talks about how that final line of Singers Remember when the Living Cannot moved her.

[[Amadea]] notices the aristocrat and rejects the offer of partnership immediately. The reason is not personal. it is categorical. [[Cordelia]] represents exactly the class of institution [[Amadea]] has spent her life stealing from, dodging, and surviving beneath. She does not want a companion. She especially does not want one who arrives wearing the costume of the people who built the rooms with cotton sponges in them, and who is likely not able to fend for themselves in the wilderness.

In a wilderness clearing short after, however, [[Amadea]] is ambushed by an hybrid [[Atonalis]] given her own grief over [[Artus]], the mixture of [[Discant]] [[Anxithor]] makes her take a serious injury before she can drive them off. She fights them but she is alone. Hurt, and she cannot heal herself. [[Cordelia]] who has been following her is close enough to reach her. She uses the "[[Waltz of Wandering Love]]" a somewhat [[Jolly]] tune mirroring Love Story from Indila with somewhat somber undertones. [[Amadea]] is surprised by this because she recognizes the melody, that's [[Artus]] [[Ballad]]. She first thinks she's hallucinating about [[Artus]], but realizes that no, it's the aristocrat that followed her that is playing the same song albeit differently but the melody is the same.

And also this is [[Cordelia]]'s [[Soul Leitmotif]]'s song that is simultaneously used to lift [[Amadea]]'s spirits while she is hurt and to be the instrument of her [[Healing Magic]]. The song functioning as both medical [[Strand]] and the emotional frequency that anchors the weave. [[Amadea]] recovers quickly. She is surprised. She is also, for the first time, materially in [[Cordelia]]'s debt, regardless of her preference. [[Cordelia]] says when asked why?: _"Well I just refuse to be part of the 'Living' that forget. I want to be part of your songs of remembering."

She accepts [[Cordelia]]'s company _for a while_. She proved her competence, demonstrated [[Amadea]]'s own weaknesses, and the qualification is deliberate. The resistance is still present but no longer operational. [[Cordelia]] watches [[Amadea]] during this period and sees two things at once: someone who needs healing that goes far deeper than the [[Atonalis]] wound, and the friend she has been waiting many [[Cycle]]s to give something to freely. 

When the first night falls on their camp [[Amadea]] is startled in the middle of the night on her early travels with [[Cordelia]] because [[Cordelia]] is actually a very loud snorer and the noise awakens her, while sleepless, this serves as one of [[Amadea]]'s reflection moments that she's now awake as she's not travelling alone anymore.

This reminds her of [[Corvin]] and the time with [[Lenore]], her ex-wife and mentor on the [[Militant Enclave]] as both were very loud in snoring, saying "Well, I guess that's why they were both together" as relief to herself. This is a moment where [[Amadea]] feels oddly homesick of her original home in the [[Enclave]] and still glad to have [[Cordelia]] aside even if she's somewhat cold with her. Her child version mourns [[Corvin]], [[Kay]], and [[Elaine]], her teenager era mourn [[Lenore]].

Later the day, when she wakes up and they're trailing up a waterfall she talks with [[Cordelia]] on: _"[[Cordelia]], for having such a pretty adorned face, you are one bundle of joy when sleeping, you know?. How are you planning on traveling with me, if all the wild beasts and [[Atonalis]] are able to hear that loud roaring snores of yours. I know you're aristocracy, but it's pretty obvious you've never been in the wild surviving alone. You've never had to double check your sleep before a monster wakes you up dead. What would you do if you attract something due to that snoring?"_

She teases that she's aristocracy and never has to double check her sleep before a monster wakes you up dead. While this embarrasses [[Cordelia]], [[Amadea]] says she's joking when she begins to apologize. She says her old [[Militant Enclave]] mentor actually taught her a sound proofing spell precisely for this situation as both [[Corvin]] and her had to be in the field and couldn't risk drawing anything nearby so she just so uses that spell in [[Cordelia]] whenever she gets woken up by her snoring.

She even opens up saying this was actually one of the earliest spells she mastered of something that wasn't void because she saw [[Artus]] practice it several times and remembers all the memories he played in his cello

Given that [[Amadea]] still goes by Violet by then, she realizes she had slipped up and corrects, saying that this is on the [[Ballad]], of course, trying to cover it up by completing afterward that it's part of her repertoire of practice given the [[Legend]] but it's the first slip that [[Cordelia]] does realize that this is too true and specific to be something invented.

As they continue walking, the journey begins taking toll on [[Cordelia]] who is not accustomed to walking this much. Thus the [[Waltz of Wandering Love]] becomes the sonic texture of their shared travel, she plays it every day, lifting silences without demanding they be filled. She simply makes the music available, and sometimes she tells stories about what the song means about a love trying to find love. And this is the moment that begins the first expedition, and as they continue, the scene of the [[Eleos Bloom]] carnivorous plant happens. The scene is important because is the first time [[Amadea]] is trying to use void magic on Cordelia. This also contrast because the first time she uses it is to save Cordelia. Here she goes by Violet and when the chapter ends she uses that same magic to injure her and sever the tether between both when she reveals her name Amadea:

**Setting:** A dense, mist-shrouded forest path. A soft, pitiful whimpering echoes through the fog, sounding exactly like a lost child weeping in the shadows.

Cordelia, relying heavily on the theoretical knowledge from the ledgers of her father, Lord Sylvain d'Acreon, steps off the trail to investigate, lacking the harsh survival instincts of the wild. Suddenly, the underbrush shifts. A massive, bulbous variation of a carnivorous Eleos Bloom—a glottis mouth trap—snaps its fleshy jaws shut around her.

Amadea emerges from the brush. She stops, staring deadpan at the flailing aristocratic boots of her companion sticking out of the sealed, bulbous plant, as she moves slowly towards her, stopping right where Cordelia is fighting the plant.

**Amadea:** "You know, when I said 'stay close and don't touch anything,' I didn't think I needed to specify 'do not actively feed yourself to the local flora.'"

**Cordelia:** _(Her voice heavily muffled and echoing from inside the plant's digestive chamber)_ "Violet! It's not funny! It sounded exactly like someone needing help! It was crying in the shadows!"

**Amadea:** "It’s a plant, Cordelia. Bushes don't weep unless they're trying to eat you. This is exactly why you aristocrats don't last a single Lunar Cycle out here."

Amadea sighs, stepping forward, and grabs Cordelia by the ankles. She plants her boot against the thick, pulsing stem of the bloom and gives a massive heave **(Pull #1)**. Cordelia stretches like a rubber band, but the plant's grip holds firm with a wet, suctioning noise.

**Cordelia:** Wait! Wait! It has a vacuum seal! According to my father's botanical archives, these rare Eleos Blooms clamp down on their prey to prevent—"

**Amadea:** "I don't care what your dad wrote! I have to break the seal!"

Amadea violently shoves Cordelia forward, driving her deeper into the plant's maw to break the suction **(Push #1)**.

The vibration of the resistance triggers the plant's secondary defenses. Thick, thorny tendrils erupt from the damp soil, whipping through the mist and wrapping tightly around Amadea’s ankles to drag her into the undergrowth as well.

Amadea's eyes narrow as she feels the restrain. In a flash of silver, she draws her Hollow-Point Saber.

**Amadea:** "Great it's now onto me too. Hold a beat, salad's getting defensive."

With three precise, blindingly fast strikes, Amadea severs the encroaching tendrils. The plant shudders, its jaws clamping down tighter around Cordelia's waist in response.

**Cordelia:** _(A muffled shriek)_ "Mmph! It smells like rotting peaches in here! 

Amadea yanks backward again with all her body weight **(Pull #2)**. The plant groans, its fleshy petals stretching, but stubbornly refuses to yield its meal.

**Amadea:** _(Grunting with effort)_ "By the Aria... what were they feeding you in that estate? You are impossibly stubborn!"

**Cordelia:** _(Indignant, though mostly incomprehensible)_ "My empathy is a theological virtue! And my weight has nothing to do with this! It’s the botanical tensile strength!"

Amadea shoves her forcefully back into the bloom again to break the newly formed suction **(Push #2)**.

**Amadea:** "Well, your virtue is about to be digested! Breathe out, aristocrat, make yourself skinny! We're doing this the hard way."

Amadea sheathes her saber and digs both heels into the roots. Taking a deep breath, she channels her Motif Awakening directly onto her companion for the very first time. This is the first time she applies magic directly on Cordelia. A dark, heavy hum vibrates the air as she weaves Void magic around Cordelia, manipulating the gravitational pull and spatial tension between the girl and the beast.

She gives one final, monumental, Void-enhanced heave **(Pull #3)**.

The tension builds. The plant bulges, straining against the localized spatial shift. Seeing it still won't give, Amadea lets go for a fraction of a second, shoves Cordelia's boots inward one last time **(Push #3)**, and immediately rips her backward with a violent surge of Void strength.

_SHHHH-THWUMP._

The vacuum seal breaks. Cordelia pops out of the carnivorous glottis mouth trap like a cork from a champagne bottle. She flies backward, crashing directly into Amadea. They both tumble into the dirt in a tangle of limbs, completely coated in a thick, glittering, smelly sap. The plant's jaws snap shut with a defeated clack, burping a small, pathetic cloud of pollen into the mist.

**Cordelia:** _(Coughing, frantically trying to wipe the thick sap from her face. Her old, submissive reflexes flare up)_ "I... well. Thank you, Violet. I am so sorry. I suppose I should have double-checked the Resonance before diving headfirst into the foliage. I knew the truth of these plants from my father's ledgers, but I have yet to survive in the wild... I didn't mean to be a burden."

**Amadea:** _(Lying flat on her back in the dirt, covered in slime, staring blankly up at the canopy. Her Fatalistic Embracer cynicism is in full swing)_ "Just write it down in that diary of yours. 'Rule number one of the wild: If the bush sounds like it needs a hug, let it cry.'"
