##### Act 23-24: Fulcrum of Amadea Disguised As "Filler"

Act 23 sets the groundwork introducing characterization, parties, and future alliances, while most of it seems like celebration after the rescue of [[Cordelia]], it is in its full extent during Act 24, **“Everything Beautiful and Wrong,”** that the hinge of the entire novel is set between Amadea’s temporary liberation from the aristocracy and her deeper collision with the novel’s final theological enemy. It stages, in miniature, the entire conflict of *Amadea*: joy versus grief, healing versus erasure, intimacy versus self-protective isolation, and mercy versus personhood. As the closing act of **Score IV: “Rose Seeds of High Society,”** it follows Amadea’s rescue of Cordelia and their first escape into a freer, more communal life. The act begins after the drunken celebration of Cordelia’s liberation, moves through the twin-waterfall sequence and the Carnival of Shifting Reflections, and culminates in the dinner with the Purest of Love and the Eight-Wings of Lacrimosa.

Its title names the act’s governing paradox: nearly every beautiful element is real—the waterfalls, friendship, spectacle, healing miracles, communal music, and the Purest of Love’s compassion—yet each carries an unresolved ethical danger. The reader is not meant to conclude that beauty is false; rather, the act asks whether beauty can remain beautiful once it begins suppressing agency, grief, and the right to remain a distinct person. This conflict is immediately grounded in the waterfall sequence. After Selah’s invitation to the Dreamweaver’s grand finale, Amadea, Cordelia, and Sephira travel by wagon toward the cliffs of the twin waterfalls. The location deliberately recalls Amadea’s earlier bathing scene with Cordelia, but the emotional grammar has changed: where the first encounter was defined by Cordelia’s caution, bodily shame, and concealed scars, the second begins with Amadea’s unusually open, teasing, almost adolescent joy. The waterfalls trigger an embodied memory in Amadea; their scent and setting draw her close to childhood, particularly the remembered image of Kay and Elaine’s intimacy, competence, and partnership. The landscape is therefore never merely scenic—it is a sensory archive of the family Amadea lost and the kind of life she unconsciously wants: safety, play, mutual touch, and a future not ruled entirely by survival.

This matters because Amadea is usually organized around the **Fatalistic Embracer** shield: she converts feeling into motion, mission, performance, or tactical control. At the waterfalls, she briefly stops doing that. She is not “Violet,” not the infiltrator, not the rescuer, not a grieving child fulfilling Artus’s legend; she is simply a young woman allowing herself to be playful with a friend. Her playfulness is character-revealing rather than incidental. Amadea urges Cordelia to undress and enter the water quickly, echoing the older caravan dynamic in which Kay and Elaine modeled physical ease and mutual affection. She then chases Cordelia and, in a deliberately embarrassing escalation, grabs her from behind by the breasts and tries to wrestle her into the water; Sephira interrupts the scene at its most awkward moment. Amadea’s humor is entangled with deprivation: she has long resented the aristocratic nutrition, physical safety, and bodily development Cordelia received, even while recognizing that Cordelia was brutalized within another kind of cage. Her teasing turns envy and vulnerability into an action she can control, allowing her to approach intimacy without having to confess how deeply she wants it. Cordelia’s participation is equally important. Earlier, she hid her scarred body in the water because she expected to be judged; here, she can run, laugh, hide behind rocks, and eventually flirt back. When Amadea freezes emotionally, Cordelia even offers—embarrassed—to let her resume the chase and touch her again, trying to restore the connection Amadea abruptly withdrew from.

The play turns serious when Amadea catches herself reflecting on how quickly everything has changed. In pursuing Cordelia around the falls, she recognizes an emotional resemblance to her earlier closeness with Artus, especially the memory-bound tenderness of the Waltz of Wandering Love. That recognition activates dread rather than comfort. Her internal logic is cruel but consistent with her trauma: she notices that she has taken a “detour” since meeting Cordelia. She has not been pursuing the Violet Grove with the same singularity, is no longer traveling purely as Violet through Enclaves, has not followed the Tide Singer’s trail with the discipline Artus’s legacy seems to demand, and—most painfully—is experiencing happiness without Artus. That is the first sharp emergence of the **Weight of Indulgence**. Amadea reads joy as betrayal: if she enjoys Cordelia, she may be forgetting Artus; if she permits herself a future, she may be abandoning the dead. She does not merely miss Artus—she feels morally indebted to remain wounded enough to prove that his sacrifice mattered. So she stops, withdraws from the chase, goes cold, and returns to the Fatalistic Embracer persona. Her detachment is not lack of love for Cordelia; it is evidence of how dangerous love has become inside her psychic system. Every new attachment activates the prophecy that she will lose someone else, fail to protect them, or betray Artus by surviving.

Sephira breaks the paralysis through touch, grabbing Amadea from behind in a comic retaliation for what Amadea had done to Cordelia. The tactile surprise pulls Amadea out of dissociation, and she responds by using Topological Arts to flee in another playful chase. But the restoration is incomplete. Amadea can perform lightness again, yet she cannot fully inhabit it; she soon leaves the water under the practical excuse of preparing the group’s climbing equipment. Her actual purpose is to retreat to camp and listen for Artus’s Sky Glass resonating through her Soul Leitmotif. This is a crucial distinction: she does not reject affection because it is unreal. She rejects it because it is real enough to threaten the identity she built around grief. Act 24 therefore establishes the central vulnerability the Facewalker will later exploit: Amadea’s need for relief, her shame about needing it, and her belief that intimacy is a betrayal of the dead.

The group climbs to the settlement hosting the Dreamweaver’s final parade, an early form of what later becomes the Civic of the Carnival of Shifting Reflections. The carnival’s transformative magic causes identities to exchange: Amadea becomes Cordelia, Cordelia becomes Sephira, and Sephira becomes Amadea. The device is joyful on the surface, but it prepares several later questions: Who is a person beneath a role, class, face, or legend? Can identity be shared without being erased? What is the difference between liberating transformation and coercive dissolution? When Amadea wears Cordelia’s face, is she honoring Cordelia or using her as cover? Those questions become central to the Hollowing. The carnival allows reversible, playful, consensual fluidity; Lacrimosa’s later salvation offers irreversible absorption into the All-Loving Moon. The act quietly establishes that the problem is not transformation itself, but transformation without consent, return, or personhood. The carnival also begins Amadea’s relationship to **Illusory Magic**. She infuses her Obsidian Feathers with Luminance from the converging leylines, watching them become reflective and beautiful. This is technically an early magical seed, but psychologically it is more complicated: Luminance was the binding Amadea once imagined for herself before Artus died. Her new beauty therefore revives the unrealized future she and Artus once dreamed of.

Unable to bear the nostalgia released by infusing her magic with Luminance, Amadea isolates herself on a balcony. There she encounters Luminaire, though both are disguised and initially fail to recognize one another. Luminaire comments dismissively on the face Amadea wears—Cordelia’s—calling Cordelia an exiled aristocrat who indebted herself for a “petty thief.” Amadea’s response is immediate and angry: “Well, that only means that the face I am wearing has to be much more loving than any commoner, princess, or aristocrat could ever understand, including you, and perhaps that’s the charm that makes it unique even if it doesn’t fit in the rot of high society.” This exchange does several things at once. Amadea, who often sees Cordelia through the lens of privilege and class resentment, instinctively defends her as a person; she refuses Luminaire’s framing of Cordelia as a symbol of aristocratic corruption. It also foreshadows Amadea’s eventual political philosophy as Great Justiciar: institutions, labels, and social rank do not settle moral worth. Cordelia’s aristocratic face is not her essence; the relevant fact is the love, risk, and agency beneath it. That distinction later becomes the heart of Amadea’s resistance to both the Court of Delicacies and the Purest of Love.

The dinner itself begins warmly. The group meets Pagiel, Adel, Kaia, and Erastus; Cordelia praises Pagiel’s performance, Pagiel reveals that Orphael—the Tide Singer whom Amadea seeks—was meant to appear but canceled, and the conversation initially finds common ground in mutual disgust toward the Court of Delicacies’ aestheticization of suffering. This is important because the Purest of Love is not introduced as obvious evil. At this point, they are a compassionate, charismatic found family who truly provide relief, stage meaningful beauty, and oppose the same abusive aristocratic culture Amadea has just escaped. Their miracles are real, their kindness is real, and their diagnosis of suffering is not fabricated. The ideological break happens when Cordelia invokes the Auric Order’s theological virtues and its understanding of suffering. Adel and Erastus react strongly, Pagiel attempts to de-escalate, and the supper becomes a debate about whether pain can be redeemed, whether the Auric Aria is silent, and whether healing should preserve pain’s capacity to be felt or extinguish it.

Erastus is the first to lose composure. His argument has force because it begins not as abstract doctrine but as witness: he cites dying children from the hospice, children whose suffering has made theological optimism sound obscene. His first testimony is Marie’s: “Do you know what I would do if I were king? I would make sure no more children died, only old people. At around 30. Marie, barely past her first Real Cycle Birthday. Under what heavens would a children barely 5 come to think that. 30 Lunar Cycles seem like an eternity away when it’s supposed to be the prime of their life.” Erastus’s grief is not merely that Marie is dying; it is that illness has distorted her concept of time. A child should imagine thirty as distant adulthood, not as the maximum age anyone deserves to reach. Her fantasy of a kingdom is already a triage system because she has internalized the world’s cruelty too early. His second quoted child is even more stripped-down: “Will my love for you be buried with me?” The line is devastating because it reveals the child’s fear that death does not merely end a body—it may erase relational meaning. The child does not ask whether they will die; they ask whether love itself survives their death. This is exactly the wound Lacrimosa will later claim to solve by dissolving separation, grief, and isolated consciousness into a single painless whole.

Erastus then recalls another boy, whose name itself died with him on his third Real Cycle Birthday. The erasure of the name is thematically deliberate: institutional suffering turns people into cases, but Erastus tries to resist that reduction by preserving fragments of their voices. Yet even his memorialization becomes part of the logic that can harden into the Purest of Love’s doctrine—if the world keeps producing unbearable child-deaths, perhaps the only mercy is to end the capacity to suffer at all. His third testimony comes from Anne: “My dad used to get annoyed when I asked him if he wanted to play. Now he gets mad when I say I don’t have time to go outside. Why did I need to be so sick for my dad to want to spend time with me?” Anne’s line exposes a secondary cruelty: illness can make care arrive too late and transform love into guilt-management. She recognizes that her father’s attention is real, but it is also shaped by fear, regret, and the imminence of loss. The child is forced to interpret adult emotional failure while dying, which makes Erastus’s rage morally intelligible. He concludes: “First your dreams die, then your hope, only then you. What’s fair about that?” That sentence is the Purest of Love’s emotional foundation. It is not a claim that suffering builds character, nor a theatrical argument that pain is beautiful; it is the insistence that suffering is intolerable because it kills the future before it kills the body.

Erastus finally directs the argument at Cordelia: “You can only talk from privilege. If you ever walk through the corridor of a sick ward full of children, you will realize there is no god.” Cordelia freezes because the accusation hits two vulnerabilities at once. First, she is an aristocrat, and despite her abuse, she knows she was fed, housed, and protected from forms of social precarity that Amadea and these children endured. Second, she is already oriented toward healing: Erastus frames that vocation as insufficient, even naïve, when faced with systemic, terminal suffering. Her silence is not agreement with the Purest of Love. It is the collapse of a person whose empathy makes her susceptible to the evidence in front of her, but whose history makes her unsure whether she has the moral authority to answer. Later, that same fracture becomes one of the paths through which Lacrimosa reaches her: Cordelia will fear that healing one person at a time is an inadequate response to a world structured to keep producing pain.

Amadea intervenes precisely when Cordelia cannot. That act is the first clear rehearsal of her future role as a **Great Justiciar**: she does not merely defeat an enemy or protect her friend physically; she recognizes when power, grief, and moral certainty are being used to silence a person in the room. Her defense is not a simplistic defense of God, aristocracy, or the idea that suffering is secretly good. She speaks from her own history: she survived the Great Plague, the eastern wastelands, childhood deprivation, loss, and a world in which no cosmic justice arrived to protect her. Her position is therefore not, “Pain has meaning, so endure it.” It is, “Pain is real, but the answer cannot be the destruction of the person who feels it.” That intervention also establishes the future ethical difference between Amadea and Lacrimosa. Amadea’s justice begins with witness and protection: she wants fewer children to suffer, but not by deciding that the children’s inner lives are expendable. Lacrimosa’s mercy will expand beyond bodily healing into the removal of the self’s capacity to experience pain. In other words, Act 24 plants the eventual conflict articulated during the Hollowing: Lacrimosa claims, “You built Iridia so that fewer children weep in the shadows. I am offering a world where no one weeps at all.” Amadea’s answer is that weeping is not the enemy; it is proof that consciousness, personhood, and someone capable of love remain present. Ending the person in order to end their suffering is not mercy. At the dinner, Amadea does not yet possess the full language of this answer, but she performs its first moral action: she refuses to let Cordelia be reduced to a privileged symbol, refuses to deny the reality of the dying children, and refuses the implication that unbearable pain authorizes the annihilation of agency.

Amadea notices another disturbance beneath the dinner’s surface. The Eight-Wings speak of events from the Great Plague as lived experience, yet they should be impossibly old—at least around 160 Lunar Cycles by the internal implications of Cordelia’s knowledge of the wider world and the historical timeline. This is the first concrete sign that Lux Aeterna’s gifts have a cost or an unnatural mechanism. The group’s serenity, longevity, and real healing are not merely miraculous; they may be connected to a form of preservation that has already altered the natural boundaries of life, time, and selfhood. The unease is carefully paced. Amadea recognizes the discrepancy but cannot yet resolve it, because the members of the Purest of Love do not behave like caricatured monsters. The act wants the reader to feel the seduction of their offer before revealing its terminal logic. After the emotional escalation, Selah provides the gentle, persuasive version of the Purest of Love’s theology. She acknowledges Amadea’s suffering rather than dismissing it, offers belonging, describes the Dreamweaver as one who mourns alongside the wounded, and presents miracles as genuine salvation. Her key argument is not overtly violent: “Why is individual consciousness sacred if it’s only a cage for pain?” And she offers distinct invitations to the two girls: “To Cordelia: You heal bodies one at a time. We heal the souls ability to suffer.” And “To Amadea: You fight shadows. We dissolve them.” These lines are nearly perfect future-pressure points. Cordelia’s later crisis will center on exhaustion, self-sacrificial healing, and the conviction that she must save everyone; Amadea’s will center on endless shadow-watching, survivor’s guilt, and the belief that she must carry the dead’s weight alone. Selah identifies the correct wounds, but the Purest of Love proposes an ethically catastrophic cure.

Act 24 foreshadows the Hollowing not through a single villain reveal, but through an entire emotional and philosophical system. Lux Aeterna relieves pain while making a person’s frequency feel quieter, which later expands into the dissolution of consciousness through the All-Loving Moon. Erastus’s hospice testimonies establish the unbearable reality of suffering, making Lacrimosa’s apocalyptic promise emotionally credible because the suffering she answers was never fake. Cordelia freezes under the charge of privilege and inadequate healing, and Lacrimosa later weaponizes Cordelia’s exhaustion, utility-based self-worth, and desire to end suffering. Amadea defends Cordelia before she has a complete doctrine, and she later becomes the Justiciar and resistance leader who gives political form to that instinct: protection without erasure. The carnival identity-swapping is joyous and consensual, but the Hollowing becomes the dark inversion where identity ceases to be playful, reversible, and chosen. Amadea’s retreat into grief after the waterfall joy shows her unresolved self-punishment, which makes her vulnerable to Lacrimosa’s offer of rest and to the Facewalker’s counterfeit intimacy. The Purest of Love offers community, attention, relief, and beauty, and their later radicalization hurts because the reader has seen why people would choose them.

The Great Justiciar is already visible in Amadea’s behavior here, long before she holds the formal authority. Her justice is not procedural obedience—she has spent much of her life evading corrupt law—but an active defense of personhood against systems that claim moral legitimacy while denying people the right to define themselves. At Cordelia’s estate, she says, “The rules of this house are not the rules of the world,” and insists that Cordelia can choose rather than continue bleeding for an abusive order. At the Purest of Love dinner, she makes the parallel move on a theological scale: the suffering of children is real, but it does not authorize anyone to decide that the answer is to abolish the sufferer’s consciousness. Her resistance leadership later grows out of precisely this capacity to hold two truths at once: the world is cruel enough that mercy is necessary, but mercy ceases to be mercy when it destroys the person it claims to save. That is why Amadea, rather than simply arguing against Lacrimosa’s facts, becomes the novel’s answer to her. She does not deny wounds, grief, illness, or despair. She insists that care must remain consensual, relational, and oriented toward preserving a future in which the wounded person can still choose, remember, love, and grieve.

By the end of Act 24, Amadea has received both a practical and symbolic route forward: the Dreamweaver’s invitation supplies information about the Tide Singer and points the group toward the Boreal Reach, the Crescent Mist Peaks, and the Lunar Abyss. Sephira leaves on her own route to clear her name, while Amadea and Cordelia move again toward the legend that has been calling Amadea since childhood. But emotionally, they do not leave cleanly. Amadea carries the reactivated grief from the waterfalls, Cordelia carries Erastus’s indictment and the temptation of painless mercy, and both have met the community that will someday become the engine of the Hollowing. The act ends not by resolving its conflict but by planting it inside the protagonists: the wrongness is no longer outside them, because it has taken the shape of something beautiful enough to want.

_"Suddenly, everything stays in my head too long, the [[Weight of Indulgence]] is crushing. _Act 28: A Horrible, Unforgiving Nightmare_

The POV goes back to [[Amadea]] and [[Luminaire]] that begins with a dream that is [[Amadea]]'s second nightmare.

The nightmare [[Amadea]] is having is about forgetting the face of [[Artus]], she imagines them both on the night before [[The Seven Hands]] attack on the same cliff overlooking the ocean where he once told her about the stars.

They begin in a conversation with [[Artus]] talking to [[Amadea]]

_"Do you truly miss me, [[Amadea]]?"_

_"As you have no idea, my dear [[Artus]]."_

_"Did you love me that much?"_

_"With all my heart, forever."_

_"Then why didn't you say it everyday for me."_

_"I was an idiot, I'm sorry."_

[[Amadea]] pauses, contemplating in silence until she breaks it continuing: _"But I would give everything for having you with me again."_

_"Yes? Would it be any different?"_

_"Definitely, I would hug you each and every single day. I wouldn't let a single [[Moon]] pass without giving you a kiss."_

[[Amadea]]'s tears begin to run down before she continues.

_"Every day I would make you know how much I loved you, so that you never ever would doubt me again."_

She says in a soft voice: _"If only you were here..."_

She curdles into her legs, and after a long silence of repose. She gathers her [[Composure]] and asks again: _"If I was the [[Moon]], [[Artus]], would you still look for the stars?"_

However, instead of replying what the real [[Artus]] said to her that time, this dream [[Artus]] replies saying _"You'll slowly forget my face, [[Amadea]]."_, she replies _"Never, in all the stars!"_ but the next time she looks at him, his face begins blending elements.

[[Artus]] has either double interlocked eyes, different features of how his scar looked like, [[Amadea]] is terrified, [[Artus]] while morphing replies _"You will."_

[[Amadea]] trying to reach out to him, but [[Artus]] keeps being behind faces, animals, masks, moving away or looking from her at the back until [[Amadea]] has one final clarity glimpse of how he looked like when she remembers that last gaze he gave her before sacrificing himself.

The horror is not absence — it is _contamination_. The face is not gone; it is blending into everything else, which means [[Amadea]] is the one doing it, which means the betrayal lives inside her memory itself. The guilt has now become epistemological: she cannot even trust what she remembers about him, and she processes this as a failure to the debt her owes him. 

She internalizes one last thought before the dream pulls her away into reality.

_"I am becoming less of who he knew, while becoming more of what he never got to be. How can I even claim to honor his dream like this... Would you even recognize me, [[Artus]]?"_

[[Amadea]] wakes up crying, clutching the [[Sky Glass]], and then realizes [[Luminaire]] was present in the scene.

[[Luminaire]] reacts to [[Amadea]] having a nightmare about [[Artus]] while they're still chained together, to make her realize how does it look from the outside and offer some solace which becomes the entire reason of why [[Amadea]] begins to soften with her, quick exchange about [[Daedalus]] and [[Aurelian]] but she doesn't open up, just briefly mentions them to ground [[Amadea]].

[[Luminaire]] replies talking her out of what the real [[Legend]] was, _"Not exactly, but still something like that. I was not related but I honestly wish I was. He was my mentor, and whom I'd actually call father. He tried to keep his fangs in check most of the time, that's why [[Daedalus]] was the only compassionate thing in the empire. As [[Amadea]] echoes back the words to [[Cyril]]: _"Hope isn't the point, action is."_ [[Luminaire]] says that the line of thought is remarkably similar to what [[Daedalus]] said about his own life as his [[Jealous]]y: _"If you can't believe in yourself, build something first and then believe in the undeniable existence of it. As she recovers, [[Amadea]] looks at her [[Celestial Astrolabe]] and realizes that they have entered the [[Phase]] marking her 6th [[Real Cycle Birthday]], making her 24 [[Lunar Cycle]]s old. This becomes the founding principle of [[Luminance]] and the realization of [[Luminaire]] on how everything is a fractal understanding the nature of [[Sky Glass]] thanks to her [[Forged Rivalry]] with [[Amadea]]. The entire situation mirrors the song of "I kissed a girl. _"This was never the way I planned. Not my intention. I got so brave, drink in hand. Lost my discretion. It's not what I'm used to. Just wanna try you on. I'm curious for you. Caught my attention. I kissed a girl and I liked it. The taste of her cherry chap stick. I kissed a girl just to try it. I hope my boyfriend don't mind it. It felt so wrong. It felt so right. Don't mean I'm in love tonight. I kissed a girl and I liked it. I liked it. No, I don't even know your name. It doesn't matter. You're my experimental game. Just human nature" - Sang by [[Amadea]] from her perspective._ This is the only time [[Amadea]] struggles to fight with her [[Weight of Nature]] given the romantic exchange between former rivals which fuels even more the tragedy when [[Luminaire]] decides to submit to [[The Hollowing]]. One of the canonical [[Deep Mirrored Bond]]s of founding [[Legend]]s. 

For [[Luminaire]] being seen as herself and not an instrument triggers the [[Weight of Indulgence]] that eventually makes her impossible reconciliation with being a crystalline instrument again the root of why she chooses to hollow. She learned through [[Amadea]] that she can feel emotion and that she is a person who can choose for herself. This is what begins changing the [[Dissonance]] into [[Consonance]], and upon entering a zone of [[Coherence]] they finally climb their way out of the [[Lunar Abyss]] and up to the Boreal Reach again.

_Act 30: Strand Pools and Time Bubbles of the Silver Cascades_

Navigating temporal anomalies; solidifying the newly forged group dynamic.

They eventually find each other on the top when [[Cordelia]] and [[Orphael]] arrive first, then begin looking for [[Amadea]] and [[Luminaire]]. They find [[Sephira]] in the middle ground where she's saying she's exhausted and scared to death but saying she fought and won her ornament by following [[Orphael]], and she keeps clinging to him.

Then they finally find [[Luminaire]] and [[Amadea]] who were about to fall to the chasm again, [[Cordelia]] and [[Orphael]] pull them upward with a [[Strand Pool]] of their found magic and when they appear with the cuffs they are all startled.

There is an awkward reunion between all of the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] as [[Sephira]] and [[Orphael]] have become entangled in a relationship that is as messy as [[Luminaire]] and [[Amadea]] with each other, while [[Cordelia]] is asking about the entire situation teasing both of them. The struggle between light and shadow continues even as both are pushed to embarrassment but stubbornly decide to show and refuse to yield to one another. It begins with [[Luminaire]] saying _"What? Is there something in our faces?"_ And immediately [[Amadea]] catching the direction of the sentence saying _"Oh, This? Ah, it's a symbol of our newfound friendship! You wouldn't believe how the [[Crystal]] of her chest shifts when you touch—"_ as [[Luminaire]] interrupts [[Amadea]]'s opportunity to embarrass her and instead replies back. The dynamic of [[Cindergale]] appears again as they're each other fighting for dominance.

This revelation serves a dual purpose. In the immediate narrative, it acts as a euphoric release of the enemies-to-lovers tension. However, structurally, it plants a devastating time-bomb. By proving that Luminaire is capable of deep feeling and desire, the narrative ensures that her inevitable, tragic defection to Lacrimosa's "Hollowing" in later movements will be agonizing for both Amadea and the reader.

Finally this is broken by a [[Lux Aeterna]] tethered among the climbers: One of the mages or pilgrims in the Peaks is quietly under Manifestation I of [[Lux Aeterna]] when they reach the SIlver Cascades, moving with eerie tirelessness, feeling no fatigue, scars faded. [[Luminaire]], as [[Hollowmarch]] heir, might recognize the sigils of the cult of the [[Moon]] and be slightly disturbed.

They begin the proper ascent and the challenge of figuring out a path upward until they reach the Silver Cascades. The party journeys through the entire myriad figuring out how to deactivate the dangers and exchanging all of their information, with [[Cordelia]] properly establishing [[Detective Magic]] and [[Illusory Magic]] alongside Luminaire and Amadea who are now cooperating.

#### Score VI: Crux Upward to the Whispering Gallery

[[Crystal]] | [[Absolute Certainty]]

_Act 31: The Crescent Veils of Aetherlight and Lunehymn_

The POV begins with [[Orphael]] and shifts to [[Amadea]].

Continuation of the expedition, all the virtuoso meet up, they find a sanctuary called the Grief Cathedral which is on one of the sections where the mist flickers more heavily with the rays of [[Aetherlight]]. 

The first to arrive and introduce everyone is [[Orphael]], he goes in alone to get them a place in the [[Enclave]] given his reputation on the patron of arts. In the negotiation they then realize that [[Orphael]] has tracked a [[Pleasure Parasite]] that attached itself to the burden he has been having of physical exhaustion around his shoulders.  Critically, he is the only one when this happens. [[Cordelia]] learns about the fact that [[Orphael]] had one of these and what they can do after she's looking for [[Amadea]] asking if anyone had seen her after she was playing her [[Soul Leitmotif]] by the moonlight.

The removal took less than a few [[Pulse]]s. Whatever lived at the base of [[Orphael]]'s shoulder came away cleanly, it was a small, translucent thing, more membrane than creature only visible through the rays of [[Aetherlight]], as it folded in on itself when the light touched it and left nothing behind but a faint ring of bruised skin

That's all it ever is, the cathedral keeper said, not unkindly. Small. Easy to remove. Which is the first deception, that something so easy to remove should be so difficult to notice.

He set it aside without looking at it again as it dissolves back into static.

_From all the [[Atonalis]], the [[Erosyx]] are not predators in the conventional sense. They do not hunt like a [[Carnalix]] would do. They wait at the edge of a wound and offer what the wound needs most, not false pain, not fear, those are crude instruments. It offers false relief. Specific relief. The exact shape of what you've been missing. And the host accepts it because it is indistinguishable from healing. Because healing also feels like this: the shoulder unknotting, the breath going longer, the sense that the thing you have been carrying has finally been allowed to settle. You cannot consent to your body reacting to grief. The tears come whether you permit them or not. The parasites know this. They hunt in the shape of the wound you offer, and they always attack through the one thing no one can deny — the body. The mind can build armor. The body cannot deny itself._

He looked at [[Orphael]].

_You would have noticed eventually. They always give themselves away. When the relief stops feeling like rest and starts feeling like necessity, and that is not healing. That is dependency. That is the parasite making itself the source._

He paused.

_They consume what any type of weaponized intimacy makes available. Sweat. Tears. Anything that can be extracted alongside emotion, really. These parasites gorge on the particular chemical signature of grief held too long in the same part of the body. Love that consumes is not love. It is hunger wearing the face of love, and the face is convincing because the hunger is real. It found a real wound. It offered a real sensation. It only borrowed the shape of something that could have genuinely healed you — and used that shape to ensure you would never seek anything else ever again._

He collected the [[Rose Seed]] left behind by the extinguished creature without ceremony and planted it on the garden beneath the altar.

_You came in time. That is what matters. The damage is reversible. It always is, at this stage._

Once they negotiate and accept [[Orphael]] they agree to provide refuge for them for a while. And in the [[Aetherlight]] they help in ceremony to finally break the [[Emberwhisper]] chains.

With the found peace, [[Luminaire]] purifies [[Sky Glass]] for the first time ever creating the repeatable [[Resonance Box]] out of the shard of [[Artus]] that makes [[Amadea]] indebted to her, and the change of tension between them. It's primarily important because the [[Sky Glass]] unrefined was the color of [[Crystal]] pink. However, a [[Resonance Box]] that became reusable and functional with purified [[Sky Glass]] shifted back to [[Resonance]], the same original color shift that happened to [[Artus]] all those [[Moon]]s ago. It's symbological, and it's surprising for [[Luminaire]] as well as that means it's capable of catching sounds and storing them indefinitely.

This is revolutionary for [[Amadea]] in so many layers, and they properly share a night together that begins with a fight again but ends in the [[Soul Leitmotif]] of [[Luminaire]] to [[Resonance]], and [[Amadea]] teases her about it saying that if she's going to stay afterward with her or what. [[Luminaire]] opens up about her past despite the teasing and narrates her story of her [[Luminance]] [[Motif Awakening]], and how she is tied to her duty regardless of what [[Amadea]]'s delusional ideas point toward and how she's indebted to figuring out how to use [[Sky Glass]] after her epiphany properly in [[Hollowmarch]] when she goes back after reaching the Whispering Gallery.

The act the tension has been building toward since Act 29 — they share a night that begins, characteristically, with a fight. It ends with [[Luminaire]]'s [[Soul Leitmotif]] shifting to [[Resonance]] as well — the same color shift that [[Artus]]' gem made in Act 11, and its current color shifted moments before. The pattern now repeated with [[Luminaire]]'s [[Soul Leitmotif]]. [[Amadea]] notices immediately and teases her: _"Are you going to stay afterward with me or what?"

[[Luminaire]], rather than deflecting, opens up: she narrates her original [[Luminance]] [[Motif Awakening]], the discovery of fractals in the [[Sky Glass]], and her duty to return to [[Hollowmarch]] to properly develop what she learned. She is going back. She tells [[Amadea]] plainly that no matter what [[Amadea]]'s "delusional ideas" might point toward, she is indebted to her purpose, and she is leaving after the Whispering Gallery.

While delivered through teasing, this honesty hurts deeply [[Amadea]], specifically because [[Artus]] never had a "returning to duty" after their waltz. He had no competing loyalty. The fundamental difference between these two relationships registers as a new kind of loss, drawing [[Amadea]] closer to the [[Erosyx]]'s resonant wound frequency: the grief of being loved only as function, the denied identity of a person who keeps being left.

This is [[Amadea]]'s third nightmare

it opens up with an [[Artus]] that won't look at her and her pleading for attention. [[Amadea]] speaks to the ghost of his silhouette at the distance:

_"[[Artus]], I miss you more than I can bear without breaking. I haven’t been able to let you go, but the truth is worse than that—I don’t want to let you go. I’d rather stay with this pain than accept a world where you’re no longer here. It hurts to admit it, but I still picture you here with me, in a life that never happened, in a “us” that lives on in the ballads. Since you left, there’s been no peace—and if there is, it’s fake. 

As he continues looking away, [[Amadea]] becomes more desperate:

_"You’ll always have a place in my heart, even if I’ve ceased to exist in yours."_

After this, a chorus of dancing silhouettes like the ones in of the [[Great Plague]] in the dawn that drowned in fire replies back to her:

_"Liar! Liar! Liar! You've gone past the unthinkable with [[Luminaire]]!"_

_"Where is the grove? You've left us all to die."_

[[Amadea]] fights back, saying: _"That's not true, what other options did I have!? Step back demons, let me see my knight!"_

However, even as she silences these voices, [[Artus]] is still at the distance turning his back.

_"Why won't you look at me? Have I really made such a big mistake that you don't want to see me again? Is this for finding someone new!? I'm so ashamed to be sharing the only intimacy I've kept to you."_ 

The dissonance this causes makes her dream of the moment she shared with [[Artus]] as another vivid nightmare and the most distressful of that night about continuing to forget [[Artus]]' face.

[[Amadea]] tries to root herself by saying:

_"It's just a dream! It's just a dream, right!?"_

However, the chorus of ghosts and silhouettes on the distance repeat back to her:

_"What difference does that make?"_

_"You still can't leave, you have only chosen to forget."_

As the figure of [[Artus]] blends in the distance with the ghosts of the biological fathers she can't recall, [[Amadea]] cries out:

_"Please, answer me, [[Artus]]. I'm begging you. I'll forget [[Luminaire]], [[Cordelia]], anyone. I swear, please, give me another chance, I won't betray you ever again! You are my only anchor and the one I cherish most in the whole world."_

_"Please, just one more time. Hold my hands! Touch my face! Hug me! And talk to me heart to heart like you used to! I can't do this alone..."_

It features a version of [[Artus]] that has become her own internal accusation against herself, and the begging is [[Amadea]] _begging herself to forgive herself for surviving_. It compounds the shame that [[Amadea]] has for finding new relationships, going to even the extend of sharing a night together with [[Luminaire]], and how she processes it as a betrayal of their own intimacy and that she's cheating on him and his memory.

_Act 32: The [[Scorching Truth]] of the [[Atonalis]]_

The Grief Cathedral is one of the [[Enclave]]s they find in the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] and it explains what an [[Atonalis]] is, how they have the seeds in everyone of us, revealing the first pairs of [[Scorching Truth]]s of [[We Are Atonalis]] which is one of the reveals that begin fracturing [[Amadea]] as she realizes she's been slaying people that are as broken as her during her entire life.

She now understands that every monster she has killed her entire life has been a version of someone who never got a [[Cordelia]], a [[Lenore]], a seed to plant from [[Artus]] dream. This shatters her entire relationship to combat. She believes that she has not been a hunter. She has been a mirror that has been slaying the version of herself that never healed, over and over, since Act 8.

By learning that the monsters _are_ the innocent who succumbed to their wounds, the boundary between protector and predator dissolves. She is lashing out at the hypocrisy of their mission, realizing that the "righteous crusade" of her life is a lie, and by extend attacks anyone doing anyone doing anything similar like the Grief Cathedral.

[[Amadea]] lashes out saying _"None of these are the good guys, you aren't the good guys, we aren't the good guys. For the aria's sake there isn't a single good guy in the world! Why does anything of this matter!? No one has been purging the world of evil, we've been all just putting down people who couldn't cope, and I can't stand a single word more of these hypocrites before I put someone else down._

As she continues lashing out her [[Soul Leitmotif]] on her hand begins to flicker with the Spiraling state, the Grief Cathedral priests notices and she realizes that [[Amadea]] is nearing the state of an [[Atonalis]] for which he takes cover. [[Amadea]] notices that he is afraid of her and laughs:

_Seriously? Is this a sick joke? Now you are scared of me!? 

Then shifting to a theatrical tone and mocking them for portraying her as an [[Atonalis]], and readying her own [[Obsidian Feather]] blade.

_Are you perhaps fearful that I may tear the entire structure down like one of the monsters outside? Hahaha, of course! Honestly, what difference does it even make if I was right now the one putting down the entire cathedral and not a monster, at least the [[Dissonance]] is honest. And I don't even need to be an [[Atonalis]] to take down the entire place."_

As [[Amadea]] threatens the Grief Cathedral priest, similarly to a [[Violux]], [[Cordelia]] steps between them and fights with her as an echo of what happened earlier in the family estate, she tells [[Amadea]] when she's acting out on her worst as she keeps getting into a physical fight breaking things: _"I know you're not some kind of 'Villain' but you're not a good person either. Honestly I wish I could say, you do all these things because there's no good inside you but the fact is, there is a lot of good. You've just always chosen the opposite whenever anything gets to be too good to be comfortable."_

After her fight, the [[Scorching Truth]] also makes her a target for all [[Atonalis]] nearby which is how [[Amadea]] gets lured by a shapeshifter mirroring [[Artus]] and her profound shame for the situation with [[Luminaire]]. Her refusal to look past the past is what makes [[Amadea]] fail victim alone to the [[Facewalker]] that has been tracking her since the nightmare of [[Artus]] on the [[Lunar Abyss]]. It finds her after luring her given that she is resonating at the exact wound frequency an [[Erosyx]] feeds on due to her denied identity, and the grief of being loved only as function. 

[[Amadea]] finally declares settling down her rage. Her immediate violence quelled but her desire for isolation solidified, Amadea lowers her weapon but refuses to yield emotionally:

_"Fine, if you want me gone, I'll leave on my own terms this god forsaken place. They'll rot from the inside, just like everything, anyways."_ 

[[Cordelia]] is at first startled but then her protectiveness does kick in, and follows after [[Amadea]], and when [[Cordelia]] does push back when she finds [[Amadea]] outside she has calmed down and her [[Soul Leitmotif]] has turned back to Fractured, the spiral isn't visible anymore. She points this out and tells [[Cordelia]]: _"Look, you don't have to worry for me. I'm strong alone by myself, I've survived many [[Lunar Cycle]]s, and now even, the literal [[Lunar Abyss]]. A pity fight in nowhere land won't turn me into a monster but I won't hear a single word of these hypocrites either. I just need some time to gather my thoughts before we keep moving. So let me be and go back to comfort your new friends that are now scared of us."_

This is the culmination of her nightmare with [[Artus]] coming to fruition. By telling [[Cordelia]] to "go back to comfort your new friends," [[Amadea]] is deliberately fulfilling the promise she made to [[Artus]] in her dream: _"I'll forget [[Luminaire]], [[Cordelia]], anyone"_. She uses her [[Fatalistic Embracer]] resilience and strength as a barricade to push [[Cordelia]] away, framing her isolation as a pragmatic need to "gather her thoughts" while actively ensuring she cuts off her only sources of emotional support.

This is how the "[[We Are Atonalis]]" [[Scorching Truth]] does not just cause guilt — it _amplifies_ her existing wound frequency catastrophically. She is now resonating at peak [[Erosyx]]-readable grief: denied identity, guilt about [[Artus]], the mathematics of outliving him, the ambiguous loss of [[Luminaire]], the fight over her accountability with [[Cordelia]] and now the horror that every monster she dispatched was as broken as her. She becomes a homing signal in the [[Lunehymn]] fog exhausted and wanting for an out.

The [[Facewalker]] draws her in by behaving as a shapeshifter that is preying on the lack of shape of [[Artus]] that [[Amadea]] can't remember, it is wearing his face in changing patterns hoping that it can soon wear [[Amadea]]'s face as well.

First she uses her habit of tending to her [[Primary Instrument]] as means to have some stability and peace of mind before she transitions to playing her [[Soul Leitmotif]] in the moonlit clearing by herself as the [[Moon]] draws high above with the same song she used to play with [[Artus]] before he died.

She is sobbing alone on the clearing and tending to her violin trying to ground herself when the shapeshifter appears:

_"[[Artus]]? She says at the distance."_

_"No. No, you're not—you can't be—"_

_"I'm not real," the Facewalker said, in Artus's voice. "But I can be, for a little while. If you let me."_

When the [[Facewalker]] lures [[Amadea]] in the crescent mist, it humanizes itself by wearing the face of [[Artus]], even counting back the words that [[Elaine]] said to her in Act 2 to prove its relationship to [[Amadea]]: _"If you ever lose your way in the dark, trust the person who knows exactly how to tie this knot."_

As it physically embraces her, its hands reach around to the back of her head and perfectly tie [[Elaine]]'s complicated knot. This proves to [[Amadea]] that the creature understands the deepest parts of her architecture.

And for the larger lore, it signals that the [[Facewalker]] is not just mimicking [[Artus]]. It has violently extracted every abandoned intimacy [[Amadea]] has ever felt — from [[Artus]]' face to her surrogate mother's touch — weaponizing her entire history of loss into a single entity.

_Act 33: Where are you!? Please! Answer!!_

This a [[Cordelia]] POV chapter.

Amadea vanished after her fight with Cordelia and Luminaire. Days pass. She does not return. Cordelia finds [[Amadea]]'s left [[Celestial Astrolabe]] which is the first warning sign as she usually carries that everywhere, so she begins tracking her through every method she has — Detective Magic, Strand Pool reading, questioning pilgrims and Enclave travelers — and the trail goes cold in a disturbing way.

A pilgrim in the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] talks gives Cordelia the motto she didn't know she needed: **"NEVER FACE AN [[EROSYX]] ALONE."** after hearing the story of the [[Facewalker]] [[Legend]] that allegedly came from someone who broke in the Grief Cathedral. The specific emphasis, the specific creature-class — her blood goes cold. She reconstructs what she knows: [[Amadea]]'s wound frequency after the [[Artus]] nightmare, the [[Scorching Truth]] acting like a beacon, the fight with [[Cordelia]] and [[Luminaire]] that isolated her. She realizes in horror exactly what happened and what she is now racing against. She comes to realize how the last moment [[Amadea]] went away is by a [[Memory Field]] left behind on the snow.

_"Amadea has not slept properly since the Boreal Reach. Amadea keeps saying she is fine. Amadea has been performing grief for two [[Lunar Cycle]]s and calling it moving forward. The last time anyone saw her was when she heard her playing her [[Soul Leitmotif]] alone at the first [[Bar]] of night."_

[[Cordelia]] realizes half sentence what has happened.

[[Cordelia]] leaves without explanation to anyone — not [[Sephira]], not [[Orphael]], not [[Luminaire]]. She goes alone into the Crescent Veils of [[Lunehymn]] mist after [[Amadea]]. What follows is the entire chapter tracking her desperation as she runs an advanced [[Detective Magic]] operation in territory she is not trained for, without combat formatting, exhausted, up against the structural limits of her own body.

The entire chapter tracks her desperation figuring out how to do advanced [[Detective Magic]] and making her own [[Cindergale]] [[Ornament]] [[Motif Awakening]] as she is forced to the brink of exhaustion but she refuses that gets in her way.

Even with the warning of _"Breathing the mist too long induces [[Lunehymn]] Narcosis that induce the vivid hallucinations of past pilgrims' deaths, forcing you to relive their final moments regardless of where these occurred."_ She is focused to do for [[Amadea]] what she once did for her in the estate, no matter her condition as an aristocrat, the clawing of deaths at her empathy, and her lack of format combat training plus the narcosis she's inhaling due to the concentrated [[Lunehymn]] mist that is the most unforgiving natural hazard.

However, as the track progresses, the fact that she has to run through the [[Lunehymn]] Mist induces for her narcosis several times where she's forced to witness the deaths of everyone who came before, yet forces herself through until she collapses where _"Everything is a Nightmare."_

At this point she almost gives up as she's choking by the amount of mist she's been inhaling, the cold of the air, and the exhaustion. She is crying and coughing on the floor questioning why she doesn't have the physique of [[Amadea]], saying that if only she could push through like [[Sephira]] or had the intellect of [[Luminaire]] none of this would stand in her way, rekindling her [[Submissive]] nature. However, it is on this hallucination that she sees the figure of [[Amadea]] extending her hand, as the exact same pose when she rescued her from her [[Submissive]] family degradation, and she decides to continue as a hot burning feeling curls in her chest. 

This is where her [[Cindergale]] [[Ornament]] [[Motif Awakening]] occurs. The key: [[Cordelia]] has always been [[Strand]] and [[Flux]] — raw emotional openness, the courage to feel everything without armor, and to thread history. [[Cindergale]] is its opposite: the refusal to let what you feel impair what you must do, perfect focus held despite perfect emotional storm. The [[Motif Awakening]] happens not when she suppresses her feelings but when she refuses to let her terror, her condition, and the hallucinations for [[Amadea]] be an obstacle. She is terrified and focused simultaneously. That is the [[Cindergale]] principle embodied: emotion and precision operating in parallel, not in competition.

She struggles in defiance against all odds with the new ticking sound of [[Amadea]]'s [[Celestial Astrolabe]], trying to focus all her magic no matter what she feels, what she sees, or what she hallucinates.

_Act 34: And You Have Bleed Enough, Remember?_

The POV goes back to [[Amadea]] where we are in a disturbing state of her interiority and the interaction with the [[Artus]] shapeshifter where the [[Atonalis]] was wanting to be [[Amadea]] so badly saying "I want hair like yours, hands like yours, fingers that curl in the cold like yours, I want eyes to stare, and to cry when I'm feeling lost like you." The [[Facewalker]] is touching each part of [[Amadea]] as it describes the eerie passage.

[[Amadea]] is recalling all the times she's been left behind, starting with the fact that she can't even remember her biological mother's name nor past aside from fragmented memories. She also can't remember who her father actually is, and is in profound conflict regarding her identity. She experiences a deep psychological conflict regarding her maternal figures. While she yearns to remember her birth mother, she acknowledges that it was [[Elaine]] who raised her and provided the very architecture of her magic and survival, but more importantly, she realizes that ultimately both died alongside the real father she can't remember and [[Corvin]].

_"Mother... I- I- I-."

_She cuddles into the ghost of Artus taking her hand until the touch makes her able to talk._

_"I-I'm still that girl in... in the river... Still scared. Still alone. Still wanting someone to hold me and tell me I've done enough. Tell me I can stop now. I thought I'd gotten past this, but the fear is still there — the same fear from the river, the same fear of being alone in the dark... I never stopped being her."_

For this very reason, the [[Artus]] shapeshifting, even with the uncanny hallucination it has, is incredibly effective as in the cold, [[Amadea]] deeply wants the illusion to be real, even knowing it's not. The combination of this desperate need for relief of her grief to finally be taken away, plus the things it constantly whispers to her in how the [[Facewalker]] wants to be authentic as she is while disguised as [[Artus]] is the moment that [[Amadea]] starts giving in to the embrace and the comfort it is giving with the physical touch and the face she's agonizing over losing.

Past this threshold she begins to internalize the idea that: _"Maybe this is a fitting end. This is what I deserve for driving everyone away. Whether it is [[Cordelia]], [[Luminaire]], my real family, or any member of a found one. I'm always left alone. I'm sorry, [[Artus]], I really tried, but I couldn't earn the title you've bestowed upon me all those [[Cycle]]s ago. I truly never deserved the circlet."_

Ultimately, it's one of the closest times [[Amadea]] comes to dying as she's in the embrace of the [[Facewalker]]. She is rescued by [[Cordelia]] who pierces the shielded ice exterior of the cage as a tornado of [[Cindergale]] like [[Sephira]]. In the burst of fire, [[Cordelia]] finds [[Amadea]] in the embrace of the [[Erosyx]], and immediately uses the motto of how to fight the horror and with her newly acquired knowledge of the Grief Cathedral she steps as the role of witness on the motto Reveal, Severe, Remember. 

This is a mirror scene to the beating that [[Cordelia]] had to endure with her bare back, and she crashes in to break her paralysis of guilt, and grief. She then delivers the famous quote about the [[Atonalis]], circling back to the "And you have bled enough" that [[Amadea]] taught her when she broke in the estate to save her. After hurting the [[Facewalker]] it retreats, and as [[Amadea]] is horrifyingly bare naked, [[Cordelia]] offers her back her dignity, proper clothing and delivers the definitive speech of [[Atonalis]]:

_"[[Amadea]]. Look at me. No— Don't talk. Look at me. I know it sounded desperate. I know it felt like genuine fear and desire. I too feel pity for them, but we do not share their burden.

_Understanding is not consent to be consumed, they are only the shadow of a person who decided to stay broken. They are not the victim and that's their trap. It is the crystallized refusal to heal, given claws._

_Whatever it whispered to you... that was born from honest intent. It truly believed it needed you, and that you were the only thing wounded enough to resonate. But it only cares that you bleed for them. And you have bled enough. Remember?"

In the whole cathartic exchange [[Amadea]] realizes that there's even entities that desperately envy her type of life, no matter what she has suffered, giving her value to her own self esteem. 

[[Amadea]] is shocked cold, and silent, while [[Cordelia]] is clinging to [[Amadea]], hugging her tearful of how she almost lost while desperately crying and saying she shouldn't have pushed her away. While [[Amadea]] is still in shock, she says to [[Cordelia]] that everything has been the fault of her own agonizing nightmares, and that, if anything, she should be the one apologizing, not [[Cordelia]].

The encounter ends with a somber laugh shared between both of them and the exchange of the [[Waltz of Wandering Love]]. After [[Artus]] this is the first time someone saves her and it didn't have to end in tragedy. For [[Cordelia]], the exchange triggers the evolution of her [[Jolly]] [[Legend Trait]] into [[Fiery Passion]].

_Act 35. A Dream Bathed in Gold_

[[Cordelia]] and [[Amadea]] hunt down the [[Facewalker]], and in the combination of [[Cindergale]], [[Flux]], [[Void]], [[Crystal]] and [[Strand]] it's the first time they properly fight together to the [[Waltz of Wandering Love]]. Once the monster is vanquished, [[Amadea]] whispers to it the final words as it dissolves into static: _"So long, [[Artus]]..."_

Before heading to the gallery, the [[Auric Aria]] manifests to her in a dream as she's cowering. She only says two words in the entire interaction: _"Rise, [[Amadea]]"_ as a callback to the coronation of [[Artus]] but now under the light she bestows upon her is the blessing that she can continue onward without [[Artus]].

This evolves [[Amadea]]'s [[Reverent]] [[Legend Trait]] as they reunite with the rest of the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] which show affection for [[Amadea]], giving her back her self-worth, particularly from [[Luminaire]] whose whole exchange makes her feel guilty for what she told her earlier. This capacity for empathy is a breakthrough emotionally for [[Luminaire]] as she's treated as an equal and solidifies the [[Weight of Indulgence]] of what she had experienced with [[Amadea]] in opposition of [[Hollowmarch]]'s treatment.

_Act 36: [[Consonance]] Lies in the Future, Not the Past_

This is the Founding Vow and final ascent for the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] to the place of pilgrimage.

Atop the gallery when they reach the amphitheaters they all shout their [[Motif Awakening]] to figure out what they have to do which changes the trajectory they each have after the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]].

[[Orphael]] is the first, he gets [[Discordant Interference]] to mend his past and the shame of his [[Lustful]] nature.

[[Sephira]] is the second, she gets [[Consonance]] for which her wish lies in the future.

[[Luminaire]] is the third, as it produced [[Discordant Interference]] for which she will go back to [[Hollowmarch]].

[[Luminaire]] tells [[Amadea]]: _"See? I don't hold any particular grudge against you, anymore at least. Although don't believe I will forget for everything you've done to me as a criminal. I still have my own path to fix backwards, and an obligation to serve."_

[[Cordelia]] is the fourth, she gets [[Consonance]], and she will find the future to mend in the quest of wandering with [[Amadea]].

[[Amadea]] is the last to shout her [[Motif Awakening]] wish, and the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] signals to her that her wish is in the future, not in looking back at [[Artus]] and the life she's been performing through [[Ballad]]s, which is the direct lead to creating [[Iridia]], and the ultimate push she needed to heal and mend the past.

#### Chapter VII: The Dawn of [[Iridia]] and the Violet Empress

[[Strand]] | [[Echoing Bonds]]

_Act 37: There Is A Place, Where the Trees Grow Violet_

Following the ride down from the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]], each of the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] go their own separate ways. [[Luminaire]] departs with [[Sephira]] back to [[Hollowmarch]], [[Orphael]] joins [[Cordelia]] and [[Luminaire]] for the next [[Enclave]] following the hunt of a legendary beast precisely on the lands that [[Amadea]] wants to find.

[[Amadea]] follows the lead of [[Orphael]], and with [[Cordelia]], they finally uncover that the Violet Grove does exist, and gathering the bandwagons of survivors from the Boreal Reach, the pilgrimage begins to found the city of [[Iridia]] at the very center.

She recruits anyone following the cause with the promise of a dreamland and safe haven for all, like Alena and [[Deliah]], she mirrors how [[Artus]] used to improvise the speeches on their time stealing together with the banner of several golden stars that represents [[Iridia]], and reclaiming the legendary tree of the violet grove.

The founding of [[Iridia]] has several members that are pious of the [[Auric Aria]], and it presents the first theological conflict for [[Amadea]] in her newly formed [[Waltz Pillar]] society that grows for the next chapters.

The moment [[Amadea]] reaches this place, she realizes this is the exact description of Elaine's dream, this is the "Avaloch Basin" she thought, as this is has the exact place: the silver sand, the mountain that dreams of fractals, the large [[Eclipsed Waterfalls]], but it is Iridescent when the water meets the sand due to how the [[Fractal Kaleidoscope]] of the [[Grand Thread Rings]] of the [[Great Harmonic Loom]] reflect on the converging [[Leylines]].

Thus, the real "Avaloch Basin" of [[Elaine]]'s dream is the exact chalice basin where Iridia is eventually founded near the boreal reach. [[Amadea]] names it as the "Iridescent Basin" and the city of Iridia is founded on the very center of it.

_Act 38: A Safe Haven of [[Ballad]]s & Fantasy [[Civic]] Plays_

Founding of [[Iridia]], [[Reality Bender]] evolution from [[Amadea]]. She finally makes the [[Echoing Bonds]] of [[Artus]] planted on the beginning of what will be the new grove.

The memory and promise is finally a reality.

[[Iridia]] begins with the [[Civic]] of Poetry, [[Ballad]]s, and Stories for guilds that replicate the idea of what [[Artus]] had done for her to transform grief into something meaningful. it is also a teaching lesson for [[Amadea]] to realize how loss has become something beautiful, and is now inspiring generations of new children.

[[Orphael]] goes on his quest to grow the [[Tide Singing Arts]] in [[Iridia]], the nearby [[Enclave]]s and leaves to join with [[Sephira]] in [[Hollowmarch]].

The chapter also shows the reality of these [[Civic]] plays painting sets, rehearsing lines, the anxiety of performers to beautifully contrast the apocalyptic horror outside the city walls. It shows the citizens engaging in the mundane labor of creating art, proving that they are not just surviving, but actively choosing to live which is the core thesis of [[Amadea]] transformation of living instead of merely surviving.

In [[Iridia]], originally [[Cordelia]] follows the laws of the plays exactly as they are, acting subconsciously on her abuse as if [[Amadea]]'s institutional law is above everything. Seeing this, [[Amadea]] breaks her paralysis saying that rules aren't absolute and that she should always question authority, especially if it's [[Ballad]]s, they're meant to portray emotion, not to be constrained by rules.

_Act 39: The Seed of [[Revelation]] of the Violet Grove_

The child between, [[Deliah]], the Boreal King's Daughter and [[Cyril]] the Bartender Informant of [[Amadea]] is the first baby born as truly iridian.

[[Motif Awakening]] to [[Luminance]] when she realizes the tree wasn't a violet legendary tree at all, it was an [[Auric Peach]] tree.

[[Amadea]] [[Luminance]] [[Ornament]] manifested the moment the planted seed from [[Artus]] grew, and it turned out to be a seed of a regular [[Auric Peach]] tree. This happens because her whole reality and determination has existed alongside this ideal of the violet garden. 

The sheer cognitive friction processed as joy and liberation of learning that the seed was an [[Auric Peach]] all along, one of the most common trees and not a legendary one is a moment of pure [[Emotional Authenticity]]. [[Amadea]] laughs finally in tears of relief: _"Oh my, you adorable idiot, [[Artus]]. We were chasing the very end of the world all for an [[Auric Peach]] Tree. But now seeing it in the center of the [[Iridia]], it is the only golden light between all the violet. Perhaps that was the lesson all along."_ [[Amadea]] unlocks her [[Luminance]] alongside lots of [[Fragment of Lucidity]].}

This moment also makes her reflect on her teenage fantasy of her original [[Soul Leitmotif]] being [[Luminance]] modelling herself after [[Elaine]]:
_"If only you could look at it, [[Artus]]. In the end I managed to get a [[Motif Awakening]] to [[Luminance]], even if as my last [[Ornament]]. Thanks to you."_

_Act 40: [[Dear Fugue]], My Beloved Black Wing_

The chapter focus on [[Lazarus]], on the [[Court of Delicacies]] and the struggles with the growing new [[Major Settlement]] of [[Iridia]], and how [[Amadea]] begins to experiment and master [[Dear Fugue]] by teaming with [[Orphael]] who teaches her about [[Flux]] after witnessing one of her [[Ballad]]s with the new founded [[Enclave]] near her [[Capital]].

She later creates her signature Obsidian Empress construct named "[[Dear Fugue]]." that sometimes she refers to as Black Wing. [[Dear Fugue]] runs on the same principle that [[Daedalus]] figured out for the [[Auric Bells]] by having the imbued [[Resonance Box]] fill first before making the energy turn into the construct that allows for the flock of [[Obsidian Feather]]s to gather.

[[Dear Fugue]] is inspired by nature of the creatures near [[Iridia]] that are the precursors to dragons as wyrms and other winged serpents.

_Act 41: Dawn Must Come for Those Who Cannot Watch Themselves_

Reintroduction of [[Lenore]] who has heard the myth of a new safe haven and realizes it's [[Amadea]] who did manage to found [[Iridia]]. She rejoins the main cast with [[Amadea]] as [[Iridia]]'s scholar, and architect of [[Cosmic Motion]] imitating the water systems of Lol-Ha' in the city capital.

First conflicts with [[Lazarus]] on the [[Court of Delicacies]]. She realizes that it's necessary to have a [[Shadow Order]] to keep the light of [[Iridia]] afloat, she properly becomes a [[Great Justiciar]], and starts creating the [[Civic]] apparatus of her [[Civilization]], including the [[Obsidian Feather Society]]. She starts getting involved with [[Luminaire]] and [[Hollowmarch]] as an opposing mirror to their theology on suffering, and reviews her own version of the [[Auric Order]] mainly focused on the [[Waltz Pillar]] instead of the [[Aureus Pillar]].

_Act 42: Do You Recognize Him!? Of Course You Do!!

The [[Court of Delicacies]] is the [[Loss-Beauty Lunatic]] evolution of those who followed [[Junius]] after the [[Silver Blood]] kingdom fell. [[Pollux]] is one of the most prominent figures that eventually becomes the leader by assassinating the ruling master, and his own mentor, [[Lazarus]] after [[Kastor]]'s death due to the ideological conflict on the nature of beauty, of wounds, and quality vs quantity of [[Lazarus]] & [[Pollux]].

[[Cordelia]]'s aristocratic family has direct ties of the fragmentation of the [[Silver Blood]] kingdom that fell apart from the death of [[Medea]]. This ties back in the next chapters to the [[Amadea]] / [[Medea]] dynamic on [[Artus]] [[Mythic Drift]].

As the conflict escalates with the [[Court of Delicacies]] the real antagonist [[Pollux]] appears, as [[Lazarus]] has been dead for a time already and it is him who controls the corpse with the [[Ink Arts]] of his [[Void]] tethers. This is the first major reveal of the structure of the organization.

When word spreads through the [[Obsidian Feather Society]] that [[Lazarus]] isn't alive anymore and it's [[Pollux]] who has been puppeteering his cadaver it sparks the transition into the war of the [[Shadow Order]] courts between [[Iridia]] and its owls against the foxes of the [[Court of Delicacies]].

[[Pollux]] reveals himself to [[Amadea]] as the one behind [[Lazarus]] pulling the strings, He reveals that [[Kastor]], the fox-masked pugilist that gets killed early was the younger brother of [[Pollux]], but the reveals don't stop there. [[Pollux]] weaponizes the first major reveal by taunting [[Amadea]].

However, it doesn't stop there, he begins mourning his brother, and through the [[Ink Arts]] steals the core of [[Dear Fugue]], saying that he had to attend a funeral that shouldn't ever have had to happen as he was his younger brother but says he is grateful that [[Amadea]] gave him such a beautiful wound to carry so that [[Artus]] [[Sky Glass]] alongside her [[Rose Seed]] would be even a better pair for his collection. [[Pollux]] thanks [[Amadea]] for the gift she gave her, his own [[Void]] [[Motif Awakening]] due to the loss of his brother.

He will say that without her he wouldn't become the [[Loss Anchor]]. She gave back the favor that [[The Seven Hands]] [[Anxithor]] first gave her purpose when it killed [[Artus]] on her lap. 

The [[Anxithor]] [[Amadea]] and [[Artus]] fought, called [[The Seven Hands]] had both that moniker and that many hands because it represents how many hands [[Corvin]], the leader of the caravan, had to grab of all the children that he had to grab to take them from the cold of the dying world like he grabbed [[Amadea]]. The reason it looks like that and has that many twitching is his trauma of rescuing children as a [[Threat Cartographer]]. The massive scythe it wields comes from the idea that he is saving people and himself from death. [[Kay]] and [[Elaine]] both died in the dawn that drowned in fire, but [[Corvin]] survived, and from there he became a monster.

**The Final Scene of Score VII**

_"You wouldn't know the lengths I've had to sacrifice just to learn your pathetic origin story. Perhaps you never asked," he says, turning the [[Resonance Box]] shard in his gloved fingers. "But do you know who is the monster that killed your knight? You never asked who [[The Seven Hands]] had been in life. It must have been someone pretty strong to even manage to turn into an [[Ascendant]]. And you do know someone who was that strong."_

_Amadea's hand goes to her saber._

_"Its name was [[Corvin]]. Caravan master. Music teacher. The man who taught your [[Artus]] to play the cello."_

_She goes still in a way that Cordelia, watching from the shadows, has never seen._

_"You remember the night he disappeared? Yes, that Discant attack that destroyed your first world?. Everyone assumed he died covering the retreat. Brave, stupid, beautiful [[Corvin]], dying so the children could run."_

_[[Pollux]]'s voice softens, mockingly gentle._

_"He didn't die. He broke. The Discant didn't kill him—it just... opened the door. And what walked out wasn't [[Corvin]] anymore. Just the part of him that couldn't stop protecting. The part that counted children every night and couldn't sleep if the number was wrong. The part that loved so much it forgot how to be anything except vigilance. The part that yearned for the future he himself had stolen from him."_

_"He became the counting. The hands. The scythe you saw. He became the thing that killed the boy you loved—because Artus stepped between it and you, right when your own mistake should've killed you because you were drenched in the same fulfilling prophecy of the fear of losing the future you just learned to love. You caused him to die, both of them."_

_Amadea is not breathing._

_"Ah, it's so beautiful! He would have been so proud of Artus," [[Pollux]] continues. "If he'd been human one more day, he would have seen your knight and thought: 'They finally made it, a prodigy in the duet of strings. Finally, this is the pair that will make it through love and all.' But he wasn't human. He was already the monster. And monsters don't get to be proud. They only get to be hungry. Hungry of mistakes like the one you did that ultimately killed your fearless knight."_

_"Honestly, I'm even surprised you and your doomed knight managed to take down an [[Ascendant]] [[Atonalis]]. Even I haven't heard of such a miracle in any of the entire records of the [[Court of Delicacies]]."

_He pauses to boast himself_

_"What I would give to meet this man if I could! Such a beast taken by a duo of a prodigy [[Spellweaver]] and his baggage! Truly, a deep loss of potential, a shame that [[Artus]] died instead of you. He would've been a prodigy of the sword if it wasn't because the failed empress '[[Amadea]]' killed him. He certainly would have been a much better ruler of [[Iridia]]."

_Hearing those words, [[Amadea]] is about to lunge in a fit of rage._

_"Ah, ah, ah! Not so fast, my dear, don't let yourself be blinded so quickly by emotion or I may accidentally break this."_

_However, [[Pollux]] interrupts her, holding hostage the [[Resonance Box]] of [[Artus]]._

_"You want to know why I collected your shard? Not for its beauty. No. Yes, it is undeniable, but that's what killed [[Lazarus]]. No, I have it because it carries the last frequency of a man who loved too much and a boy who loved too well. That resonance—that specific chord—is the most beautiful wound I have ever heard. And you, Empress of Nothing, gave it to me."_

_He waits. As if expecting her to thank him._

_When she doesn't as [[Amadea]] is still trying to control herself, He tilts his head. "I'm graciously sharing information you were to blind to see, you know. You should be grateful."_

_"If not, I'll have to teach you first how to give proper respect."

_He raises his own hand to the air with his [[Flux]] [[Soul Leitmotif]], and points at the vein work filled with the hue purple of [[Void]]. and then shouts dropping the theatrical act: "LOOK, DO YOU RECOGNIZE HIM!? Of course you do!! That's my little brother, [[Kastor]]! Right here, with me, with the life you cut short in the funeral I shouldn't have to ever attend as his big brother. Exactly placed on my own [[Soul-Key]]"

_"Thank you, False Empress [[Amadea]]. For my brother's death. For my awakening. For completing me the way that [[Corvin]], [[The Seven Hands]] [[Anxithor]] that slaughtered [[Artus]] for nothing, completed you."_

**Amadea's Reaction (Internal, then external)

She does not scream. She does not cry, although she is in the verge of both. She goes **silent**, slightly ventilating but with her blood piercingly cold.

**Internal beat (shown through action, not told):**

_She remembers [[Corvin]] sitting beside her after her mother died. She remembers him teaching Artus the cello, patient and warm, saying "the earth is the thing that doesn't move." She remembers the last time she saw him—standing alone, cello in one hand, broken wheel in the other, buying them time._

_She thought he died a hero._

_She thought he was at peace._

_And all that time, he was becoming the thing that would take Artus from her. Not out of malice. Out of love. Love that had nowhere to go. Love that curdled into vigilance. Vigilance that became hunger._

_The same thing could happen to her._

_She sees it now—the path she is walking. [[Obsidian Feather Society]]. The protection that never stops. The refusal to let anyone else die for her. That is [[Corvin]]'s path. That is the path that ends with seven hands and a scythe and no memory of the girl you once saved._

_She does not flinch from the vision._

_She chooses, in that moment, to be different._

_She will protect. But she will also build. She will remember. She will let herself be loved—and let herself lose—without becoming the counting._

_She looks at [[Pollux]], and grounds herself by deliberately grasping her blade hard enough to draw blood against the blade so that she can feel something._

_She says, very quietly: "You showed me my future. Now let me show you yours."_

_She draws the [[Hollow-Point]]._

After the encounter [[Pollux]] escapes but deliberately leads another agent as bait for [[Amadea]] as means to break her before she can fully process what he revealed about [[Corvin]] and the death of [[Artus]].

His last words before he vanishes in the dusk are:

_"Fine, if you haven't stopped fighting long enough to notice. I'll wait. I'm very good at waiting. And when the breaking happens—when the empress finally becomes the beautiful wound she's been carrying on my brother's blood—I'll be there."

_"One last remark before you drown in grief, my quill. I must admit you're so [[Reckless]] for letting your emotions get the better hang of your judgment, you're even closer to the future of [[Corvin]] than I originally thought. I never had your [[Resonance Box]], you beautiful idiot! The [[Sky Glass]] of your self-condemnation is still with you. How dumb do you feel!? It never left your side! Ah! Even I am delighted at the genius of my [[Reverberation Arts]]!"_

#### Chapter VIII: Ink Written in [[Obsidian Feather]]s

[[Weight of Purpose]] | [[Fragment of Meaning]]

- This chapter begins revealing all the truths of [[Amadea]]'s past and compounding them after the appearance of [[Pollux]]t: [[Corvin]] was who killed [[Artus]] after becoming the dreaded [[The Seven Hands]] [[Anxithor]], The Tragedy of the [[Legend]] of [[Medea]] and the real origin of the [[Waltz of Wandering Love]].
- The entire chapter is the fight between two undercover organizations from the newly founded [[Iridia]] and the [[Court of Delicacies]] that has been haunting [[Cordelia]] and [[Amadea]] since the pugilist and the original clash between [[Amadea]] and [[Cordelia]]'s familial affairs.

_Act 43: Safety Without Freedom is Just Another Form of Violence_

The chapter opens by the continuation of the [[Corvin]] revelation, [[Amadea]] panicking, and entering a similar spiraling state but grounding herself in her order, and in [[Cordelia]]. She however, begins to overcompensate becoming a martyr herself to prevent to have the fate of [[Corvin]].

[[Ligeia]], the armored singer of the [[Court of Delicacies]] uses the exact same prismatic polearm technique that [[Elaine]] used, giving a dark mirror to [[Amadea]] of her own childhood and surrogate mother even if not strictly related. At her worst point, it curls into an obsession after realizing the truth of [[The Seven Hands]] that killed [[Artus]].

On the subject of theology, she leans [[Iridia]] ultimately deciding to be only [[Waltz Pillar]] leaning as she refuses to be fully [[Chorus Pillar]] or fully fledged [[Aureus Pillar]] by seeing both ends of the spectrum. [[Amadea]] explores her [[Reverent]] nature.

Furthermore, [[Pollux]] focuses on the weaponization of [[Amadea]]'s unknown past is the climax of his symbolism on their early fights against [[Pollux]] after [[Corvin]]'s revelation, where he deliberately brings [[Amadea]] to her lowest and offers her the one thing even the [[Facewalker]] could not: her _origins_.

He doesn't actually know the details of her biological family. He bluffs and weaponizes the one nickname [[Corvin]] used for her: "Oh please 'my little sharp note' don't make me eat my own words." Pollux weaponizes that for Amadea. But the bluff works because [[Amadea]] has a wound that predates [[Artus]], predates [[Elaine]], predates everyone — the wound of a name she doesn't know and a face she can't remember and a word spoken before static that she will never reconstruct. [[Pollux]] finds the wound that has no scar tissue because it was never allowed to close, and he holds it open. [[Amadea]], however, sees through [[Pollux]] bluff after thinking through, replying back: _"If you really knew my mother, you would know the entire meaning of mother's sacrifice. She used her dying breath to stand in a freezing current solely to give me a life she couldn't ever see. My mother didn't die to [[Static Criticality]] so that I would later succumb to the surrender of the [[Void]]. You are nothing but a liar."
