[[Pollux]] reaction to [[Amadea]]'s ability to see through his facade is a profound moment for him, as this confirms that [[Amadea]] is an upmost priceless possession. The moment of [[Amadea]]'s [[Cunning]] defiance motivates him to see her as the perfect quill which is valuable precisely because it's hard to get which feeds harder into his [[Delusional]] concept of having [[Amadea]] ultimate physical, mental, and ideological surrender.

_"I am surprised, Empress of Nothing. You truly are a sharp note. That's right, "[[Amadea]]", even I couldn't unearth the pathetic death of your mother. It proves how utterly worthless she was, but that also makes your meaningless defiance truly, the most beautiful quill fit for my [[Magnum Opus]]. I'm deeply relieved that you're still the woman who killed the last tether of my beloved family."_

_He lets the silence stretch just long enough to become suffocating. He failed to break her with her mother's ghost, so he pivots to the phantom she actually remembers._

_"But I wonder how much you truly recall of your own miraculous fate. Ever thought of the name [[The Seven Hands]]."

_[[Pollux]] begins pacing, his ink strings twitching with predatory rhythm._

_"Sure, you could tell yourself it had seven limbs because [[Corvin]] wanted to shield the innocent with the [[Auric Heptacode]]. But tell me, Empress of Nothing... on the night your memory began to intertwine with his, how many children were in that caravan?"_

_[[Amadea]]'s mind races back to the cramped, freezing wagon. She counts the faces in her memory. Herself, [[Artus]], the boy who died of the cough, the one accompanying him. [[Carmina]] and two others huddled in the lower level once [[Corvin]] went down._
[[Cordelia]]
_"Seven?," [[Amadea]] says, confused of what point he's making.

_"Ah, my quill, you surely can't be this idiotic. I just said you were a sharp note, don't make me eat my own words over your own rotting memory."_

_"Are you only trying to provoke me? I didn't expect you to resort to such cheap tactics after this entire planning—"_

_"Your knight was not there." He interrupted._

_[[Amadea]] freezes, that is right, [[Artus]] was not with her in [[Corvin]]'s caravan, he was in [[Kay]]'s. the word catching in her throat as a terrible, creeping [[Dissonance]] takes root. 

_"There were only six of us." She says with words coming quiet._ 

_[[Pollux]] stops pacing. He glimpses back at her, his voice dropping to a jagged, devastating whisper._

_"Then why was he counting to seven?"_

_[[Amadea]]'s earned [[Composure]] drops. [[Corvin]] dropped a child during the [[Static Criticality]] that saved her. He was never counting the living children in the wagon; he was obsessively, endlessly counting the ghost of the one he let slip. That's the reason why he didn't want to reveal the truth about the [[Auric Heptacode]] and go back to the fact that he will reveal it if she's still alive by the end of the [[Echo]] and what [[Lenore]] had interrupted. They all had a fresh wound of someone who didn't because they went to save her._

_"Someone was taken by the [[Void]] before you." He breaks the silence._

_[[Pollux]] finally laughs at the tragedy._

_"It's so beautiful! [[Corvin]] never snapped during the final [[Discant]] attack. Every single night, you were sleeping inches away from a man incubating a monster that had that very hallucination every single day."_

_Act 44: The Shadow War of the [[Delusional]] Fox Against the Great Owl_

[[Pollux]] is one of the most prominent users of [[Ink Arts]] which makes him also obsessed with the idea of using the [[Obsidian Feather]] of [[Amadea]] as the quill to write in ink the music sheet of all his compositions for his [[Soul Oscillator]]. His trait of being a [[Over-giving Devout]] manifests profoundly on the idea of subduing the [[Obsidian Feather Society]] and using [[Amadea]] as the instrument to write his musical compositions. This delusion is metaphorical as [[Obsidian Feather]]s are the signature attack of [[Amadea]] using a volley of [[Crystallization Magic]] imbued of [[Crystal]] and [[Void]], and they don't exist as physical objects beyond her attacks. He views her as THE [[Obsidian Feather]], masterpiece quill, and crown jewel from a purely aesthetic perspective given her [[Hollow-Point]] combination, and the [[Shadow Order]] she made of the same name, the [[Obsidian Feather Society]]. [[Age Crisis]]

Moreover, he initially mistakes [[Amadea]] for a [[Silver Blood]] given the color of her hair. However, he does note that she doesn't have the signature [[Silver Blood]] eyes, instead she has green [[Resonance]] colored ones. This is a profound irony that he weaponizes later to compare her to [[Medea]] as he believes she's not even properly a [[Silver Blood]] yet she's resting on the grave of one. That irony is part of why she considers [[Amadea]] the [[Magnum Opus]] of the [[Court of Delicacies]] which was founded originally by [[Junius]] and the betrayal of a real [[Silver Blood]].

An armored cross singer, [[Ligeia]], from the [[Court of Delicacies]] learns to ride [[Sephira]] and she is also facing a fight of an evenly matched singer who is used her own whistling fan music to continue improving her own standing and magic. This profound humiliation makes [[Sephira]] understand the principle of [[Rhythmic Entrainment]] of [[Cindergale]] and is the first proper principle of [[The Principles of Magic]]. [[Ligeia]] uses a long double polearm that acts as a [[Luminance]] kaleidoscope. It is revealed that she is also a true [[Silver Blood]], like [[Amadea]] she does possess silver hair but she has the actual lineage to [[Lunehymn]], which is the reason why she wields and armored mask to cover her eyes and pass like [[Amadea]] as a regular person and her backstory appears as she was recruited by [[Pollux]] during his expedition to [[Amadea]]'s original home continent to uncover her story in the time [[Amadea]] was in the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]] after assassinating [[Kastor]].

[[Sephira]] teaches [[Amadea]] stamina and resistance to carry momentum in a fight, [[Amadea]] teaches [[Sephira]] precision to make each [[Dance]] devastatingly more effective, pinpointing each other's weaknesses and how to surpass them.

[[Luminaire]] passes on the words of [[Aurelian]] to [[Amadea]] that she learned from [[Daedalus]] when she was younger: _"Meaning is applied human-" she softly chuckles, "and well sometimes [[Demi-Human]], devotion. And to be devoid of devotion is to be living without a reason to live."_

_Act 45: The [[Great Justiciar]] Who Keeps Sacrificing Herself_

The political struggles with the [[Court of Delicacies]] challenge [[Amadea]]'s leadership, forcing her to realize that protecting people requires compromises she hated in her youth and guides her path as a [[Great Justiciar]].

[[Amadea]] doesn't recall the entire details of her childhood and of the journey with [[Artus]] but a secret character of her past, [[Carmina]] joins with [[Pollux]] during the war of the [[Shadow Order]]s, and it's the reason she has a breakdown akin to Mighty Nein. [[Amadea]] is unable to shake the feeling that she knows her. She survived with [[Corvin]] until he became a monster.

This chapter considers the fight where [[Carmina]] attempts to kill [[Amadea]] and directly confronts her:

"You know I heard your ballad. Again, and again."

"The first climax is a theater on my own death. I had countless nightmares about that exact moment and you can't even remember it properly."

Carmina smirks, changing the paint in the environment.

"It's funny, isn't it? How people can have such a different memory of the exact same thing. It's all a matter of perspective, I guess."

She pauses one moment to throw a painting of Artus representation as three golden lines. The same painting of [[Artus]] she used to represent as a child.

"And your 'warmth' is only meant to save yourself."

"But Artus—" Amadea tries to respond.

"You left me, Amadea," Carmina hisses, her eyes burning with cold fury. 

"You left me to die in the flames. I was pinned. I was screaming. And you ran. You ran to save your precious knight and you left me behind." 

"Carmina, I didn't—"

"Don't." Her voice cracks, but her gaze holds.

"I saw you, I saw you turn away. I saw you choose." 

"I tried to—"

"You tried nothing."

Carmina draws her blade, her eyes glistening with tears she will not shed. 

"You tried nothing, 'Violet'. You made your choice. And now I am going to make mine. To paint the world beautiful in the cinders that drowned in fire."

[[Amadea]] ends up hitting the killing strike first in a rising lotus of jagged [[Crystal]] and [[Hollow-Point]] that impales her, with the blood staining the [[Obsidian Feather]] flower red. This is the same type of banned form of the [[Hollow-Point]] that later Amadea outlaws as she sees this magic she used to kill one of the core members of the court of delicacies as a crime she herself committed once.

The climax of the physical exchange — [[Amadea]] impaling [[Carmina]] with a [[Crystal]] lotus — is a devastating corruption of [[Amadea]]’s own identity and magic.

- **The Perversion of Beauty:** Throughout the narrative, Amadea fights against Lazarus and the Court of Delicacies, who believe in the aestheticization of wounds and forcing suffering to bloom into "Rose Seeds". By impaling Carmina with a _lotus_, Amadea inadvertently mimics the exact horrific artistry of her enemies. She has turned the only other caravan survivor, and her childhood friend's death into a beautiful, crystalline display.
    
- **The Weaponization of Crystal:** [[Crystal]] magic in [[Arcanoria]] requires [[Absolute Certainty]]. To cast the lotus, [[Amadea]] had to summon absolute, unflinching certainty to strike down the girl she once failed to save. The spell proves that when pushed to the brink, [[Amadea]]’s survival instinct overrides her guilt and morality.

Despite being devastated after this fight [[Amadea]] uncovers the name of the [[Stravos Household]] and of [[Leda]] the violinist on [[Lazarus]] records due to the insight of the dying breath of [[Carmina]] and the link to her past.

As the [[Pollux]] situation progresses, the shattering applies for both [[Cordelia]] and [[Luminaire]]. [[Cordelia]] due to her family situation, to [[Luminaire]] due to the revelation that her [[Soul Oscillator]] is like [[Pollux]] and that it is supposed to represent her voice. Information that was previously withheld by her family to use her literal soul's voice as an instrument of the state.

_Act 46: A Noble Sacrifice of A Cost Too Great For A Single Soul To Bear_

The act is a 3 theme breakdown between [[Amadea]] and the [[Corvin]] struggle that she's repeating the same patterns as a [[Threat Cartographer]]. Plus the realization that she has been replicating [[Artus]]'s martyrdom pattern in how she leads. Unbearable precision about what [[Amadea]]'s leadership style has cost someone she loves. She comes to the truth of the [[Stravos Household]] and of [[Pollux]] backstory.

[[Cordelia]] finds the burrowed well in the story of [[A Burrowed Name]], and she gazes into it as part of her research on the [[Legend]] of [[Elara]]. [[Sephira]] is with [[Cordelia]] during the expedition, they recreate together the [[Void]], [[Cindergale]] and [[Strand]] used in the ritual to realize how to heal internal wounds by watching the dancing shadows of the purifying fire.

Then [[Miracle Magic]] researched by [[Cordelia]] once she finally finds the ruined hometown of [[Elara]] and how the origin of sacrificing everything to save something loved made her become [[The Cradle of Echoes]], proving that both [[Corvin]] and [[Elara]] faced what [[Amadea]] must face as well but each of them broke into [[Atonalis]].

Then the beginning of the capture on the revelation of [[Medea]], her past, and the real meaning behind the [[Waltz of Wandering Love]] when the agent is planted after [[Carmina]]'s death.

_Act 47: The Second Death of [[Artus]]_

The [[Waltz of Wandering Love]] is a song composed originally by [[Medea]] when she founded the [[Silver Blood]] kingdom, this later realization hits very hard [[Cordelia]] and [[Amadea]].

This is one of the Spiraling moments for [[Cordelia]]. It makes [[Amadea]] realize that the Violet Empress never existed and who came before her ended in tragedy. [[Amadea]] is able to ground herself by being the protection of [[Cordelia]]. "We can't both break at once." Theologically, they are mirroring the [[Selenea]] / [[Auric Aria]] dynamic without knowing the truth of the [[Sixth Cycle]].

As she comes to the realization of the second death of [[Artus]] due to [[Mythic Drift]] and that the Violet Empress never existed while [[Medea]] only points to a grave this poses a fundamental question _"What am I even fighting for?"_

[[Carmina]] despite being killed by [[Amadea]], plants her final painting which was a trap in case she died. It is her final act of vengeance as something she left as the encore of her last play with the chronicler to show [[Amadea]] the proof that the Violet Empress is nothing but the death of [[Medea]].

Thus, [[Carmina]]’s final act of vengeance — having [[Ligeia]] paint the violet rhombus with silver edges using her own [[Silver Blood]] — is the most brilliant and devastating psychological strike in the entire [[Sonata]].

- **The Corruption of Childhood Innocence:** Back in the caravan, [[Carmina]] was the young girl who painted the members of their found family onto scraps of cloth. She originally drew [[Amadea]] as a silver-edged violet rhombus to represent the gem of her legendary crown. Weaponizing this specific childhood memory turns a symbol of innocent adoration into a death sentence for [[Amadea]]'s identity.

- **The Reality of the Violet Empress:** [[Carmina]] uses [[Ligeia]], a known [[Silver Blood]], for the painting. In the true history of [[Arcanoria]], the title "Violet Empress" was not born from flowers, but from the color the ground turned when Queen [[Medea]]'s [[Silver Blood]] mixed with [[Lunehymn]] as she bled to death from thirty-three stab wounds delivered by her closest confidants.

- **The Destruction of the Myth:** By painting the rhombus with actual [[Silver Blood]], [[Carmina]] connects [[Amadea]]’s childhood crown directly to [[Medea]]’s horrific assassination. [[Carmina]] is forcing [[Amadea]] to realize that the "Violet Empress" she modeled her life after is just a mythic drift of a slaughtered woman.

The painting was her plan in case she died at the hands of [[Amadea]] to deliver a paralyzing message: [[Amadea]] is not a savior, and her crown is not a symbol of hope. Her crown is a bloodstain of betrayal, and by killing [[Carmina]] to save herself, [[Amadea]] has proven she is no better than the traitors who left [[Medea]] to die on the steps of her throne.

_Act 48: Wear the Fox-Mask, Empress of Nothing_

[[Pollux]] and [[Amadea]] reach the climax of their confrontation as he makes the ultimatum to surrender and become his queen of grief. He uses the fact that [[Amadea]] is on a Spiraling [[Composure]] again now that she knows the entire truth.

The [[Pollux]] / [[Amadea]] [[Legend Relationship]] is the stress test of the system of asymmetry: For [[Pollux]], [[Amadea]] is [[Sworn Duet]]. For [[Amadea]], [[Pollux]] is [[Vowed Nemesis]]. There's an entire tier of 5 stages, almost the entire spectrum of difference in perception where both readings are correct. [[Pollux]] twisted logic sees gifts in the confessions that will break [[Amadea]]'s [[Composure]]. [[Amadea]] detects them as attacks.

[[Pollux]], the [[Loss-Beauty Lunatic]] antagonist reveals the truth in the midst of the ideological confrontation. He has been researching [[Amadea]]'s past—not out of kindness, but because her grief is _useful_ to him.

The antagonist reveals this information specifically to induce a catastrophic psychological spiral in [[Amadea]]. As a member of the [[Court of Delicacies]], his goal is to make [[Amadea]] surrender to her wound, just as he did when she killed his brother [[Kastor]].

By proving that [[Corvin]]’s intense desire to protect others turned him into a monster, the antagonist holds up a terrifying mirror to [[Amadea]]'s current path. [[Amadea]] has built the [[Obsidian Feather Society]] on the premise of absolute protection, vowing to "die in the silence" so others can live. The antagonist is trying to show her that her noble crusade is the exact same path that shattered [[Corvin]]'s humanity. 

[[Pollux]] wants her to break, aestheticize her loss, and realize that trying to heal the world only creates more beautiful monsters.

His objective is an ultimatum to [[Amadea]]:

_"Wear the fox-mask. Aestheticize loss as I do. Let the feeling wear you the way you've been wearing that pathetic memory. Stand beside me as my queen, muse and legacy—with your intoxicating grief displayed in the blooming garden besides [[Lazarus]], and my brother's death."_

_He tilts his head. The mask he is offering catches the torchlight._

_"Or I will take your wound myself. Make it beautiful without your clinging, hopeless stubbornness. And when despair finally crushes you—when you surrender to the exhausting shape of your own overwhelming decay—I will harvest that beautiful [[Rose Seed]] from your [[Atonalis]] form and set it in the crown of my collection. That [[Obsidian Feather]] is the one I will use to write the music sheet of my [[Magnum Opus]]."_

_A pause._

_"The choice is yours, Empress of Nothing. Performance or peace. Either way—you were always mine."_

- **Trauma as a Commodity:** _"You wouldn't know the lengths I've had to sacrifice just to learn your pathetic origin story."_ To this antagonist, trauma is not something to be healed; it is a rare artifact to be excavated, studied, and collected. He treats Amadea's deeply personal suffering as a piece of lore he "earned" through sacrifice.
    
- **Contempt for Moving On:** _"Perhaps you never asked... You never asked who it had been."_ He scorns Amadea for trying to survive and move forward. For a Loss-Beauty Lunatic, failing to obsessively pick at the origins of a wound is a failure of devotion. He is offended that she simply survived the [[The Seven Hands]] [[Anxithor]] rather than worshipping the tragedy of its existence.
    
- **Theatrical Cruelty:** _"turning the Sky Glass shard in his gloved fingers."_ He is highly performative. He holds the physical manifestation of her trauma (Artus's memory encoded in the Sky Glass) as a stage prop, relishing the power dynamic of holding her grief hostage while he sets the stage for her devastation.

His obsession with her comes from the fact that she killed [[Kastor]] and broke his [[Numb]] [[Legend Trait]]:

- [[Pollux]] was [[Numb]] before [[Kastor]]'s death
- The collection under the mentorship of [[Lazarus]] was an aesthetic practice, not a lived experience.
- Then [[Amadea]] kills [[Kastor]].
- And [[Pollux]] feels something for the first time in decades. Not just pain—awakening. His [[Void]] [[Motif Awakening]] happens precisely because the loss shatters through his numbness.
- [[Kastor]]'s death didn't just add to the collection. It created [[Pollux]] as a feeling subject. Before that moment, he was a curator of others' wounds. After that moment, he has his own wound. He is no longer outside the collection—he is inside it, and he kills [[Lazarus]] to inhabit as the heir and legacy of the collector.

| Normal Response                         | [[Pollux]]'s Delusional Response                                       |
| --------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Hate the person who killed your brother | Revere them as the one who _completed_ you                             |
| Seek revenge                            | Seek _eternal proximity_ to the source of the wound                    |
| Want the killer to suffer               | Want the killer to _stay_—as the centerpiece that keeps the wound open |



**[[Medea]] and the [[Waltz of Wandering Love]]**

Amadea is already in the streets before Cordelia catches up to her — not running, but moving with that particular quality she has when she doesn't want to be followed: tight turns, low shoulders, her hand resting at the hilt of the [[Hollow-Point]] saber not in threat but in readiness, the way she always holds herself when she is expecting the world to hit her somewhere new.

The [[Obsidian Feather Society]] is not a parade. It is a geometry. Three agents at the north end of the canal district, two at the bridge, one on the roof, and Amadea moving between them like the notation between measures of music — the silence that holds the structure together. Cordelia has been watching this long enough to recognize the pattern. She times her approach for the gap between the north agents and the bridge, and she arrives at Amadea's shoulder without announcing herself, because she has learned that announcing herself gives Amadea the full second she needs to find a reason to send her away.

_"You changed the formation at the south canal,"_ Cordelia says.

_"There was a new variable."_

_"The variable was me."_

Amadea does not answer. But she does not change direction either. That, Cordelia has learned, is as close as agreement gets.

This is the texture of their collaboration now — the constant negotiation between Amadea's instinct to absorb consequences alone and Cordelia's refusal to let her. The Waltz tissue has not come up since the rescue from the estate. Neither of them has named what they are to each other in terms that could be held and examined. There is only the work, and the music that fills the silence between the work, and the way Amadea has started, very occasionally, humming a few bars of the Waltz when she thinks Cordelia is asleep.

She does not know Cordelia is not asleep.

Cordelia does not tell her.

The first masked operative the [[Obsidian Feather Society]] detains in this chapter is not a fighter. He is a chronicler — a small man in a pewter fox half-mask, carrying a leather satchel full of folded paper, maps of sound: transcriptions of performances, ballads, Spellweaver compositions, all marked in the margin with the same annotation system, the same notation of emotional frequency as if music were being catalogued not for pleasure but for preservation. The fox mask is different from the pugilist's. Thinner. Older. The kind worn not for combat but for ceremony.

He does not resist. He sits when Amadea places him in the chair in the back room of the Enclave safe-house, and he folds his hands in his lap, and he looks at her with the particular patience of someone who has been waiting a long time for this conversation.

_"You've been following Orphael's lead through the canal district,"_ Amadea says.

_"We have been following a song,"_ the chronicler says.

_"What song."_

He reaches into the satchel — slowly, because Amadea's hand is on her saber — and removes a single folded sheet. He sets it on the table between them and smooths it flat with both palms. It is a transcription. The key signature, the waltz time, the particular ornamental phrasing in the third measure.

Cordelia, standing at the window, turns.

The chronicler is watching her, not Amadea.

_"You know this one,"_ he says. Not a question.

Cordelia's hand tightens on the sill. The transcription on the table is not exactly the version she plays. The notation is older, more formal, stripped of the small variations she has accumulated over [[Cycle]]s of playing it alone in the estate's back corridors where no one came to correct her. But the structure is the same. The wandering motif in the melody. The unresolved cadence in the fourth bar that she always played through rather than correcting, because correcting it felt wrong in a way she could not name.

_"Where did you get this,"_ she says.

The chronicler smooths the sheet again, a gesture that is almost affectionate. _"Where did you?"_

He does not perform the revelation. He delivers it the way an archivist delivers a document: matter-of-factly, without decoration, as if the weight of the information is entirely the listener's problem.

The Waltz of Wandering Love was composed at the founding of the Silver Blood kingdom. The composer was their queen — Medea of the Silver Blood, first sovereign of the Selenea-blessed lineage, whose founding of the kingdom is the reason the Lunehymn concentrations in that territory ran deeper than anywhere else in the Old Testament age. She wrote it at the moment of the kingdom's establishment, not as a celebration but as a reckoning — with the wandering she had done before the founding, the love she had carried through it, the people she had hoped would find her once she had built something permanent enough to be found.

_"A love trying to find love,"_ Cordelia says, almost to herself.

_"That is what she called it, yes."_

There is a silence in the room that has a different quality than the silences that preceded it.

Amadea is very still at the table. She is looking at the transcription, not at the chronicler, and her expression has not changed — but her left hand has moved from the saber hilt to the surface of the table, flat, and her fingers are pressing against the wood the way they press against the neck of the violin when she is working through a new sequence: small deliberate pressure, not playing, just feeling.

_"She's been dead for a long time,"_ Amadea says. Her voice is neutral in the particular way that means she is doing arithmetic. _"How does a founding song end up as a lullaby in an aristocratic estate."_

The chronicler looks at Cordelia.

Cordelia already knows the answer before he speaks. She has known something adjacent to it for [[Cycle]]s without allowing herself to approach it directly — the way she knew the shape of the Waltz before she knew its name, playing it by ear from the fragment she first heard from her grandmother, who heard it from her grandmother before that, handed down through generations of the family as _traditional music_, as _old custom_, as _something we have always played_, without origin and without explanation.

The Silver Blood kingdom did not simply fall. It was dismantled from the inside. Medea was betrayed by her closest confidants — stabbed thirty-three times in her throne room — and the aristocratic class that survived the collapse, the noble families who had either participated or stood by or simply moved quickly enough to claim territory in the aftermath — those families rebuilt their power on the architecture of what she had built, using the same Leyline concentrations, the same civic structures, the same cultural inheritance, without acknowledging where it came from.

Cordelia's family is not the Silver Blood. They are the **inheritance of the betrayal.**

She says nothing for a long time.

The chronicler waits. He is very good at waiting.

_"The song,"_ Cordelia finally says. Her voice is even. She has a great deal of practice making her voice even. _"My family played it as a lullaby."_

_"Many families in the fragmentation did. The Silver Blood cultural inheritance was absorbed rather than destroyed. Easier to claim a tradition as your own than to explain where it came from."_

_"And the woman who wrote it—"_

_"Died on the steps of her own throne. She did not reach them. She fell approximately two-thirds of the way up. There are accounts."_

Cordelia turns back to the window. The canal district outside is silver with early morning light and she looks at it without seeing it.

What she is feeling is not grief, exactly. Grief she knows the shape of. This is something older and more structural — the sensation of a floor revealing itself to have been built over a body. Not metaphorically. Literally. The family that broke her for giving freely, that punished her for warmth, that handed her the whip she was meant to offer to the person beating her and taught her to say _thank you_ afterward — that family had been playing the song of the woman they destroyed as a _lullaby_.

She had been playing it in defiance of them, in the back corridors, the only music she kept that was only hers.

It had never been only hers.

It had always been Medea's.

Here is the structure of Cordelia's spiral: it does not arrive as a breakdown. It arrives as a series of recognitions, each one landing before she has finished processing the previous one, each one drawing the same shape at a different scale.

The song she played to resist the estate was the song of the woman the estate's founding betrayed.

The song she played to heal Amadea in the wilderness clearing was a founding song — composed at the moment when someone else had also tried to build something permanent enough to be found.

The song whose title she engraved on the linen tissue — the tissue Amadea used to find her in the dark after the crystal awakening, the tissue that sent Amadea on the rescue mission through the estate — was a three-hundred-[[Lunar Cycle]]-old piece of evidence, a ghost that had been trying to name itself in her hands every day since she was a child and she had not known what it was testifying to.

She has been Medea. Not in the grand sense. In the granular one: loving freely inside a structure designed to punish the act of loving. Playing the song of someone who also loved freely and was stabbed for it. Carrying the grief of a woman she never knew, encoded in harmonic structure, without knowing whose grief it was.

The unresolved cadence in the fourth bar that she never corrected — because correcting it felt wrong.

It is wrong. It is unresolved because Medea died before she could resolve it. She composed the song at the founding of the kingdom and she never heard how it was supposed to end because the kingdom ended first, and she ended with it, alone on the steps of her throne in a pool of silver blood, imagining a peace that was not there.

Cordelia's hands are shaking. She becomes aware of this as a piece of external information — her hands are shaking against the window sill — and she tightens them into stillness with the deliberate practice of someone who has been trained to accept pain without visibly registering it. Then she stops. She lets her hands shake. She has bled enough. She is not performing composure for the estate anymore.

_"The song is the witness,"_ she says, and she is not talking to the chronicler.

Amadea hears her.

Amadea's Recognition — _The Violet Empress Never Existed_

It lands differently for Amadea.

She has been doing the arithmetic since the chronicler said _Medea of the Silver Blood._ Eight [[Lunar Cycle]]s ago, she was a girl sitting in a ruined arch-pass while a boy named Artus told her about a ballad he had heard — a story about a queen, a grove, an empire built in violet flowers. He gave her the [[Legend Title]] from that ballad. He planted the seed. He told her that she would be the empress of a legend that had already happened, that she was not building something new but fulfilling something ancient, that the violet grove was real and waiting.

He was eight Moons from the Great Plague when he heard that ballad.

He misremembered the tree species. He misremembered the ending. He fabricated triumph where there was only betrayal — not out of dishonesty but out of the desperate alchemy of hope, the way grief converts its rawest material into fuel because the alternative is not surviving the winter.

The Violet Empress was Medea. The violet grove was a Glimmerfern grove. The empire that Artus believed was a triumphant precedent was a wound made polity, ended by a knife, ended by trusted hands.

Amadea at her Crystal Motif Awakening — on the ground, holding a linen tissue engraved with the title of a three-hundred-[[Lunar Cycle]]-old founding song, with the Sky Glass gone and Cordelia gone and Artus already dead — had compressed every loss she had ever carried into a single wish: _I wish I had the power to transform all of this agonizing loss into anything that would, one day, make real the title of my greatest knight._

She had been wishing herself into a precedent that ended in tragedy.

She does not move from the table for a long moment. Then she picks up the transcription — very carefully, by the corner, as if it is something that might transmit its weight through direct contact — and she reads it again. The key signature. The waltz time. The wandering motif. The unresolved cadence in the fourth bar.

She has heard Cordelia play this every day for months.

She has heard Cordelia play this on the night she was injured, bleeding in a wilderness clearing, barely conscious, and the song reached her before the healing did — reached her in the part of the brain that processes frequency before it processes language, before she could decide whether to accept comfort or refuse it.

She was already inside Medea's song before she knew Medea existed.

She sets the transcription down.

_"The empress he believed in,"_ she says, slowly, _"died alone on the steps of a throne she couldn't reach."_

_"Yes."_

_"He didn't know that."_

_"No one who heard the ballad knew that. The ballad was a drift of a drift. The tragedy had been edited out by the time it reached him."_

Amadea is quiet for the span of several breaths.

_"So I've been chasing—"_

_"A beautiful, loving mistake recorded by our original founder [[Junius]],"_ the chronicler says, and he sounds genuinely sorry. _"Yes. Do you know why 'Violet'. my dear Empress? That's the color the [[Glimmerfern]] tree drenched the moment her [[Silver Blood]] blended with the [[Lunehymn]]. The name itself, is the color that appeared in the ground the moment she died. That's the tragedy [[Junius]] painted in the very blood of the mother who had adopted him."_

This is the moment the chronicler stops being merely cooperative.

He tells them — without being asked, in the way of someone who has been waiting to deliver a testimony — what the [[Court of Delicacies]] is. Not what they do. What they _are._

[[Junius]] was in the throne room. He was 3 of the 21 wounds. Medea's last words were directed at him: _"Even you, [[Junius]], my son?"._ He survived. He lived with that for the rest of his natural lifespan and then beyond it, because the Loss-Beauty Lunatic path does not allow ordinary death. It converts grief into an aesthetic system, a theology of preservation: if you cannot heal the wound, you perfect it. If you cannot undo the loss, you make the loss into something beautiful enough to justify its own existence.

The [[Court of Delicacies]] is [[Junius]]' grief, institutionalized. It wears fox masks — the animal of the liminal, the creature that moves between human and wild, between death and life — because the entire institution exists in the space between Medea's living and her dying, between the moment before _"Even you, [[Junius]], my son?"_ and the moment after. They do not move forward. They have not moved forward in three hundred [[Lunar Cycle]]s. They preserve the wound because the wound is all they have left of her, and to heal it would be to lose her a second time.

_"And the fox-masked pugilist, [[Kastor]]?"_ Amadea says. _"He stole from me. He defeated me. He took the Sky Glass."_

_"He was not stealing,"_ the chronicler says. _"He was collecting. We keep everything that carries the frequency of the original loss. The Sky Glass carries yours. Yours carries—"_ he hesitates. _"The shard was resonant with a very old grief. It sang at the frequency of the Silver Blood lineage. We heard it from across the canal district. You should see the surprise in [[Pollux]] eyes when he realized you were not a [[Silver Blood]] yourself despite having silver hair. The irony is deeply poetic and that's why he considers you the [[Magnum Opus]] of the [[Court of Delicacies]]."_

Amadea goes very still.

Artus' memory — encoded in the Sky Glass — resonates at the frequency of the Silver Blood lineage. Because the ballad Artus heard was about Medea. Because the hope he carried was built from Medea's story. Because his grief, and hers, and Cordelia's, and the three-hundred-[[Lunar Cycle]]-old founding song — they are all running at the same frequency. They have been running at the same frequency since the night in the arch-pass when he gave her meaning to a title that belonged to a dead queen.

_"The Waltz,"_ Cordelia says, from the window. She is still not turning around. _"You were there. In the Enclave circuit. Before the pugilist attack. You had people at the performance."_

_"Yes."_

_"You were following the song."_

_"We follow it wherever it goes. It is the last thing she made freely. Before the knives."_

#### Score IX: The Light in the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] and [[The Principles of Magic]]

[[Weight of Flaw]] | [[Fragment of Lucidity]]

_Act 49: The Dawn of the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] of the Feathered Wyrm_

The chronicler is not a threat. He is a mirror.

The [[Court of Delicacies]] and the [[Obsidian Feather Society]] were built from the same founding grief. [[Medea]]'s death is the origin event for both. The [[Court of Delicacies]] preserves the wound because preservation is all [[Junius]] knew how to do with loss. [[Obsidian Feather Society]] was founded on Amadea's crystal-compressed wish to _transform_ loss into something that would make the title of her greatest knight real — to take the Sky Glass-weight of every person who died in the dark and build something that prevented it from happening to someone else.

_"We die in the silence so that you may sing of tomorrow,"_ Amadea says, not to the chronicler but to the room.

The chronicler nods. _"We live in the silence so that the song is preserved exactly as it was."_

_"That's not living."_

_"No,"_ he agrees. _"It isn't."_

He stands. He does not reach for the satchel. He leaves the transcription on the table, and he crosses to the door, and he pauses with his hand on the frame.

_"She wandered,"_ he says. _"That was the word she used. In her journals. Not searching — wandering. She said the difference was intention. Searching implies a destination. Wandering implies trust in the route. She trusted people who were not trustworthy. That is the tragedy. Not that she was weak. That she was right to trust and wrong about who."_

He looks at Cordelia's back. He looks at Amadea.

_"The song ends where you take it,"_ he says. _"We never found out where it was supposed to go."_

He leaves.

The room is quiet.

Cordelia finally turns from the window. Her eyes are dry, not because she is not affected but because she has learned over a very long time to let the internal landscape register at its own pace, separately from the external one. She looks at Amadea, who is still sitting at the table with the transcription in front of her and both palms flat on the wood and her expression doing the thing it does when she is processing something she has not yet found language for: totally still, fractionally too still, like a string held precisely between two notes.

_"The legend was a lie,"_ Amadea says.

_"Yes."_

_"He gave me a name that belonged to a woman who was betrayed and died alone."_

_"He didn't know that."_

_"It doesn't—"_ Amadea stops. Starts again. _"I have been building toward a precedent that doesn't exist. Everything I told myself about Iridia — that it was_ possible*, that someone had already done it, that the violet grove meant the idea had already been proven — none of that is true."*

Cordelia crosses the room. She does not sit. She stands next to the table, close enough that Amadea would have to move to create distance, and she picks up the transcription and she looks at it for a moment, and then she begins to hum it — not performing, just the melody, the way she has done every day for months in clearings and at camp edges and across the silence between them.

Amadea's hands press harder into the wood.

_"The precedent doesn't exist,"_ Cordelia says, still humming, the melody and the words simultaneous in the way that musicians do when they speak through a song rather than about one. _"Medea tried and was betrayed and died. That's true. But the song survived. She built something that couldn't be stabbed. That's also true."_

_"She didn't build the song to survive. She built it because she was in love with something she was trying to hold onto."_

_"Yes,"_ Cordelia says. _"That's why it survived."_

This is not a dramatic declaration. It does not announce itself with fire.

What changes in Amadea in this chapter is a premise, not a power. The Reality Bender trait operated on the understanding that she was navigating toward something — bending the seams of the world around a fixed destination, a violet grove that existed as proof, a legend that predated her and authorized her direction. Every use of that trait, every fractional warping of causality toward the outcome she needed, was oriented around _that point_ as north. She could bend reality because she knew which direction she was bending toward.

The [[Celestial Astrolabe]] breaks when she learns the north star was a reflection of a tragedy.

The World Shaping Maverick does not need a [[Celestial Astrolabe]]. The World Shaping Maverick is the premise that she _is_ north now. Not because she claims authority she hasn't earned, but because the external authority she was deferring to — the legend, the precedent, the promise that someone had already proven this was possible — has dissolved, and the only thing left is her own compacted certainty: the crystal wish she made on the floor of absolute loss, holding a tissue engraved with the title of a song she now knows belonged to a dead queen, with Artus gone and Cordelia taken and the Sky Glass stolen.

She made that wish not from hope. She made it from the refusal to stop.

That refusal doesn't need a legend to authorize it. It was never authorized. It was only ever the compression of every loss she had ever carried into a single direction: _forward_.

She picks up the transcription. She folds it carefully. She puts it in her coat.

_"We need to move the east-canal agents,"_ she says. _"The [[Court of Delicacies]] knows their positions."_

_"I know,"_ Cordelia says.

_"And we need to play the Waltz differently. If they're tracking the frequency, we need to—"_

_"Amadea."_

_"—introduce harmonic variations, something that shifts the resonance signature without—"_

_"Amadea."_

She stops. She looks at Cordelia.

_"She wandered,"_ Cordelia says. _"That was the word. She trusted people who weren't trustworthy. She died alone imagining peace that wasn't there."_ She pauses. _"That's not the part you're repeating."_

The room is quiet.

_"The city isn't built on a precedent,"_ Amadea says finally. _"There isn't one."_

_"No."_

_"It's built on—"_

She doesn't finish the sentence. She doesn't need to. The waltz is playing — not in the room, not audibly, but in the structure of the scene itself, in the shape of everything that has passed between them, in the linen tissue folded in her coat that sent her through the walls of Cordelia's family estate, in the unresolved cadence that Medea never got to finish.

Iridia is what comes after the wandering. Not the destination the wandering was aiming for. The place the wandering _made_, by refusing to stop.

The Violet Empress never existed. The color of Violet itself was a terrific truth.

That is not the obstacle.

That is the _answer_.

Cordelia plays it that night.

Not as healing. Not as texture of companionship. Not as the private resistance she has practiced in back corridors since childhood. She plays the full version — with the unresolved cadence in the fourth bar, which she now understands is not a mistake but a statement, the sound of something that didn't get to finish — and she plays it in the open, at the edge of the [[Obsidian Feather Society]] canal-district safe house, in the dark, where anyone listening can hear.

Amadea does not tell her to stop.

The Waltz of Wandering Love was composed by a woman who loved, trusted, was betrayed, and died alone imagining a peace she never reached. The song survived. It passed through three hundred [[Lunar Cycle]]s of hands that didn't know whose grief they were carrying. It reached a girl in an aristocratic estate who played it as defiance. It reached a wilderness clearing. It reached the bottom of a pocket in a moment of absolute loss. It sent a girl through the walls of the institution that destroyed its composer.

It is playing now in the canal district, in the dark, carried by the hands of a woman who knows whose song it is, for the first time in three hundred [[Lunar Cycle]]s, by someone who knows where it came from and plays it anyway.

That is the anthem. Not triumph. Memory. Not a legend. A grave, and a choice, and the wandering that keeps going even when the [[Celestial Astrolabe]] breaks.

The Violet Empress never existed.

_Play the waltz anyway._

The realization of the truth of [[Medea]] sends them into a deep spiral, but this is the first step that transforms [[Amadea]]'s [[Reality Bender]] [[Legend Trait]] into [[World Shaping Maverick]].

After the night, [[Iridia]] is losing the [[Shadow Order]] war but as [[Amadea]] learns the limitations of [[Spellweaving]] and the hardship of coding principles into teachable patterns. She finally understands how to make the revolutionary construct of [[Dear Fugue]] which reaches his apex as the feathered wyrm, and is the reason why [[Amadea]] becomes renowned as the [[Void]] [[Mythical Virtuoso]].

Elder [[Lenore]] becomes a key figure in [[Iridia]] to ground [[Amadea]] even despite [[Medea]]'s tragedy: _"As long as there's the stars, the [[Moon]], and the earth, it will all work out. As long as there's the will to live, you can turn any place into a paradise. After all, you are alive, and the opportunities for a future are everywhere in all the corners of the skies. I doubted it once, but your [[Legend]], [[Amadea]], proved that fate can be overturned. You survived what [[Corvin]], [[Nivienne]], and even I couldn't."_

Moreover, [[Amadea]] unearths the story of the [[Stravos Household]] and of [[Pollux]] & [[Kastor]] past, following the lead of [[Leda]] the violinist and of [[Markus]].

_Act 50: I Am The North Star of My Own [[Celestial Astrolabe]]_

They finally realize the plan on how to beat [[Pollux]].

This is the the moment of transformation of [[Amadea]]'s [[Reality Bender]] into [[World Shaping Maverick]] which crystalizes further her [[Crystal]] [[Motif Awakening]]. _"You are again filled with Determination. For you are the Determination."_

When [[Amadea]] is prompted to abandon the legacy of [[Iridia]] she declares: _"But I refuse. I won't be written out of my own story. I will claw for a better world with my own hands regardless of the insurmountable of the pain I must face."_

_Act 51: The Seven Founding Architects of [[The Principles of Magic]]_

[[Cordelia]] is a key character to [[Amadea]] as well in her relationship to [[Luminaire]] which is why the 3 found so many schools of magic like [[Illusory Magic]], [[Healing Magic]], [[Detective Magic]], etc.

This is the formal rise of the [[Mythical Virtuoso]], establishing [[The Principles of Magic]]
 and [[The Registers of Magic]] based on what they have all gathered across their entire journey. They take on the former legacy of [[Daedalus]] and [[Aurelian]].

As words get out, [[Pollux]] questions [[Amadea]] about her decision to create [[Forbidden Magic]]: _"You tried to outlaw everything I do, what is the fun in that? But I'm glad that I keep such an impact in your mind as much as you do in mine. That's the sweetest thing anyone could has ever done for me. 'To try to preserve the [[Dissonance]] of my strings by immortalizing them into law.'  It sounds so delightful, but I digress with calling it [[Atonalis]] magic, mine is much more refined, filled with distortion, yes, but devoid of [[Discordant Interference]]."

_Act 52: Run Into Love To Destroy Who You Used To Be_

[[Amadea]] realizes on her deepest trauma that it will not be because she forgot the girl in the river. It will be because she finally steps into the water, takes that little girl by the hand, and decides to become the mother she lost, both to herself and to the world of Iridia. This is how she destroys her version that looks on the past and finally walks forward into love. (Image of the little girl in the river being rescued by herself.)

This is the fight between the [[Court of Delicacies]] and the [[Obsidian Feather Society]] divided in two fronts, one in the underground passage with [[Amadea]], and the second team of [[Cordelia]], [[Sephira]], and [[Orphael]].

[[Ligeia]] reaches the climax fight with [[Cordelia]] and [[Sephira]] as she sings the story of her own past when she was abandoned to die in the cellar. The song is a deep point of [[Emotional Authenticity]] which increases the yield of [[Ligeia]]'s own unadorned voice as it isn't distorted anymore by [[Voice Projection Arts]], becoming a terrifying spear of pure clarity.

As the warfare ignites with [[Sephira]]'s [[Cindergale]] and the friction of her [[Luminance]] kaleidoscope spear the entire place echoes with the lyrics of her [[Soul Leitmotif]].

**Pre Chorus:**
_"Please stay with me I say".

_"I pray just once to dead air."_

**Chorus:**
_"The warm wind caresses my face, and I claw at the door. The world is burning to ashes."_

_"And here I am at the last at the lock, at the last of my screams, at the last of my love."_

_"I can feel the pressure outside, my heart pounding inside, telling all pretty lies."_

_"I can't feel the pain in my throat, echoing through my own voice. Suddenly all the cold goes blank."_

**Bridge:**
_"The silver in my blood is a terrifying, damning, curse. A familial lie, bound to tragedy and bleeding a wounded heart."_

_"The sound right outside rises to a harmonized voice, as if the lines between the soil drip themselves down into tears."_

_"The orphaned girl lies lying low. Alone around the ruins starving. It must have been a sick miracle. Why wouldn't she just die and end her bloodline."_

**Chorus 2:**
_"The warm wind caresses my face, and I claw at the door. The world is painting in cinders."_

_"I just want the pain all to stop, where had I just gone wrong, why must I be abandoned?"_

_"It doesn't matter now anymore, I am not the same girl, not the same sacrifice."_

_"The tragedy is painted in ink, etched forever in art, Let this world rot in beauty."_

During this dual encounter [[Cordelia]] and [[Sephira]] are locked in a desperate struggle while [[Amadea]] begins unearthing the last pieces of [[Pollux]] past for the final confrontation.

Parallel, the final confrontation then spearheads the climax where [[Amadea]] confronts [[Pollux]] directly in the same language he uses on their final fight:

_"You are right [[Pollux]], we are similar. I know because for the longest time I ran from love because I knew it would destroy me. But that [[Amadea]] died with an [[Erosyx]] atop the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]]. That is the difference between me and you. Now I run into love because I needed it to destroy who I used to be. That's why I am the north star of my own [[Celestial Astrolabe]]."_

_"You, [[Pollux]], are still running from love. You didn't grieve [[Kastor]]. You taxidermized him. You made his death so beautiful that you never had to feel it. And now you're doing the same thing to me — making my wound part of your collection so you never have to feel yours. I am not your crown jewel. I am not your answer and I've never have been your quill. I am the fantasy you're using to run away from [[Kastor]]."_

_Act 53: The [[Magnum Opus]] of Obsidian & Shadow Ink_

On the aftermath, their fight becomes physical, and it turns into the climax of the wars of the [[Shadow Order]]s on the aerial fight amidst the cliffs of a ravine when he's engaging atop of [[Dear Fugue]] with [[Amadea]]. As they're both fighting, when nearing a crushing death, [[Pollux]] realizes what is about to happen with the falling debris of [[Dear Fugue]]. He begins to thank [[Amadea]] as he realizes both are about to be crushed by the falling construct, he delivers the last of his speech in which she uses the ink strings beneath to wrap her arms. _"Thank you, Violet Empress [[Amadea]]. For making me rencounter with my beloved little brother."_ 

He then strikes a conductors pose with both arms upward mirroring the pose he has forced [[Amadea]] into with her wrapped forearms of the conductors V she is making. Then, as the last act of his [[Magnum Opus]], [[Pollux]] uses all of the strength of the strings to pull [[Amadea]] before [[Dear Fugue]] collapses into both. [[Amadea]] is flung upward, and before he dies in a similar death like [[Kastor]] as [[Dear Fugue]] strikes at him on the wall of the ravine that then falls into the abyss below, he delivers his final gift to [[Amadea]] etched in the words he intonates as he strikes the vowing pose of the show ending right before [[Dear Fugue]] collapses into both: _"Live."_

##### [[Pollux]] Climax Death Scene

I can't maintain the shape. [[Dear Fugue]] is falling.

I hear the notes of all the performance that are beginning to mute my own, until I can't feel the motion of the bow of the violin.

The strings bite into my forearms as I tear against them, and for one impossible heartbeat I think I can still wrest the movement back into my own hands. But Dear Fugue is already falling apart above us, the structure shuddering as the harmonics begin to break one after another, and I can feel the whole construct losing itself beneath the weight of the collapse.

I can't play the violin.

I try to stabilize it, to hold it together long enough to matter, but the damage is already too far gone. The failure keeps spreading. The air itself feels like it is descending.

"Please, [[Artus]]..." I say as a plea to the [[Void]] as a few tears run through my cheek.

Only then does Pollux move.

The ink strings rise finally catching to me beneath the ruin and wrap my arms before I can break away, coiling tight around my forearms until my resistance is trapped inside the bind. It hurts. He pulls hard, finally stopping me and the violin. The motion drags my body into his reach just as Dear Fugue dissolves completely.

The construct is no longer falling in pieces — it is unmaking itself overhead, collapsing into a crushing death that is now only moments away from taking both of us.

Pollux looks at me, and for the first time I see something like relief in him?

Not fear. Not hesitation. Relief, as if the end has finally arrived in the exact shape he was waiting for.

He turns back to me and speaks.

“Thank you, Violet Empress Amadea.”

"What?" 

I go still.

This is the first time he's ever said Violet Empress, and not another mockery of the title. What do I do? I can't discern. I cannot tell, in that instant, whether he is mocking me or naming something I was never meant to carry. Violet Empress.

The title lands wrong and sharp, like a blade turned sideways in the throat of a song. I have heard many names in my life, but that one feels as if it was waiting for me before I was ever born.

Then he uses all the strength in the bindings to drive me upward.

It stiffens, I can feel the pain bite into my arms. The motion is sudden and violent, and I feel myself wrenched into the air as Dear Fugue fully gives way above us. The construct is gone now, dissolving into ruin around the space where we should have died together, and the force of the collapse becomes one last catastrophic surge. Pollux forces my arms higher, dragging me into the conductor’s V, and then mirrors it with his own body, rising into the same terrible shape as if we have become the two halves of a final measure neither of us has the right to finish.

I can still pull through. I fight him harder.

The ink strings drag across my skin. Pain flashes white along my forearms, hot and humiliating, and I wrench again, trying to break the pose, trying to keep some part of myself unclaimed in the last seconds before the collapse. But the wreckage is already closing in, and Pollux only smiles through the strain, his breath uneven, his eyes bright with that same strange relief — as if the end has finally arrived in the only shape he can bear.

“I finally hear how to run into love.”

It all suddenly clicks. 

He is conducting our mutual death.

He then asks. "Do you hear it?"

I do. That's the confirmation.

Not the words. The notes of the crescendo he's been playing.

The repeating pattern in the strings, each key repeating in itself. The pulse of his obsession. The final movement of something he has been building toward since before I understood he had chosen me as the page for his madness. Dear Fugue groans one last time, folding inward above us

Why the relief?

My confusion sharpens into fury. I pull against the bindings again, harder this time, and for a moment I think I might break free. Then he speaks over the collapse, soft as if we are alone in a concert hall instead of standing beneath a falling monstrosity.

“Thank you,” he says again, and there is something almost reverent in it now. “For making me rencounter with my beloved little brother.”

Why is he thanking me, has he finally gone insane on the last moments?

I do not know what to do with that.

The words hit me like a fracture. Not because they are kind — they are not kind — but because they sound like a truth told by the wrong mouth. Pollux is looking at me as if I have opened something in him that even he cannot bear to name directly, and for one disorienting instant I cannot decide whether I am his enemy, his duet, or the wound he has decided to cherish forever.

That is when he takes the same pose I am trapped in.

The conductor’s V.

His arms rise with mine, mirroring me exactly, his expression tightening into something solemn and final. The air around us feels suddenly charged, as if the whole ravine is holding its breath before the last downbeat. He is no longer just binding me. He is finishing the performance with me inside it.

"My final gift, My [[Magnum Opus]]." he rises as the last of the notes loop back.

I prepare myself for the end. Perhaps this is the end of the story.

I hate him for how perfect it is.

I hate him for how much it looks like fate.

I hate—

“Live.”

Then he says it, and before I can make any sense of the words of confusion, salvation or damnation he just declared, the pain turns sharper than ever.

The moment he did the vowing pose, all the strings attached to my forearms pulled upward with the same motion that makes him pull downward for the end of the performance.

The sting of the burning strings makes my entire body arch. The sharpest pain I've ever felt clawing at every layer of my arms, sending the same tension that is pulling me upward as I hear that word before it all devolves into a distant scream.

For a moment the word is quiet.

Until it breaks open in the howling of splintering crystal, quieter than the roar of the last collapse as Dear Fugue strikes the ravine wall where both of us should have died together. Quieter than the sound my mouth utters at the sheer clawing pressure embedded into my forearms.

But it reaches me anyway, and I feel something in my chest seize as the construct breaks apart in a burst of sound so violent it might be applause.

That is what it is, somehow.

Not praise. Not celebration.

Applause.

The end of a performance no one survives in the same shape they entered it.

The impact hurls me upward with brutal force, ripping me out of the binding as Dear Fugue collapses beneath us. For a split second I rise above the ruin, suspended in the same air that seconds before had promised my death, while Pollux falls with the wreckage, pulled down into the abyss below as though he has chosen to bow to the very end he orchestrated.

I cannot tell whether he saved me or condemned me.

Perhaps both.

Perhaps that is what he meant to leave me with.

"Why?" I don't understand. "Is this the signature he meant to immortalize in me?"

_Act 54: [[Potential of Creation]], [[Key of Attunement]], [[Mastery Over Chaos]]_

[[Amadea]]'s forearms are bleeding from the pull as the strings etch into her skin but this last movement saves what would've been a double death. This action leaves [[Amadea]] wondering about the entire situation and it's one of the catalysts, albeit twisted, that helps her push through [[The Hollowing]] during the real fight of ideology with [[Lacrimosa]] in the [[Age Crisis]]. In the aftermath of her survival, she decides to heal the bruises in her right arm, the one where she has her [[Soul Leitmotif]], but she keeps all of the scars [[Pollux]] made on her left arm when he saved her by pulling [[Amadea]] upwards. 

This distinction is a reminder of her past and her future, the left arm is The Violet Empress, the arm that holds her instrument steady, and the one threading the notes of the neck of the violin, with all of the [[Potential of Creation]] of her entire story and the arm that is imbued with all her suffering, sacrifices and tragedy while her right arm is the Obsidian Empress, the arm that directs the bow, establishes dynamics, agency, and makes the actual sound of the violin, it is her [[Mastery Over Chaos]], her commanding arm of [[Iridia]] and its [[Shadow Order]]. 

Her heart in the middle is the [[Key of Attunement]] of who she is in the present state, having the ability to look for agency with her right arm while maintaining a tether to her past, legacy, sacrifices, and memory in her left arm. This distinction is the formal birth of The Obsidian Empress of [[Iridia]], and it evolves her [[Reverent]] [[Legend Trait]] to its utmost [[Luminance]] [[Apex Trait]], [[Consecrator of Terrestrial Skies]] as she internalizes the practice even in her performance on the violin to create the place where the sky meets the earth.

When [[Cordelia]] reaches to her to cure her after meeting with her atop the meadow overlooking the ravine where she was fighting [[Pollux]], [[Amadea]] repeatedly dismiss her attempts to cure her, saying: "No. These scars are mine, and it's the signature of the triumph of the [[Obsidian Feather Society]]. They're what anchors the future to the past."

[[Ligeia]] attempts to take her life to die alongside [[Pollux]] but [[Sephira]] intervenes, inviting her alongside [[Amadea]] to join [[Iridia]] and forge a new legacy. She tells her: _"Preserving the pain may feel like honoring the past, but those wounds only poison the future. I would know best because I was the [[Cynical]] [[Coward]] that left everything to die once. I won't run away anymore, and I won't let you be taken either."_

After triumphing over the [[Court of Delicacies]], she says the famous phrase when she realizes some hardships and seeming as a "tyrant" for chasing justice. She determines that as a [[Great Justiciar]] is up to her to determine how to protect those who can't fend for themselves while being ruthless and precise as a shadow:

- _“No matter if hypocrites call me tyrant. Let the shameless few even name me monster. But when the history is written, let them also say this: I lost. I loved. And I built a world where fewer children weep in the shadows — on the very land of those who abandoned them. That is not nothing. That is everything. I vow to die in the silence, so that my children may sing of tomorrow; for singers remember, when the living cannot.”_

- _"The [[Obsidian Feather Society]] is that someone who must watch the shadows so that dawn can come for those who cannot watch themselves."_

After the chaos settles, the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] meet again to encode the final chapters of [[The Registers of Magic]] and the final version of [[The Principles of Magic]] cementing their status permanently as [[Mythical Virtuoso]] when the word spreads out about the founders of magic. This is where she develops too her famous phrase for [[Essence Sacrifice]]: _"Loss is Transformation."_

#### Score X: The Empire of Laureated Marble

[[Weight of Indulgence]] | [[Fragment of Catharsis]]

_Act 55: The Tragedy of Two Prodigies

The act features the crushing expectation of [[Luminaire]] and how she has tasted being a person, increasing her expectations for what will follow in her hollowing when she is the first to defect to [[Lacrimosa]]. It features her return from the [[Shadow Order]] wars with the rest of the [[Mythical Virtuoso]] and the founding of [[The Principles of Magic]].

The entire issue beings with the [[Weight of Indulgence]] and the relief that comes, ironically, from [[Amadea]] and what they shared together in the [[Crescent Mist Peaks]]. As it's the [[Weight of Indulgence]] that introduced her to a reality where she has seen that she can feel.

It also expands upon the family of [[Luminaire]] and [[Cordelia]] and the parallels they shared as tragic prodigies as [[Amadea]] finally arrives at [[Hollowmarch]], looking at the city of [[Legend]]s by herself for the first time ever after the [[Court of Delicacies]] has been extinguished by the death of [[Pollux]].

This features [[Father Raphael]], the history of [[Hollowmarch]], the Grand Auric Colosseum to prove [[Mastery Over Chaos]] and use [[Chorus Pillar]] infidels as prisoners and gladiators. It pinpoints the story of [[Daedalus]], [[Aurelian]], and how [[Hollowmarch]] transformed over the [[Cycle]]s mirroring the Roman Republic into the Roman Empire and the expansionism of [[Hollowmarch]] that made [[Luminaire]]'s family the ruling family of the most important empire of [[Ages]] II.

_Act 56: The Colosseum of the Aurean Winds_

It expands upon the arc of the colosseum and the way in which [[Hollowmarch]] is an institutional cage that has turned jagged in [[Crystal]] far beyond what [[Daedalus]] or [[Aurelian]] originally intended, it goes into their backstory of [[Legend]] and their connection as orphans and [[Vaelia]] as the first [[Soul Oscillator]] of [[Resonance]].

_Act 57: The [[Sacrificial Lamb]] of Contrition, [[Revelation]], and [[Piety]]_

It explores the convergence of how the [[Seven Weights]] compounded unto her, beginning with the [[Weight of Value]] transforming into the [[Weight of Purpose]], and then escalating until only the [[Weight of Indulgence]] is the seductive solution offered by [[Lacrimosa]] at [[Luminaire]]'s lowest point:

1. [[Weight of Value]] is the one that says: _"You have no worth on your own."_
2. [[Weight of Purpose]] says: _"So your only meaning is to serve."_
3. [[Weight of Nature]] says: _"And this is what you are — you cannot change."_

It features the institutional abuse of her family and the mirror it places on [[Cordelia]] but from the burden of being a prodigy born too perfect as a ruler and instrument to the estate. At the worst, it features [[Luminaire]] alone playing her [[Soul Oscillator]] as the marble walls echo with the sound of her piano.

_Act 58: Let Go Of This Place! Join Me, Join [[Iridia]]!_

[[Amadea]] gives [[Luminaire]] an out, and tells her to escape to [[Iridia]], to leave [[Hollowmarch]] behind. They have a profound talk on the rooftops overlooking the capital of the empire of laureated marble on its [[Golden Age]] glory of the [[Age of Behemoths]] as several [[Void]] [[Luminance]] jellyfish-like [[Pure Light]] beings drift around them floating through the city as part of the Colosseum celebration. 

![[Amadea-10.png]]

The [[Dreamweaver]] is present in [[Hollowmarch]] and that's how it escalates to [[A Pact of a Thousand Eyes]] later on one of the outer regions.

[[Luminaire]] ultimately refuses out of burden from the [[Weight of Change]].

4. [[Weight of Change]]: _"Because if you or they leave, everything will be destroyed."_

But the question leaves lingering in her mind, and is part of the ultimate catalyst when she decides something for herself and choosing to hollow.

_Act 59: [[A Pact of a Thousand Eyes]]_

The chapter starts with [[Luminaire]] still spiraling before the catastrophic [[Flesh-Exalting Transcendence]] that happens in the outskirts of [[Hollowmarch]].

What if she had done things differently, if she had escaped with [[Amadea]]. The [[Weight of Potential]] of everything that didn't happen of all the futures where [[Daedalus]] lived to see her coronation, where she didn't accept the burden of the crown, echoing back her original [[Motif Awakening]] to [[Luminance]]: _"I wish I knew why did I accept? Why did I not say no?" but twisted to be crushed under the [[Weight of Potential]], from what she hasn't done, and what she was entrusted as potential as the legendary heir to the throne.

5. [[Weight of Potential]]: _"What if I said no?"_

When [[Luminaire]] is being crushed by the entire stack of the weights, it's the final [[Weight of Flaw]] that condemns her:

6. [[Weight of Flaw]] says: _"It doesn't matter. It's your fault for being this way."_

Each of the [[Seven Weights]] reinforces the others, it began with the [[Weight of Indulgence]] that cracked open the [[Suppressed Diamond]]. Then the lack of self-worth ([[Weight of Value]]) confirms the servitude of her [[Selfless]] [[Diligent]] is natural ([[Weight of Nature]]), which confirms the servitude is purposeful ([[Weight of Purpose]]), which makes the threat of loss terrifying ([[Weight of Change]]), which compounds with the what if of another life ([[Weight of Potential]]) that finally confirms the person deserves their fate ([[Weight of Flaw]]), which further erodes self-worth ([[Weight of Value]]). The [[Weight of Flaw]] is particularly important to [[Luminaire]] because she is a pious of the [[Auric Order]] and her failing is rooted in her theology as she feels that she's failing the second most important [[Theological Virtue]] after [[Piety]] that she's supposed to embody as a [[Mythical Virtuoso]]: [[Revelation]] as [[Luminance]].

Then [[A Pact of a Thousand Eyes]] happens and the true start of the [[Age Crisis]] of [[Ages]] II begins in the outskirts of [[Hollowmarch]]. This is immediately detected as an attack and [[Luminaire]] is dispatched to deal with it.

The singing chorus in unison during [[A Pact of a Thousand Eyes]], and conducted by [[Pagiel]] later reads in the layering of many voices as an ethereal requiem mass bathed in [[Dream Magic]]:

_"Children born of one emotion. Our devotion's deepest ocean. No division, reasoned we'll be freed."_

_"Joy mirage's kingdom come. No one left at stake now that existence is on the wake. A part is wholly ending. A line in any final song. So long. So far. We will be atoning. At last eternal through the past, above a blinding, loving star."_

_Act 60: What If You Could Be Yourself, [[Luminaire]]?_

The circuit is self-sustaining. It is, in acoustic terms, the pre state of the [[Dissonance Core]] that finds an exit before it continues to fracture deeper her [[Composure]]. This is when the [[All-Loving Moon]] makes its move with [[The Hollowing]]. Offering the ultimate [[Weight of Indulgence]] of being useless. For someone with compounding low self-worth, the burden of autonomy — having to make choices, take responsibility, and navigate a world where they feel inadequate and caged their entire life — is agonizing. 

On her Spiraling [[Composure]], [[Luminaire]] hears the offer of [[Lacrimosa]] after being sent to deal with the [[Purest of Love]]:

 _"I see you. I see what they have made of you. And I see what you could be if you were not forced to be their instrument. Your [[Soul Oscillator]] is you, and it is only yours alone._
 
  _"But you have spent your entire life being used. Being shaped. Being refined into something useful. From the very confine of your soul._
  
  _"And you, my dear. Are as [[Selfless]] as you are deserving. Who is there to save you when you're the savior to everyone. Who cares for [[Luminaire]] when she cares for all? It is tragic. You are as worthy, as everyone you've ever strived to serve, to save, to work. The voice in the keys you touch should be for none but for your most masterful song. They are you, and your beautiful soul."_
  
  The words [[Lacrimosa]] said strung her deepest string, but it was the following that broke her:
  
  _"What if you could stop? What if you could be something useless? Something that serves no purpose? Something that simply exists, without obligation? You have worth even if you have no function, let go of the chains in your own [[Weight of Potential]]."_

When [[Luminaire]] hears the offer of [[Lacrimosa]], this makes the [[Weight of Indulgence]] the only escape where she submits their relief to a powerful neurological and magical anesthetic in [[Lux Aeterna]]. As her tears begin running down of the side of her cheek, the [[Sympathetic Vibration]] of the [[Lacrimosa]]'s [[Auroral Ribbons]] gently stroke her in an almost alluring embrace.

_"There is a version of you, [[Cordelia]] that thinks sacrificing yourself for others is love but it's not noble. It's self destruction disguised as care. You have spent your entire life saying 'Yes' to fix everyone's problems but never asked for help in return. You call it love but it's really fear, the fear that if you stop being the [[Miracle Guardian Angel]], you'll stop being useful, and you'll stop mattering, to [[Iridia]], to [[Amadea]], to the same family that you've never got yourself to see the end of."_

_"You were hurt so many times that you stopped caring where you got burned next. And you kept smiling because you didn't think anyone could handle the mother of all healers seeing herself fall apart in an attempt deep inside you know is futile."_

[[Cordelia]] tries to ignore her, keeping her focused on saving the souls of the fallen but the piercing voice of [[Lacrimosa]] is taking a toll alongside the physical exhaustion.

Then [[Lacrimosa]] attacks directly what she is doing and why she is doing it.

_"You fear that worth is tied to what you do, not who you are. This isn't beautiful or [[Selfless]], it's you avoiding your own needs by focusing first on others. You believe you have to earn basic care again through exhaustion like all those [[Cycle]]s ago."_

_"That's not love. That's a transaction where you always lose. Real love doesn't require you to set yourself on fire to keep others warm. It doesn't demand you disappear, kindling your own soul, so others, like [[Amadea]], can shine. You never escaped the estate, [[Cordelia]], it only changed faces."_

[[Lacrimosa]] takes hold on her to comfort the crying [[Cordelia]], saying:

She offers one of her [[Auroral Ribbons]] as comfort, beginning to curdle around her as a hug.

_"Join our family, [[Cordelia]]. Join [[Luminaire]], not the Grand Ivory Princess. Join [[Orphael]], not the Legendary Tide Singer. The intervention changes from a rescue into a [[Cordelia]] joining the [[Purest of Love]] because [[Lacrimosa]] perfectly mimics the cure to [[Cordelia]]'s deepest trauma. You built [[Iridia]] so that fewer children weep in the shadows but they are dying in a misguided crusade."_

And my horizon is forged from the crushing weight of everyone I have ever loved. Let us see if your heavens possess enough gravity to move a [[Singularity of True Love]]."_

She realizes that true love is a grueling endurance that has to be kept for the sake of love itself. True love does not provide you with an immunity to heartbreak. To embody [[Essence Sacrifice]] is to let yourself truly love.
